# Yogyakarta Residency Letters 1792–1809

Transcribed from the Peter Carey Archive

## Abbreviations

### Rulers and Titles
| Abbreviation | Expansion |
|---|---|
| HB II | Hamengkubuwana II, Sultan of Yogyakarta |
| PB III | Pakubuwana III, Susuhunan of Surakarta |
| PB IV | Pakubuwana IV, Susuhunan of Surakarta |
| MN I | Mangkunegara I (Raden Mas Said, reigned 1757–1795) |
| MN / M.N. | Mangkunagaran (princely house of Surakarta) |
| MB | Mangkubumi (title, often refers to the Sultan) |
| MB II / M.B.II | Hamengkubuwana II |
| P. | Pangéran (prince) |
| R. | Raden (Javanese noble title) |
| R.A. | Raden Ayu (Javanese noble title for women) |
| R.M. | Raden Mas (Javanese noble title) |
| R.T. | Raden Tumenggung (Javanese noble/official title) |
| R.T. / Rg. | Raden Tumenggung / regent (Dutch: regent = bupati) |
| Ng. | Ngabehi (Javanese official title) |
| Kyai | Religious or honorary title |
| S.S. | Susuhunan (title of the Surakarta ruler) |
| CP / C.P. | Crown Prince (Kroonprins) |
| DRI / D.R.I. | Danureja I (the Patih / chief minister of Yogyakarta) |
| DR II | Danureja II |
| PWP / P.W.P. | Pangéran Aria Prabu Prangwedana (later Mangkunegara II) |
| PRI / PRII | Misreading in original notes for DRI / DRII (Danureja I / Danureja II, Patih of Yogyakarta) |
| PWD | Pangéran Prangwedana (Col-Commandant of the Legion; Mangkunagara II) |

### Institutions and Geography
| Abbreviation | Expansion |
|---|---|
| V.O.C. | Vereenigde Oost-Indische Compagnie (Dutch East India Company) |
| E.I.C. | East India Company (British) |
| G.G. | Gouverneur-Generaal (Governor-General) |
| N.E. | Java's Northeast Coast (administrative region) |
| N.O.K. | Noordoost Kust (Northeast Coast of Java) |
| A.N. / ANRI | Arsip Nasional (Republik Indonesia) |
| KITLV | Koninklijk Instituut voor Taal-, Land- en Volkenkunde |
| TNI | Tijdschrift voor Nederlandsch-Indië |
| E.MN | Eastern mancanagara (Madiun group) |
| MNI | Mangkunagara I |

### Currency and Units
| Abbreviation | Expansion |
|---|---|
| R.R. | Ronde Reaal / Rixdollar (silver coin) |
| N.D. | Nederlandsche Ducaten (Dutch ducats) |
| Stuiver | Dutch coin (1/20 guilder) |
| Dubbeltje | Dutch 10-cent coin (2 stuivers) |
| Pikul | Unit of weight (approx. 125 lbs / 60 kg) |
| Bau | Traditional Javanese land measurement |
| Jung | Javanese land unit (approx. 4 cacah) |
| Cacah | Javanese land/household tax unit |
| Corge | Unit of count (20 pieces) |

### Officials and Persons
| Abbreviation | Expansion |
|---|---|
| Wat / Waterloo | Waterloo, Resident of Yogyakarta |
| Eng / N.E. | Nicolaas Engelhard, Gov. of Java's NE Coast |
| VdB / V.D.B. | Van den Berg, colonial official |
| Van IJ | Van IJsseldijk, Resident of Yogyakarta |
| D. | Herman Willem Daendels, Gov.-Gen. (1808–1811) |
| W.A. | W.A. Alting, Governor-General |
| G.W. | G.W. (Gustaf Wilhelm) Wiese, Resident of Yogyakarta (1808–09) |
| J.A. | J.A. van Braam, colonial official |
| A.H. | A.H. (Albertus Henricus) Wiese, Governor-General of the Dutch East Indies (1805–1808) |
| A.A. | A.A. Buyskes, Rear-Admiral / Lt-Governor-General |
| B.F. | B.F. von Liebeherr, Resident of Surakarta |
| Van Ovt / P.G. | P.G. van Overstraten, Governor-General |

| Abbreviation | Expansion |
|---|---|
| Kom. Gch. | Kommissaris Geschriften (Reports of Commissioner J.F.W. van Nes, 1847) |
| Kècu / Kecus | Thieves, bandits, highwaymen (Javanese) |
| Nuwala | Royal charter/letter (High Javanese; misread in notes as "Naltan") |
| Saendjes | Misreading in notes for Daendels (Herman Willem Daendels, Gov.-Gen. 1808–1811) |
| TNI | Tijdschrift voor Nederlandsch-Indië (post-1845 Tijdschrift van Nederlandsch-Indië) |
| Van Pabst | P.H. van Lawick van Pabst, Resident of Rembang (1808–1811) |
| von Winckelmann | F.C.P. von Winckelmann, Brigadier-General, Semarang |
| Rongga | Raden Rongga Prawiradirja III, bupati wedana of Madiun (1796–1810) |
| Sasradiningrat | Surakarta patih (in office 1812–1846); earlier bearer of the name |

### Document References
| Abbreviation | Expansion |
|---|---|

| MSS | Manuscripts |
| A.J. | Anno Javanico (Javanese calendar year) |

## List of MSS in Arsip Negara

*   **Mémoire van Overgave:** Van IJsseldijk 1798 dated Surabaya [v] (copy in KITLV; signed off in Surabaya, August 1798)
*   **Van den Berg 1803** ("Adj: Yogya") [?] (KITLV H 97 (8); copies also in De Kock campaign archive and Djokjo Brieven) [v]
*   **Letters Yogya - Batavia:**
    *   4 Dec 1808 – 7 Louwmaand 1810 (Engelhard - Wiese) (Yogya Bundel No. 22)
    *   6 Louwmaand 1810 – 12 July 1811 (Wiese - Moorrees - Engelhard) (Yogya Bundel No. 27)
    *   11 July 1803 – 10 March 1807 (Waterloo) (Yogya Bundel No. 49) [v]
    *   23 July 1809 – 11 Nov 1811 (Ceremony for Receiving Daendels [transcribed as Saendjes; official visit 29 July – 2 August 1809] at Yogya; P. Engelhard - Raffles) (Yogya Bundel No. 24)
    *   Aankomende officiële 1810-1811 (Brieven van den Minister, Moorrees en Engelhard aan Res Solo / Resident Solo) (37 Yogya - Dj.A. [Djokjo Archief; transcribed as D'A] 1810-1811) [^p3-1]

---

> Look at THESE IN LEIDEN.
> See LOr 2162 Letters P. Engelhard - Yogya court.
> Look at TNI [Tijdschrift voor Nederlandsch-Indië] 1844 Vol III for history of Daendels [transcribed as Saendjes] period.

## 1792 Conference

Promise PB II to lay out 3 small forts along road to Jambu/Ungaran. But Van O. promises not to press PB II too far on this; abandons idea of 3 small forts but building a fort at Klaten instead—promising PB IV to bear 1/2 cost. Marbok chosen as suitable place—good water/rice supply. Height/equidistant to Ngal Yogya. Promising HB II to bear 1/2 cost to maintain fort = V.O.C. to take decision later on 3 small forts. Gradak to be net up at Klaten.

P. of Pepper cultivation: promising HB II to encourage inhabitants to plant pepper. Pacitan/Loano/Bintang to be set aside entirely for pepper. Total 700 cacahs to be under overseer of Res/Patih—pop. to be freed from corvée labour. Lands to be viewed as having been given over entirely to V.O.C. During harvest, Eur. opzieners to be placed. All pepper from lands in future to be monopoly of V.O.C. Restating HB II's undertakings to V.O.C.

Necessary arrangements to be made by Patih/Res regarding indigo cultivation. 100 cacahs to be given for indigo cultivation, corvée services remitted, & Patih to provide baton from Gradak.

P. Ngabehi: In previous conference HB II shown understanding: to be given 1000 cacahs free of corvée & to be allowed to sit on a stool, not on ground in presence of Sultan = like P. [?] [?] [?] but on understanding Ng. would make no further demands for increase in his landholding/prestige. & name corvée/mil. duties as CP. Pringgalaya/P. Anom given name/status as P. Natikusuma & to be given names [?] / [?]. Signed HB II / Van O / Van R. Yogya 19 Aug. [?].

Acte: for MN signed/sealed + PB IV/Van O = 8 Aug. [?]. Guaranteeing grandson PWD the succession & name as brother of PB IV—P. Aria Mangkubumi. & guaranteed 4000 cacahs. Signed/sealed Van O. 14 Aug. 1792. Surakarta.

## 1793 Loano/Pacitan Report

1793 van IJ.
On pepper plantations: Loano, Gintang, Pacitan.
* 700 cacahs / 168 jungr nawah.
* 100 cacahs Indigo = Idea of netting up Indigo factory.
* Owners / farmers to be released of corvée services to Sultan.
After van O.'s visit to Yogya Apr. 1792 = van I. makes visit to pepper estates.

Loano + Adjacent lands = Winong / Malarang = 350 cacahs.
2 hours journey to W. Yogya.
+ Gintang = 150 cacahs.
But pepper not suited for cultivation in area. During w. monsoon Praga floods its banks. Pepper gardens flooded.

Loano - suited to pepper cultivation: Red/moist soil. Small quantities of pepper had grown there for years.
+ Malarang similarly favourable: But too dry in E. monsoon.

Instead of Gintang, pepper should be planted in Nilo Jiyang / Pekalongan.
Complaints of wong cilik about oppression / extortions by local chiefs.
But no. of inhabitants not v. great. / Would plant more pepper if could escape from oppression.

Demangs of Loano - not forthcoming.
+ Lurahs fear to give information.
By new system = Demangs cut out of local admin. of corvée deliveries.
Not enough buffaloes.

Tum. Mangundipura = Family relationships & Demangs.
(Tum. Loano)
Says that Demang want 6 jungr of land each.

---

Ngt.
1. Gropan Gilles = European gardener as opziener, estates.
2. Should have 30 pairs of buffalo at his disposition.
3. Pragem to be given by R. Adip.

4. Govnat. to give money to Sultan for rent of land.
5. Pepper to be delivered first to Yogya then on Semarang. But v. costly in time: Res. decides to need yearly a pairing suitable Javanese to sort pepper; have it sent direct to Semarang.
6. 130-132 ponden per pikul of pepper.
7. Demang - 6 jungs of ricefields.
8. Instead of rented district, Winong Gintang would ask Sultan for lands of Nelotiyang / Pekalongan near loans + subjects to take part in laying out of pepper gardens.
9. Subjects to be protected from arbitrariness of chiefs.

For every 5 cacahs - 1-2 men to take part in planting.
6 jungs ricefields for each of 2 heads of loans.
Sultan owes subjects of corvée labour for crown - no can devote himself to ricefields / pepper.
Work in pepper plantations - obligation for having sawah.

HB II delivers area loans / Pacitan + willing to help in delivery of pepper Semarang.
Willing to exchange Winong Gintang for Pekalongan / Nelotiyang.
Lands belong to C.P. Ratu Bendara (ex wife of HB I) to be given other fertile lands in Majegan (domain) estates of Sultan. Local pop. to be freed from labour services.
HB II despite having given orders about planting of pepper in area - doubtful about beneficial results.

Fear of HB II / Nayakas that if pepper plantations extended, V.O.C. influence would be increased in Central Java. + Wong alik would think that HB II had resigned authority in admin V.O.C. like pasisir areas.
1793

1793 Van IJ. P.J. Br. 45
Loano/Pacitan Report 2 W. van IJsseldijk
'Eerbiedige Bericht'

700 cacahs of pepper lands—to be increased by 100 cacahs by new agreement. Labouren from Winong (where no pepper planted) to be used on estates.

V.O.C. could now begin to expect yearly 5,000 pikuls of pepper promised by HB II.

Original Act of Verbintenis of ARI.
Pleasant adjacent lands/desas:
Promised HB II—V.O.C. to be allowed 100 cacahs for setting up Indigo Factory at Pacitan near Merapi. But indigo requires huge investment of labour (e.g. expenditure to get wood to burn indigo cooker). Doubtful if 100 cacahs enough.

Difficult to get Bupatis to surrender people = all hands needed to meet pajak/corvée services of HB II.

Indigo factory proposed to be set up near Merapi. But lack of people and wood.
∴ turns down proposed site.
Area Winong more suitable = mountain streams/woods.
Rice in wet season, indigo in dry season.
HB II prepared to surrender people on 100 cacahs in Winong and allowed to look after rice fields in wet season.

In Loano/Winong etc. + 200 from Solo.
700 cacahs (168 junges) in Pacitan: not counting 200 cacahs in Pacitan.
Have v. good appearance/buzzed closely together.
Indigo/pepper could be produced there: Pepper planted in wet monsoon/dry harvest. Fewer people needed could be used for indigo.
Not counting 87 desas, 119 junges suitable for pepper.
Outside Pacitan 2,245 people could be used in pepper estates.

2 Demang increased to 4 by Sultan to organize labour force:
Need for giving more buffaloes to inhabitants Loano/Semarang.
HB II suggests ½ to be provided by Res. / ½ by Patih.
Note that Sultanate keeps a semblance of control over the small land of Pacitan: Extremely suited for cultivation of pepper, now growing well—similar to plots in Banten.
* 40 hours journey e.g. Yogya – Transport difficulties.
* Difficulties in smuggling could not be prevented.
* Pacitan Inhabitants more disciplined/have to be treated much more carefully/gently than those in Loano/Winong.
* Able to export pepper along rivers. Population already used to growing pepper. Difficulties in Pacitan do not outweigh benefits.
* 2–2 1/2 f.p. (rijksdaalders) should be paid per pikul of pepper.

Pacitan – 200 cacahs/ 48 jungs ricefields. – Restricted to pepper cultivation. 200 to Nawah. Nawah ringed by mountains. Roads across mountains often impassable. Smuggling easy, but Chinese manning tollgates should be warned against it. Inhabitants have enough ricefields to live on – pepper could be grown [now to V.O.C.].

Survey of ricefields in Pacitan shows that since peace of 1755, [it] had greatly increased in area. But increased area hidden from courts so local farmers could appropriate newly opened up ricefields.

Ending of corvee services – Regent declares that local inhabitants can make a good living.
Giyanti Partition – more prosperous than in Hada [or] Kartasura.

Because [of] unusual oppression [by] local people – By heads/officials – must be handled more carefully than those in Loano.

Long distance from court – Reg. Nitrawijaya (now Wongnocitro) followed by Noz – takes advantage of situation. But old Nitrawijaya still in control. Population would depart if handled roughly.

Res. should keep an eye on Regent – (now Nitrawijaya, not as fair as his father) to prevent oppression.
Nawah – [should] keep them happy. Total area of [land] to be raised from 6 – 12 jungs. People should also be liable for labour services in pepper plantations.

Loano – 26 jungs could all be planted [with] pepper.
## 1793 Loano/Pacitan Report 3

Van IJsseldijk [^p26-1]
Pj. Br. 45 Van IJsseldijk (Herbudige)

Bay of Pacitan—½ an hour’s journey across, hemmed in on mountains on both sides—open to south wind. Possible to use boats to take off pepper—but dangerous pirates who use large 2-masted brigs. Therefore, [need] to export pepper over land. If V.O.C. surveys bay, it would arouse fear in Sultan/Sunan that [they are] preparing to take it over.

8,000 old pepper trees in Loano—only produce 70-80 pikuls p.a. 10,000 [pepper trees] in Pacitan—in more flourishing condition than Pacitan—local inhabitants better able to look after them. 300 pikuls could be obtained this year from Yogya Pacitan. Ruota 370 [pikuls] [from] Solo.

Total Pacitan/Loano lands suitable for pepper: 800 cacahs, 1 million pepper trees in Loano, 192 jongs [^p26-2]. And another 144 jongs in both districts could probably be used for cultivation. 50 sq. roods = 1 jong.

Pepper gardens to be ringed [with] low walls in order to allow plants to sprout. Planting of dadap trees to shelter pepper bushes—already 100,000. Better maintained in Pacitan than in Loano. Each year 60-70,000 new shoots could be expected.

**15 Jumadilakir Dal 1719 (28 Jan. 1793)**
For Pepper/Indigo: Loano, Loano/Nolotiyang, Pekalongan, 700 cacahs Malarang/Plaosan/Winong given to V.O.C. and 200 [cacahs] in Pacitan.

MZI: Frees them from corvée services so [they] can concentrate on pepper/indigo.

V.O.C. to pay Bp. [Bupati] Pacitan for pepper.
To prevent smuggling, all pepper passing through Jurgata’s property is V.O.C. monopoly.
Export of pepper: loans, Pacitan to be paid by V.O.C.
Production to be raised to 5,000 pikuls of pepper within 2 years.

But HB II [Hamengkubuwana II] began to be excused from promise of pushing pepper production in other places in Java. (Promise made on accession in Apr. 1752).
Land, ricefields of Winong to be set aside for Indigo Factory.
When people not working in Indigo Factory to work in pepper plantations—at least 48 days p.a.

11.30 p.m. Van IJsseldijk & Van Overstraten. Death HBII at 11 p.m. After death goes to the kraton where he will remain until burial of the body. All is well except for usual consternation on such weighty event occasions.

[^p29-2] of Java's N.E. coast and renewal of treaties. All to promise their obligation to observe this; CP promises to await arrival of Governor before coronation. State affairs in hand by Van IJsseldijk / ORI [^p29-3].

No princes appear to be willing to rise against provisions; do extra provisions sent that not put into effect.

Van IJsseldijk - Van O. | Pealangan/Pepper cultivation: Expected agricultural difficulties surrounding. Talks to HB II about improvements & rent at Boyolali. Baking of stoves/burning of lime/cutting wood would take some time to complete.

30 Infantry from Yogya garrison to be sent to N. coast areas to strengthen garrisons: Leave 23 Sept 1793.

Part 3 No. 1.

NvB 1793 (Yogya) -1793-

**5 Oct.**
Van IJsseldijk → Van O. Letter 2 Oct. [from] Van O. Arrival in Sunda Strait [of] French ships. MB II to prepare promised reinforcement troops. MB II promises cavalry company → [in] Semarang if necessary.

**6 Oct.**
Van IJsseldijk → Van O. Letter Van O. 6 Oct. Demand audience [with] MB II = ask delivery of forced deliveries of food. As regards payment [of] troops = MB II asks them to be considered NOT as mercenaries but reinforcement. (Niets als huur maar als hulp - [Do not treat as hire but as aid]) Payment same as for English war in 1781 = MB II takes council & Regent - MB II reinforcement troops should be kept under his own command & be paid a lump sum each month to be divided up amongst Regents. Van IJsseldijk suggest 3000 Rp. mon. MB II rejects it as insufficient. 4000 Rp. mon. for 1000 men. Who would march to Batavia in times [of] need. Less than in 1781 when V.O.C. more prosperous. Pay 5 Rp. p. man p. month.

MB II trust 20 days in Semarang/Surabaya. No payment demanded.

Document (Bewijs) given to Van IJsseldijk about the agreement:
- Payment during Eng. War 1781 (earlier ptg letter)
- [1781] gives serious burden to V.O.C.
- 1792 MBII promises to make convenable arrangement with V.O.C. about reinforcement troops in case of need.

30 Oct 1793
Van IJsseldijk & Van O.
- During war 22 Oct.
- Attempt to get better arrangement than 4000 J.p.d. promised for 1000 troops:
- But despite arguments by Van IJsseldijk only reduction of 200 J.p.d. = 3,800
- Because MBII says that his people need this sum for their upkeep
- Mantris & small followings: have to pay the salaries of their people/salaries fixed.
- Earlier audience informed by R.T. Wirajuda, Kliwon Mangkuwijaya:
- Each commoner in Eng. War 1781 gets 5 J.p.d., now 3 1/2 J.p.d.
- MBII says that he would feel ashamed if his troops deserted in Batavia because of low wages.

MBII more accommodating about troops in Semarang/Surabaya:
After 20 days should receive following payments:
- Major: 15 p.m.
- Captain: 10 p.m.
- Lieutenant: 7.24 p.m.
- Ensign: 5 p.m.
- Sergeant: 3.36 p.m.
- Corporal: 2 p.m.
- Ordinary soldier: 1 p.m.
+ 40 kg rice/ 3 kg salt.
Port. 3 No. 1.

Van IJsseldijk -> Van O.

Payment: 1000 men sent to Banjaran. [^p36-1]

NVB 1794 - 1794 - (Yogya)

*   **3 Jan:** Van IJsseldijk -> Reede (Solo). Latest exchanges of land between Solo/Yogya in area of Pinging noted in letter Jayaningrat -> ORII.
*   **19 Jan:** Van IJsseldijk -> Van O. M.B.II reports marriage in his family [^p38-1].
*   **20 Jan:** Van IJsseldijk -> Van O. Dispute of M.B.II + Govnt. Bangil - Japan cleared up.
*   **6 Feb 1794:** Van IJsseldijk -> Reede. Messengers of Jayaningrat = Raden Ng. Riksa-aja, Ng. Kertalinga. To settle dispute in Pesalingga kidul. M.B.II told:
*   **22 Feb 1794:** Van IJsseldijk -> Van O. Arrival in Batavia of Nederburgh / van Rijckevorsel as Commissioners-General. Appointment of Alting as G.G. Letter greeting to new G.G. written by M.B.II.
*   **18 Mar:** Van IJsseldijk -> Van O. Concerning indigo - hopes to deliver indigo in August 1794.
*   **18 June 1794:** Van IJsseldijk - Reede (Solo). News dealing P.A. Mangkudiningrat from Solo. Appointment of Jon R.M. Prawiravata commanding Jantana Regiment. Mendarat Vala Desa of Becakan = Jon van Digwiry. [^p38-2]

---

- **17 July**: Van IJsseldijk -> Riede. Inquiry into attack on a prauw on Vala River belonging to Capt. Ji Vok Liang.
- **11 July**: Van IJsseldijk -> Van O. Planting of pepper - difficult in rainy season. Indigo fabric NOT yet finished.
- **28 July**: Van IJsseldijk -> Van O. Appointment of second wife as Raden Ayu [^p39-1]. Name of R. Ayu: Dipati Anom.
- **15 Sept 1794**: Van IJsseldijk -> Van O. Arrival of Stralgeld payment for HB II.
- **10 Sept 1794**: Van IJsseldijk -> Van O. Dealing with Sultan's oldest but unofficial daughter = R. Ayu. Anca Sindureja. [^p39-2]
- **27 Oct 1794**: Van IJsseldijk -> Van O. Dismissing Bp. Won [^p39-3] Mn R.T. Sastradiningrat [^p39-4] and Raden Rongga PWDII appointed. Earlier dismissed as Bp. Won in 1790 by HB I. Sastradiningrat given Panglipang, father-in-law of DN, and 1500 cacahs for upkeep. Independent Jwon R. Rongga. Son-in-law HB II (? Prawirasentika) Bp. Danuwinata given rank R.T.

NvB 1794 - 1794 (Yogya)

13 Oct 1794
Van Ryck -> Van O.

Agreement HB II to Pepper Plantations on lands (?) ceded to V.O.C.

Plantations should be brought as soon as possible to necessary broadness.

Javanese workers there discharged from all forms of *Heerendienst* (labour services).

Van Ryck: would remain free of any oppression from their own heads or European overseer.

See that no displacement of people from Sultan's lands to V.O.C. lands.

Recual GHB II to allow measurement of land near annexation by V.O.C. Jurisdiction guaranteed to be held by Katon / : under British Law.

HB II says cannot persuade Bupatis to plant pepper - because already have to cede land for estate. Fear that inhabitants would be brought into poverty (te veel bewaard, tot misnoegdheid, ja zelfs tot armoede).

Great prejudice against this estate in Loano and Pang [unresolved: possibly Pangéran or HB II].

Promises compensating money / weather other for Katon for pepper. Lands would follow -

PLAN TO EXTEND ACTIVITIES OF INDIGO FACTORY
No commerce in pepper to be allowed in the VL as monopoly remains with V.O.C.

Returning messenger to DRI [..] to hook up exchange of land in LOANO.

PB IV to G.G. van Overstraten (Solo Br. 53W), 10 Jul. 1796: R.T. Janrawijaya placed as R. Adip. Rangkunpraja instead of R. Adip. Jayaningrat.

ID to ID, 11 Jul. 1796: Grandson (latu PB I) raised as Crown Prince in Solo (see also ID to ID, 5 Jun. 1796).

Van IJ - van Overstraten, 4 Mar. 1795: Completing buildings in Yogya fortress by MN Regent. To build new or repair old one-ton officer's buildings, nipa roofs. New pepper pakhuis completed. Bet Pomp. HB II gives orders to PRI to see to repairs.

Van IJ - van Overstraten, 14 Mar. 1795: MB II raised R. M. Nuraman as P. Pipajana.

ID to ID, 4 May 1795: Concerning troops to be sent by MB II to Batavia.

Van IJ - van Ruede, 3 Jul. 1795: Arrival in Solo of P. Aria Kusumanidiningrat from Ceylon.

ID to ID, 26 Oct. 1795: Death of P. Purwanigara, eldest son-in-law of MB II, Nong P. Ngalchi.

ID to van Overstraten, 9 Nov. 1795: R.T. Mertalaya, grandson P. Ng. appointed as Onta Regent + 500 cacahs.

ID to ID, 22 Sept. 1795: Arrival of 6 English ships at Pulo Bangpang, nr. Banyuwangi. Capt. Setwell demands provisions. Fear of attack on Oonthock = Van IJ told to keep an eye on coast. MB II to be informed & E. MN. Regents to help watch in Regencies to come to aid of Gezaghebber d Oonthock troops if necessary. But does not want to worry MB II unduly = already known about war in England & fall of Malakka. Real aims/intentions of MB II a mystery, but outwardly loyal to V.O.C. Once English really begin attack on Java then Dutch should press Sunan/Sultan to honour their promises to give troops + Dutch dragoons to accompany troops.

Nle Solo. Br. 131. Bijlagen c - van Nes: Nota over den P. Adip. MN te Surakarta-Sala 30 Jan. 1847. "Acte van Verzekering voor den P. Adip. Mangkoenegoro 1 Nov. 1790 te Surakarta".

*   van IJ - van Reede. 25 Dec. Nong R. Rongga PWD II marries R. Ay. Natijah d.v. MB II. P. Pipanana m. Nistay R. Rongga PWD II. Nota van de schikkingen die er tusschen zijn H. den Keizer van Pakoe boena IV en den W.E.G. Achtbare Mr P.G. van Overstraten.
*   van IJ - van Overstraten. 26 Dec. 1795. Tuss. MB II of arrival of English ships in Bay of Pampang.
*   14 Jan. 1796. PWD III appointed as Bp wedana because of fatick [fatigue/sickness]. An Nol. Br. no. 53.
*   17 Dec. 1795. Nota van Schikking PB - v. Overstraten, omtrent eerbiedbewijzingen P. Prangwedana aan PB IV, 1795. See M Wintu pp. 32-31 on MH-PD.
*   5 Jumadilawal Dimakir 1722. Saturdays PWD to appear in unhal court clothes on Alun-Alun to take part in tournament.
*   Also on Mondays / Thursdays to appear at Pasebaan to stand in same place as P. Mangkubumi. P. Buminata to greet PB IV when he comes out of Kraton.
*   To use name small Pasebaan as grandfather. Relations could erect another in same place if necessary.
*   If PB IV waiting. PB IV would need messengers to P. Buminata to say whether he would appear or not on that day - if not PWD could go home.
*   On all feast days - PWD to appear on Pasebaan.
*   If G.G. other dignitaries to court. PWD to take precedence after MB / Buminata; if stool given to these two princes - stool also given to PWD.
*   To carry widang at his side.
*   If PB IV has Bedaya show in Kraton - PWD to attend [with] princes from Takesturun. Taxes / corvée services to Sunan: PWD excused except for following occasions when expected to make financial contribution:
    1.  Marriage of Sunan or of one of children / brother.
    2.  When G.G. comes to court.
    3.  If Prabayeksa (priv. apartment of Sunan) repaired or rebuilt.
    4.  Ibid for Mesjid Agung.
    5.  Numbang [for] Residency (Haji).
*   2 x p.a. Phasa / Mulud to pay 50-60 Rowe Reals to be free of Gladhek services.
*   2 Buffaloes p.a. to Buminata.
*   To pay same honors to Sunan as Buminata / Mangkubumi + conduct himself like those princes.
*   Only Sunan / Res. could give PWD orders + orders of Sunan not to exceed usual honors enumerated above.
H 689

AVJ 1795-6 (Notes to G.G. N.E. Coast unless otherwise stated)

#### 4 March 1795 (Res Yogya — G.G. Java's N.E. Coast)
Fortress in Yogya: Almost complete. Houses for sergeant/book-keeper (backrower) built by Eastern Regent. VOC servant can easily move into the fort. Pepper warehouse renewals/additions: But cannot be begun until E.M.N. Regent reaches Yogya at the end of this year. Officers report on "completion of fortress."

#### 14 March 1795 (Res Yogya — G.G. N.E. Coast)
MBI receives unofficial brother R.M. Wiranata P. Dipanagara [^p46-1]. (Rebels 1822 — exiled to Moluccas)

#### 3 July 1795 (Res Pala — G.G. N.E. Coast)
Arrival in Java of P.A. Notokusumaningrat [^p46-2] (previously exiled to Ceylon).

#### 26 Oct 1795 (Res Yogya)
MBI reports death of son-in-law, only official son of P. Ngabehi, P. Purwanegara.

#### 9 Nov 1795 (Res Yogya)
MBI reports appointment of grandson Ngabehi as Outer Bupati — name R.T. Martalaya [^p46-3] — late Ratu.

#### 25 Dec 1795 (Res Yogya)
Marriage of daughter of MBI / R. Aj. [^p46-4] Adijanti [^p46-5] with HN Regent R. Rongga Prawiradiga [^p46-6] (but see letter van Reede 1725). Young brother of MBI, P. Dipanagara, visiting R. Rongga.

#### 15 Jan 1796 (Res Yogya)
MBI reports R. Rongga PWD [^p46-7] because of eye sickness, nearly completely loses eyesight. Pensioned - position given to R. PWD [^p46-8] son-in-law of MBI.

Register 1774 Book y klipn kept in a secret chest in archives. Oct. 1796. Mvo Java's N.O.K. Solo Yogya mut nal guaranty lanis etc. klipn rigintu. (Nirat Ebnt Anyar). Sunan: 85,0450 cakahs. Sultan: 87,050 Giyanti. But at 1774 count ora. 100,000.

Memorie met derzelver Bijlagen tot naricht voor den Heer Johan Frederik Baron van Reede tot de Parkler aankomend Gouverneur en Directeur van Java's Noord-oost-kust ingericht en overgeven door Mr Pieter Gerardus van Overstraten geliegeerd Commissaris en Gour. over Gele Nederlandsch Indië en gaand Gour. en Dir. bij vertrek naar Batavia in 1796.

Reason for Sunan's refusal to allow new Rigintu 1793-4: Because each believes has more land than other court & fear that if Rigintu made, process of evening out / balancing land would be insisted upon.

Solo Yogya = Believe V.O.C. can tip scales in favour one or other court = 1790 gives Solo a right.

Without presence of Dutch = Rulers would beat each other throats. Solo pregnant & plans to redress balancing power in Central Java in its favour.

#### British - Ceylon.
British capture Ceylon = & Javanese Exiles there including 2 scions of Kartasura court = P. Adipati Amangkurat / mas Ambiba.

Fear of Solo = Old prophecy in Java that one Amangkurat family would rule again one day in Java.

---

If English get to Java = would be in a position to make prophecy come true.

Present parlous condition of V.O.C. requires doubling of prudence with regard to courts.

Guard E.P.C. [^p49-1] against any contacts between English-court.

Van Overstraaten doubts PB IV [^p49-2] as cautious/suspicious (bijgelovig) as Van Greve makes out in 1790.

"Young, shrewd and full of life."

P. Aria Kusumaningrat: Bad influence on PB IV.

PB IV marries 3rd wife d.o. Bp. of Pamekasan in 1791.
Crown Prince born = 1785.
Appointed c.r. [^p49-3] in Aug. 1792 by Van Overstraaten.
Crown Prince—doted on by PB IV but little done about his education (zeer verwaarloosd). [^p49-4]
Character could might be spoiled by too much doting.

*   2 children of 3rd wife:
    *   Princess—Ratu Pembayun (born 1795).
    *   Prince—(P. Purbaya) (born 1796).

Nusateng Ratu = given to P. Mangkubumi but had since died (i.e., pre-1796). [^p49-5]

Marriage link Ratu Kencana + P. Mangkubumi.

P. Buminata m. d.o. Bp. of Pati.

Mangkubumi more fiery/energetic.
Buminata gentle/subtle.
Friendship of these princes—E.P.C. no PB IV v. fond of them.

2. MvO
Van Overstraten
1796 Oct.

PB IV R.A. Nikartaji brought up by MN I [^p50-1]. But now returns to Nalatraton. Plan PB IV to marry Nikartaji to Yogyakarta must be foiled by getting her married to 2nd Nong B.P. Numenip [^p50-2].

Pangéran

But ruling Numenip not keen for sons to marry Javanese princess but would bow to V.O.C. pressure. Too expensive and too dangerous.

Eldest Nong P. Numenip leads a very debauched life; not suitable.

Younger Nong conducts Numenip well—a major; a battalions Numenippers in Oosthoek.

HB II between 1792–1796 hated by brothers and Yogyakarta bupatis. If disturbances in Java, many Yogyakartans would desert HB II and join PB IV who had talent of making himself loved by subjects. PB IV talent for making himself loved.

Despotic/unjust character HB II = 26 April 1796. Van Ov. warn him of desperate situation in Java.

HB II tied up to this woman but would not embroil himself in hostilities and Java without approval of V.O.C. possesses enough sense for that.

14 sons = 30 daughters of HB II alive in 1796.

20 Aug. 1792 at Limah Dhuwur (kraton Yogya) Crown Prince recognized by Van Ov. Possesses a very good character = 27 years old.

---

Mangkudiningrat:
In disfavor but lives in a very reduced circumstances (in another fashion).
But conducts himself in a v. quiet way.

Ngabehi hopes to see MP appointed over head of co. [^p51-1]

[**Demang**] = married to d. of P.I. = Van den Bergh
Already reports v. bad things about him in 1780.
But makes no move during succession in 1792.

Ratu Bendara = v. much respected by other members of Royal Family.

Refuse of HB I to say respects to an incoming S.S. in Semarang.

[**But Point Eventually Resolved by Art. 24 of contract of 1792 whereby S.E. allowed to instruct felicitations to be given in Semarang and not Batavia.**]

Affair ended in 1788. PB IV also agrees to Article.
28 Dec. 1795 = MN I dies aged 71 years old.

[**P. Prangwedana allowed to succeed to 4,000 cacahs but not allowed to inherit MN I's title of P. Adipati.**]
[**Should enjoy position after Solo Crown Prince.**]

[**Trouble to get agreement of PB IV to successions.**]
Much trouble to win PB IV over to agree to inheritance of Prangwedana & difficulty in 1795.

But 25 Jan. 1796 allowed by PB IV to succeed to 4,000 cacahs & name P. Aria Prabu PWP.
Need of V.O.C. to use PWP as a balance to Suran.

3. MvO van Overstraten.

PB IV shows signs of complete loyalty to V.O.C. After death of MNI in 1785 = V.O.C. no longer pays N.p. 4,000 for upkeep of troops. Equal division of inheritance of MNI but no mention of Van R's appropriations = divided up on advice of priests. Need for V.O.C. to choose successor for PB IV in case of his sudden demise.

1790: Exchange of visits by Patih of Mataram/Yogya to each other's kratons to iron out outstanding difficulties between courts.

**Yogya mutual compliments 1790**

But mutual compliments could not be exchanged between rulers via Patih because of sickness of PB IV + marriage celebrations in Mataram = Ratu Kencana. But do not go through. PB I dies before relations could be restored via Patih.

Only 4,000 N.p. given yearly to MNI in 1790 = 1,000 set aside in 1793 for P. Aria Kusumadiningrat who had recently returned from Ceylon to get him away from PB IV and keep him at Semarang.

PB IV falls out with Kusumadiningrat in Semarang and asks V.O.C. to exile him from Java = sent to Batavia in Aug 1795. Looked after in Batavia by Commissioner for Native Affairs.

**PB IV falls out with MN Prince Kusumadiningrat**
HB I wants van Or. to be in Yogya at the time of his death = But reluctant to call van O. in vain.

P.B. IV says (sealed letter) that in the event of his death, guardianship not to be given to van O. or another V.O.C. official + Patih / E. West Inner Bupati.

Agreed to by van O. in letter 12 Jun. 1792.

Prangwedana + his house no danger to Solo court = Because completely loyal to Dutch.

R.T. Natawijaya [transcribed as R.J.] (R. Adip. Mangkupraja) succeeds as Solo Patih in 1796.

R. Adip. Jayaningrat dismissed because was corrupt / harsh government on common man.

Mangkupraja = appears to be a skilled / intelligent Javanese. Seems attached to Dutch.

Danureja I = has good heart to V.O.C. already 83 years old - lived out.

Natayuda = falls into disfavour. Only an Adjunant tuwon now. Hatred of Natadiningrat for Natayuda = ND works through wife (Ratu Bendara) to ensure downfall of Natayuda.

Van O. doesn't take up question + because critical time for V.O.C.
4. Mvo van Overstraten

People say that HB II had dismissed Natakusuma from powerful positions in order that he should not be in the running to succeed as Patih when DRI died.

Despotic Natakusuma, Rule = New Patih would have no choice but to bow to his orders rather than acting as a bridge to Dutch.

Manuhra = a v. obliging = courteous Javanese but not much talent.

Meeting van Ovt [Overstraten] + PB IV [Pakubuwana IV] at Kartasura: at first PB IV wishes to deny undertaking to give lands to Prangwedana. 

Van Nieuwkerken = possesses v. sound judgement + has talent to make himself known + trusted by Javanese / much local knowledge.

1792: HB II asks that van Nieuwkerken should be allowed to continue at court as first Resident, but falls into disfavour + asks to be replaced as Resident.

HB II - Van IJ [Van IJsseldijk] quarrel.

Van Ovt corresponds to HB II about Van IJ. HB II replies v. abruptly that [he] wants to get a new Resident by Aug. 1796 but would allow Van IJ to remain another year. Van Ovt says that Van IJ would have to stay on at least another year.

Because of danger for invasion = Van Ovt does not dare to be firm + HB II on a replacement of Van IJ.

Solo garrison 1796 = 183 men.
Yogya = 193 men.

2 leerlingen in de Javaans Taal. 1 at each court.
But because lack of European troops = none have to be sent up to Semarang.

1780s.
9 companies van den Burgh = numbering Infantry at each court = 100.

Forts at:
*   Ungaran = "De Ontmoeting"
*   Salatiga = "De Hersteller"
*   Boyolali = "De Veldwachter"

Need to repair fort at Boyolali = already undertaken in 1793.

HB II = To build a new pakhuis (warehouse) for fort (Mancanagara). To be built by inhabitants.

NB I too economical to consider laying out fort between Yogya and Jambu hills. Decision: instead to build fort at Marbon.

Patch D.R.I. putting of Yogya co-op on fort until repair to Boyolali fort completed.

Gladhays.
Netting up a gladhag at kraton = already gladhag set up at Jangtingan, Adilangu, Marbon.

Increase in prices: Japara on N. coast districts.

Priest Kyai Pugu = at Solo, 1st aparte brieg = 31 Dec. 1795.

Nothing more heard of family of P. Rongga Jajar.

van Overstraten.

Exile P. Braytak/norna to Ceylon in 1788.

2 Brothers:
*   Rongga Kuning (Nirany) [^p56-1]
*   Nanawijaya (Nanawijaya)

Killed last Puriy battles between MBI - MNI in 1750.

Ulijami [^p56-2] = Rice producing district, most heavily inhabited. All western district, pop. rice producing, area.

*   Farica Pertu & Capitan Cina Nemarony. Jan let to.
*   Retu r.r. 5,000 p.a.
*   Jan to ok.
*   300 koyangs of rice p.a. @ 15 r.r. per koyang.

Capitan Cina = Trade war pop. r.well = freely works.
But subject to numerous Bayin = although land v. fertile.

Capitan Cina has not enough rice paid for nowing.

Advantageous for V.O.C. to keep land rented to a Chinese.

Large contrast between Ulijami / Wirasa.

Pekalongan = v. suitable for rice indigo - although subject to Bayin. R.Hip. Jayadikrat so much afraid to payen [^p56-3].

*   **Punak Ana** and **Adilangu** for many Javanese (criminals).

*   **P.** A very fine (Braaf) Javanese from an old **Eminent Family**... [text crossed out] ...whom Bignena Regery when vacant.

J.36. Very few boats now being built for V.O.C. at Rembang. All wood now being taken from Blora area because heavy wood in Rembang/Semarang area becoming [illegible] scarce.

Lasem – 2,000 cacahs under Jum. Nuradipura. R.r. 192 ½ cacah money / + 1 panalang boat 9 11-12' raam in length. 6 pikuls cotton thread. [illegible] Bp. [illegible] old first Bupati of Demak.

J.37 On pressure from Van Ov. lays out an Indigo factory.

Tuban = 3,000 cacahs. Jum. Purbanegara. Not favourable reports about him = should keep a watch on him. 6 koyang rice / 325 r.d. cacah money / 6 pikuls cotton thread / 1000 logs. beams.

Laying out of Indigo factory last year.

Island of Bawean = a resty intrigue.

'786 = Benteng built by V.O.C. = Rockhower placed. Good for cocoanut culture / copra. Passes to Begiven to Ja.

J.38 Nidayu = 2,500 cacahs under Res. Gresik. R.J. Natadiningrat. My. Brotang Pan. Madura. V. [illegible] = enslaved to priests. Area delivers [illegible] 325 cacah money 200 koyang rice 6 pikuls cotton thread.
Lamongan = 1,000 cacahs. District for a long time in a languishing situation. 2 Regents in charge—both from Japara—pay little attention to District. 162 cacahs money || 250 coyangs of rice || 3 pikuls cotton thread.

J.39

Gresik 3,200 cacahs. Not what it used to be. [^p59-1] Due to increase in tollgates—laid out by rulers along Bengawan Solo. [^p59-2]

Tumenggung: Arjanagara / Bratanegara.

R. 487 1/2 cacahs money || 160 coyangs of rice || 6 pikuls cotton thread.

Since 1782 wood being brought down to Gresik—felled in Vorstenlanden. 5–600 lengths of timber from Tuban and Rembang.

Surabaya head office of Oosthoek.

J.40

Tumenggung Panji Cakranagara = success R.T. Cakranagara. Mastum Jayadirana. [^p59-3] Shrewd / intelligent / cordial man, other not so shrewd. Very loyal to V.O.C.

Mother of Tum. Panji Cakranagara = Rd. [Raden] of Panji Madura.

7,000 cacahs = annual contingent = 140 1/4 || 1,000 coyangs of rice || 24 pikuls cotton thread.

---

7 M. N.O.K. / Van Overstraten 1796.

Right over Madura handed over by V.O.C. to Surrender by P.B. II in 1743.

Divided into 3 Regencies: Madura, Sumenep, Pamekasan. Sumenep / Pamekasan surrendered to V.O.C. 1700 5 Oct.

Madura: 8,000 cacahs. Arosbaya, Balega, Sampang.

Ruled by hereditary ruler = Pan. Cakraningrat. V. gentry well-disposed to V.O.C. weak/naive/too gentle for the Madurese. Finances in poor state.

Kacang hijau = grown in quantity. Rd. 40 per coyang.

Fanatic Mahmud = who had great influence over Cakraningrat = Banished 1782. Trusted adviser.

Mangunsastra = a sly Javanese = Hated by family of Cakraningrat.

Patih = Nine 1794 Mas Aria Maneanagara. A brave Madurese = d. = Second wife of Pan.

Some members of family = Exiles at Bengkulu/Pulo Pinang = now in hands of British.

Madura not v. heavily populated = would find it difficult to produce more recruits for V.O.C. [^p60-1]

Madurese warlike by nature; prone to desert = need strong military discipline. [^p60-2]

Madurese best soldiers amongst Asiatics = better even than Javanese Sepoys. [^p60-3]

Each year = 4,000 Sp. Reaalen.
From Wharbandas Rights

- 60 koyangs green beans.
- 12,000 cans of coconut oil [^p61-1]
- 30 pikuls cotton thread.

1789 produces 8 pikuls extra because of necessary harvest.

Van ov. to Alting, 2 Jan. 1796 (Paats MN I)

N.o.t. 47. "Semarang 2 Jan - 1796"

Van ov. to W.A. Alting, 2 Jan. 1796.

**Deatg MN I**

28 Dec. 1795 = Deatg MN I Dies Early in morning. Van R goes & only a slave to MN Salem secures door of Salem. Grenadiers & Pignet went along Earlier. 

**R.A. Nekartaji Brought up By MNI**

R.A. Nekartaji = Nostag PB IV = Brought up By MN I. Brought to Solo Kraton in a carriage.

**y as Mangkubumi (Yogya)**

Mangkubumi had plan to Enter MN Salem to Take Back R.A. Nikartaji & wreak Revenge on Prangwedana. 

**bought up by Ratu Bendara NOT Imagiri**

Body of MN I Buried at Mengayeg [^p62-1] at Matewih. 

**y MN Trad. Body of MNI Buried in MN SALEM.**

Van R constantly in MN Salem = Except for New Year celebration in res = has to attend that so as not to show fear of pol. upheaval.

PB IV Demands lands Back in Excess of 4,000 cacaho Held By MN I.

23 Dec. 1795 = Gegashebber Oosthoek = Reports that 7 Eng. ships Cast Anchor at Pulea Um Pampang. Take on water/firewood at Pampang.

Agreement on Duties of PWP = Reached 17 Dec. 1795. 

**MB urging attack on PWP**

Id. to Id. 5 Jan. 1796. MB makes urgent representations to PB IV to undertake something v. Prangwedana [^p62-2].

---

But PB IV pays no attention to proposal because Van R. [Van Rijck] already in charge PWD’s [Pakubuwana’s] Dalem.

P. Purbonegara—eldest Mg. [Mangkunegara]—makes demand for 4,000 cacahs—Van R. says no change could be made in agreement by PB IV.

Not only PB IV + brothers but many of new family [are] against [the] succession [of] PWD. MN [Mangkunegara] family [are] against [the] succession [of] PWD.

News of peace between Dutch Republic = French—end period when Holland at war [with] England/France.

23 Jan. 1796: Difficulties in arranging for PWD to succeed to 4,000 cacah.

30 Jan. 1796: Large 3-masted ship seen passing N. coast on way to Bali Strait. (? Eng. warships or Eng. China vaarders).

24 Jan. 1796: Appointed PWD. Apptd. as P. Aria Prabu Pranyodana. [^p63-1]

8 Feb. 1796: Sickness of PWD Noonapta [?]—apptd. fear that he may been poisoned. [^p63-2] Does not believe in Jav. dhukuns—would only accept medicines from V.O.C. hospitalier in Sala.

3 Feb. 1796: HB II [Hamengkubuwana II] to S.G. + RV I [Batavia] promising to come to help V.O.C. + Yogya [if] auxiliaries if attacked by Eng.

Van Ov. to Alting
"Semarang Jan-June 1796"
Disturbances in Kaliwungu = local pop. demonstrate against local Bupati R.T. Natadiningrat.
Gending / Patis = Open Revolt.
Tran in Jan. Taal to Van Der Berg = Sent there. [^p64-1]

Bupati had dismissed without proper reason = Large numbers of Demangs, Mantris, Lurahs + other officials. Places given to those who pay Bupati the most [^p64-2] = money from his personal entourage. Takes money from others who had wanted to get positions = But keeps money, does not give positions.

Negroes & Debauches wives of his subjects.
Increases corvée [^p64-3] + cuts sizes of ricefields = So that could rent out lands to others along main road.
Treats officials in a v. off-hand way.

Bupati confesses faults = Dismissed.
Inhabitants want replacement Bupati from old Bupati family who had ruled them for 200 years.

Nong Kyai Sidik Mataram = (Sunan Asung) had prophesied that on appointment of Post B.P. Kaliwungu Sumadiwirya, a son of P. Lempyangan that no one else than descendants of Sumadiwirya should rule in Kaliwungu & no one else should have grave of Batara Tatang above them.

Nona [?] Numadiwinya exiled to Ceylon in 1794. Mas Wirawijaya = 28 years old, Mas Numawijaya = 21 years old. Wirawijaya appointed as Bp. Kaliwungu = Mas J. Adinegara.

AWPN health improves.

#### Appt. of PWP
ID to ID, 20 Feb 1796. Difficulty of appointment of PWP, people cannot choose a suitable name. PB IV refuses to allow him to succeed in name of grandfather MNI. PB IV suggests 4 names for PWP, but all too low.

PWP then suggests:
* Aria Prabu Amangkurat
* Aria Prabu Mataram

But Mangkunegara = unsuitable for Solo Yogya, Mataram would annoy Yogya.

Van Overstraten [^p65-1] himself suggests name of Aria Pringwidara to avoid all argument in Nalat to hurry up appointment of PWP.

1. 3 eldest illegitimate sons of MNI press for divisions [of] 4,000 [?], chaos amongst family members. Mangkunegara had put 3 sons of MNI up to asking for lands. Nona Desist [?] eldest apologizes to Van R.
2. PB IV makes difficulties about ceremonial for swearing in of PWP; eventually takes place in fort under a durian. PWP does not want to take oath of allegiance to PB IV but forced to do so; if MNI had taken oath [to] PB III.

At taking of oath = P. Ngabehi, Solo Patih/Trun. Sasrawijaya present.

---

Van Or - Alting, Feb. - Apr. 1796. N.O.K. 47 'Namarang Jan.-June 1796' Apptmt. of PWP.

PB IV whispers in PWP's ear at time of taking oath that he hopes PWP would take side of Javanese more than V.O.C. PWP now replies that would depend on whether Javanese treated him well and remained on good terms V.O.C. — otherwise would not do so.

P. Aria Danumadiningrat (in exile in Semarang) says that MB had approached PB IV numerous times to attack Saling MN. MB planned attack on Saling MN II. PB IV had often discussed it with Danumadiningrat who had always dissuaded PB IV saying it would lead to trouble for Solo Conat.

KA to be allowed to go back to Solo Conat in 2-3 days. HB II rules in such a capricious way that nothing done, subjects hate him.

10 Feb. 1796. HB II informs Van Or. of dismissal of Natunungrum as DR I. NY had done by hatred of ND towards him.

Cannot take up Yijair and HB II because V.O.C. not powerful enough.

Solo/Yogya to be informed of Treaty of Friendship between France-Holland. But would have to explain that Holland not to follow Fr. Rev. principles — which had been frequently painted in such black colours to rulers.

**Notes on Solo Court:**
*   Engages in gambling/oppressions.
*   Sasrawijaya = Nong Natadiningrat I dismissed by PB III on recommendation of Ratu; served as *inner* Patih, the Bupati of court. Had married 2 sisters of PB IV who had both died.
*   During 1790 disturbances = abroad to Rnm. of Kalangs. Because falls into disfavour of PB IV's santri advisers.
*   Sasrawijaya (Mangkubumi) after 1790 gets back old position. Because too loyal to V.O.C.
*   Intelligence showed Nax. of a soft character & humour. Not greedy/covetous for money.
*   Van Or. able to head the plan of PB IV to appoint Mangkubumi or P. Ana Kusumadiningrat as Patih.
*   PB IV corresponds with Van Or. himself without knowledge of courtiers = to keep negotiations secret.
Apologies for Faults in Style Etc.

22 May 1796: [Tang Barda].

AVJ 1796 (Yogya) -1796-

**15 Feb, Res Yogya**
Past Monday HBII dismisses R.T. Natayuda as Kliwon: replaced as Tum. Mijil. R.T. Danukusuma takes place of R.T. Natayuda. Place of Danukusuma taken by R.T. Mertanegara (later DRI). Place of Mertanegara as Dalem Regent taken by R.T. Jumadiningrat.

**4 Mar 1796, Res Yogya**
P. Adiwigana takes place. 3 Mar 1796 - Death of sister of M.H.B. R.Ayu. Jarakusuma.

**12 July 1796, Res [..] Yogy/Sala?**
News of dismissal of Tala Rara - Dayanungrat: appointment of R. Adin. Mangkupraja.

**25 July 1796, Res Sala**
Appointment of P. Adipati Anom (PBIV). R.M. Rauda reg. Braking PBIV -> P. Panular. [^p70-1]

**4 Oct 1796, Res Yogya**
Bussersck conveyed that if Eng. make a landing, HBII would send mercenaries to Batavia reinforcement troops. Would not do it under same conditions as 1793. New regulations payment troops + leads troops. Each Regent to produce troops according to Number Head [^p70-2] received:

> But these never willingly employ members of their family or accredited followers - usually take common Negorij folk (Yogya) to a pike, an unserviceable rifle, a miserable horse, a brief and yet more miserable rider - to the sum total of a Mataram soldier and his equipage.

## AVJ 1797 (Yogya)

**26 Jan 1797 (Res. Yogya):** MB II dismisses Regent Majifan R.T. Yudakusuma from all his service. Plan to appoint under-regent does not go through; rice distributed to other Regents. All are now administered by DRI.

R.T. Natayuda given 100 cacahs by MB II; seems to be winning back favor. MB II now has 500 cacah. (Re: Ratu Kedhaton)

**2 Aug 1797 (Res. Yogya):** MB II appoints son-in-law R.T. Jasrawiningrat as Kyai Adip Purwadiviningrat. [^p72-1] Rank directly under ORI. [^p72-2]

New Regent R.T. Jasrawiningrat placed as Kliwon in place of R. PWD III.

**19 Mar 1797 (Res. Yogya):** 18 Mar: Death of aunt of MB II, R. Ayu Ngabehi.

**3 April 1797 (Res. Yogya):** Dismissing 2 Bupati Dalem: Kyai Tum. Mangundipura, P. Dipakusuma. 2 Bupati Jaba: R.T. Danukusuma, R.T. Mertanayana. 

> *Changes among Nayakas:*

Kliwon of Patih ORI:
*   Bupati Dalem: R.T. Jumadiningrat (son-in-law of HB II) – 1000 cacah
*   2nd Bupati Dalem: R. Jayengsari (R.T. Purwadipura) (Brother of Ratu Kencana Wulan)

1000 cacah taken from P. Dipakusuma; son Purwadipura – DPK gets + 100 cacahs + Bawat (state umbrella). Demoted to lowest Dalem BP. Loses 500 cacahs.

---

### 1806
8 Apr. 1806. Report on gambling in Yogya.

**Gambling in Yogya**

Moor (Bengali?) Malawad Mira Mohidin comes from Semarang 2x to Yogya to gamble.

Looses sum of 4,200 Ronde maaten [in] gambling:
*   Gambles in Chinese kampong.
*   & Baba Gem Bo, Chinese Bandhar kamolan.
*   & Brother of Surabaya Capitan Cina.
*   Mas Ng. Danukrama.
*   2 sons of P. Ngabehi.
*   & Mantri of Kipatilan.

Looses 2,200 Reals in Ho. of Danukrama in diamond/money. Plays Chinese card game Piki.

Gambles in Ho. of P. Měntanegara [and] Ho. of R. Panji Nuryingrana [and] Ho. of son of Sumadiwirya: gambles at dice in various journeys; looses 2,000 Reals in diamonds/money; all money taken from father.

## 1799
(Yogya)

- **30 Jan, Res Yogya:** HB II gets permission for MN Bupati to return home.
- **3 Aug 1799, Res Vala:** Arrival E. MN Regent in Vala to provide labour services for Mataram.
- **19 Aug 1799, Res Yogya:** Death of DRI dies at 2 p.m. (54) Javanese years old. [^p80-1]
- **[No date], [No location]:** HB II wants Mertavigana to follow him. Married d. HB II / granddaughter DRI.
- **21 Aug 1799, Res Yogya:** Affairs of DRI entrusted to Mertavigana.
- **20 Aug 1799, Res Yogya:** Burial of DRI at Imagiri at request of HB I. [^p80-2]
- **9 Sept 1799, Res Yogya:** Received letter from Batavia authorising appointment of DRI. [^p80-3]
    - R.T. Natayuda re-appointed as 1st Kanvong DRI.
    - Ane Mandura (eldest son of DRI) as 2nd Kanvong DRI.
    - R.T. Danukusuma (2nd son of DRI) as assistant DRI.
- **20 Sept 1799, Res Vala:** DRI II to Semarang following 3 Tumenggung: Kyai Tum. Jendunegara, Mas [?] Jumadipura, R.T. Purwadipura [^p80-4] to give letters to Batavia & Semarang. N.E. Coast. [?] new appointment of Patih DRI.

## Van Reede MvO [1801]

Java N.O.K. no. 47. MvO van R tot de Park. aan N. Engelhard.

Memorie voor Den WelEdele Gestrengen Heer Nicolaus Engelhard, Raad Extraordinair van N.I. en aankomende Gouverneur en Directeur van Java's Noord-Oost kust, door den WelEdele Gestrengen Heer Johan Frederik van Reede tot den Parkeler, Raad Extraordinair, uittredend Gouverneur en Directeur, bij deszelfs vertrek naar Batavia, gedateerd Semarang, ultimo Augustus 1801.

PB IV [^p83-1] gives impressions being considerably less impetuous & inclined to intrigues than in earlier years = acts as trusted ally of V.O.C.

Saturdays makes tour on horseback on thine. During day always tells Res. if goes out = Sometimes also goes out at night incognito - does not tell Res. = only accompanied by few escort.

In kraton = always encircled by children = amuses himself horse riding / watching wayang / & reading Javanese MSS. [^p83-2]

3rd wife = Ratu Kimana Whyn = M. 1791. 3 sons / 2 daughters = etc. / 2 sons died in childhood. 

One s. remains = R.A. Natariah = Born 6 Rabingulakir 1720 (1793).

(Purbaya) R.A. Kastandiah born = 8 Ruwah 1721 (1794). R.M. Gnoti Malikinhatien = Born 15 Sura 1723 (1796).

Education Crown Prince neglected.

CP makes himself loved by common people because not oppressive like PB IV.

Lives at PB IV until his Salim [and] P. Mangkubumi completed.

PB IV: 12 sons/12 daughters living by nitis.

P. Mangkubumi (Born 1699) (1773) = Very proud spirit. Takes over monty Banyumas = which he gets after death of P. Prangwedana in 1795.

Great rivalry Mangkubumi/CP = MB = 4 official wives.

Buminata Born 1702 (1776)
- 1st wife = d. of Bp. g Patti = Aria Megatani
- 10 years after marriage = no children.
- M. Rustini Bp. g Patti = But no children also, none amongst nitis (:: BN impotent).

Much gentler personality than MB = more loved by CP.

P. Danureja = Nong Amengkurat. Banished to Ceylon. & his son = R. M. Nemanari = On request of PB IV moved to return to Java.

P. Aria Purbanegara = Nong MN I. M. d. of PB IV. R. A. Nupiah = made Bp. wdn. g E. MN.

P. Ayu Nekartaji = eg. n. of PB IV. Destined for 2nd son P. Numenip = But m. does not go through.

M. Dong Bp. g Surabaya = later Bp. Kudhus.

R.A. Nuba (?) Reg. Pamekasan.

MB I v. Randy Nintenes any one to Death = *unless absolutely necessary = not a vindictive man*
*Even if caught in MB I's neglie(?) = unheard of for a Jav. Ruler.*

Undang II great increase in Night Robberies = if many princes have a hand in them = p. P. Ngabchi Patih at present in gaol.

Beuweg High Jaresg (?) MB II = no concern for common man as MB I now.

*Groothartig en Oploopend van hart* = No one dares to gainsay him - not even Panjis.

Crown Prince = v. like MB I = But although loved by people = no influence on affairs of state. Does not like going out/wandering about or oppressing his subjects = *but has to do so in order to keep his Patha(?) happy.*

Mangkuninegrat = v. careful to follow old style Nyai Jav. customs = much loved by subjects = *Roth Ratu Anom from old way. Mataram Dynasty*
Shrewd judgement = has skill to win local pop.
Does not appear in European circles = closely watched by mp. Crown Prince.
But shows himself to be reasonably well disposed to Europeans.
Nathakusuma = Through mother, old Kedu dynasty = has great influence in Kedu = much apanage land there. Of disturbances 1813.

Courteous/soft character makes him loved by whole court. Acts quietly/honestly = does not press ambition. HB I had prophesied that [he] would one day play an important role. 

Many Princes/Bupatis = secretly attached to him. E.g. Mangkuningrat who shows him special respect.

HB II had promised HB I that [he] would not take anything away from positions NK. But soon after accession = [he] takes away some of NK's ornaments. = Friendship NK/HB II broken.

Nederburgh visits Ngayogyakarta = wants to visit court. But plan has to be postponed. Because HB II refuses to pay proper respects to Nederburgh as a Commissioner = wants to place him on same level as ministers.

Natan prepared to receive him [with] proper seat = But Natan refers to difficulties which had followed on Van Imhoff's visit to Solo in 1746 leading to Mangkubumi's departure from court = HB II not prepared to receive Nederburgh = HB II make difficulties about "how" = 3/4 high ranking Dutch officials who would all have to sit next to each other would make HB II ashamed in the eyes of his subjects.

## Ned. Visit to Yogya cancelled.

But HB II persists in refusal to accord Ned. any greater respect than for a Gov. y Java. (? N.o.k.) [^p87-1] 

Although would met him at Valley y Jean [^p87-2] & would send Crown Prince to Kalavan.

No stipulation for such a visit in contracts & Yogya.

But would treat Ned.’s visit to Solo if did not go on to Yogya, as great insult = Says that God had chosen him "To uphold the honours the Jav. Nation" [^p87-3]

Jnr conference & brothers ensures their loyalty = to adopt common front = & makes Rice Balls with his own hand which they all eat.

Pre [^p87-4] also recruited at conference : Pre Aug. 1799 (? 1796-1799)

R. Panji Padma Wijaya - Treasurer HB II = would produce money.
Lifewatch near new oath of loyalty to HB II & money prepared to buy armour for Prajurits [^p87-5]

Ned. has to postpone visit to Both Yogya/Solo. 

Sending of Patjis [^p87-6] to Semarang instead = word "leen" "Leenheer" [^p87-7] do not occur in Jav. Trans: of contracts.

Martingh = speaks/writes excellent Jav. would not have let words slip by = But changed? in Yogya copy.

Visit Van Ov. tells Van JJ on return to Batavia in 1798 to get reinforcements to garrisons in Solo/Yogya.

14 Jan. 1800: Reply from S.G. that reinforcements to Natal Yogya garrison could not be attempted until reinforcements arrive from Holland.

V.O.C. powerless to force HB II to do something against his will = viz. O.G. Neil's visit.

#### HB II → NK

Capriciousness of HB II with regard to NK. NK refuses to give up certain privileges accorded by HB I despite HB II's insistence.

> Special E-bèks (shield / escutcheon) on his horse on last Saturday tournament 2 May 1800. NK has to appear + new shield. [^p88-1]

Jul. 1800: HB II wants to order [the] local resident to get down from his horse if [he] crosses state pusakas being carried around town or to hide until ornaments had passed. 

But Res. van den Berg absolutely refused. HB II punishes messengers who bring Res's message.

Jan. 1801: Patih punished for behaviour at New Year's party of Res. HB II insists that toast of HB II should be drunk before that of members [of] Council of Indies.

HB II wants no presents on occasion of 3rd marriage [of] Buminata.

HB II not at all loved by his subjects, despite pledging of faith by brothers/regents at night lest trouble would fall away from HB II.

P. Aria Kunnadiningrat and my family returned from Ceylon—gets allowance 1,000 [d.?].

V.O.C. policy to keep exiled princes—banished from court. Mangkupradja well disposed towards V.O.C.—a [shrewd?] / intelligent Javanese.

#### Yogya.

Mangkunegara = Ningrat Natakusuma from Kedu Dynasty. [^p90-1]
* Not but indolent/slow = grootch van Hart.
* Judgement not sharp = but applies himself to business.
* Always follows instructions of HB II and never dies [?] except on feast days.
* Many affairs left months on end without solution because HB II feels his prestige would be affected.

DR II well disposed to European = [?] Natayuda.
Natadiningrat = bitter enemy of NY, but cannot prevent appointment of NY as tuwon after appointment of DR II.

Dutch playing appointment for DR II next and [?] for NY. Trans. left blank = Res. Yogya told to find old copy of appointment of DR II. But original copy of appointment of DR II in kraton, only copy in Res. house.

R.T. Yudanegara = [?] BR. Banjarran = made Patih of lands given by V.O.C. "in leen" to HB II.

van R considers it: Better to leave PR II without site van [^p91-1] as telling, rather than engage in quarrel with HB II over word "leen". HB II terribly exercised over word "leen".

Acte not yet given (1801).

J.G. van den Berg = unusual skill in Javanese.

Aug. 1797 Dr. Waterloo takes over from 2nd Res. Palm in Yogya because latter's poor health.

Garrison = Yogya - 164
Solo = 188
Ned. fixes number garrison for Solo/Boyolali/Salatiga - Angaran = 300 men. (3 companies)

Sunan/Sultan asked to build a Pepper Packhouse, net o' Yogya Res. Better than Solo. But E.MN. Rycato march return suddenly to Transalan when Brit. blockading Batavia. Pathnigen not finished.

V.O.C. Cannons:
HB II asks for old V.O.C. cannons = which V.O.C. about to melt down = now at cost o' scrap metal.

Jucag: Reconsidering building of fortresses on Jambu hills, continuing fear o' invasion = appearance Brit ships constantly along coast.

Decline in numbers o' Fanatics/Japas.
Prophet Kyai Tjen = bought out by V.O.C. No tent in Solo. Prophecies favourable to V.O.C.

*   **J-127:** V.O.C. price paid for *kledjes* too low. Although now being produced in greater quantities than before, price raised from N.D. 11.32 to 18.32.
*   **J.228:** N.N. Tobacco for European taste: but no strain found to be suitable. Most tobacco for Eur. in Java imported from America.
*   **J.229:** Trade in tobacco falls off because of [hostilities?].
*   **Oosthoek:** *Regunary* van O.V. to counteract netting up of *tolgates*. *Gishong Jembakan* to in *lidhu* because would act like *tolgates*.
*   **J.340:** *Elives* in *Senjaraan Taal* appto. at *óles*.

*   **15 Jan 1800 Res Solo:** Departed via MN Regents.
*   **28 Jan 1800 28 Oct Res Yogya:** Keeps an eye out for "Presti" (Paaper) in the countryside.
*   **21 Jan 1801 Res Yogya:** MB II gives permission for Yogya MN Regent to return home.
*   **26 June 1801 Res Solo:** Marriage sister of PO IV + Regent Pamekasan: Prawirodirjo - R.T. Cakradiningrat.
*   **1 Oct 1801 Res Solo:** Arrival messengers of OR II Sultan Mangkupraja: Following Head Jaksa: Nitipraja, Ngabehis: Kertadirimeja, Kartawikrama, Panadiwinia, Ungawicara, Mertadiwirya, Citrayuda - Lundas Ventaras of Kadipaten.
*   **21 Nov 1801 Res Yogya:** On occasion of good disposition MB II: After tournament on Pasirbaan: speaks to HB II in Kraton: Inquiry into ADMU of PWD III in Madura: trustworthy person would be appointed. Kyai Adipati PURWADININGRAT Head Regent Hajitan:
*   **[Back]:** Split between Natakusuma - HB II. But NK sometimes appears at court. On occasion bringing in letters etc. Very clever/shrewd prince would begin NOTHING as long as NOT been provoked by MB II. Which is against the peace of the kingdom.

## 1801

Pj. Brieven 20.48, Prokyo van den Berg, Engelhard Brieven 1801–02.

van den Berg to Engelhard, 12 Aug. 1801: Ambassador of HB II to Semarang for appointment of Engelhard. [^p97-1] P. Adinegara R.T. Natadiningrat. To leave on 25 Aug. for Semarang.

ID. to ID., 17 Sept. 1801: Return of HB II's ambassador from Semarang. van d. B to send HB II small calls from time to time like Niberg—HB I. [^p97-2] Report of van Overstraten's death reported to HB II. van Rede in Solo asks for report on HB II court in preparation for Nederburgh's visit to court. HB II asks Eng for Elephant/Persian & Bimanese Horse.

ID. to ID., 21 Sept. 1801: HB II preparing to send Embassy to pay compliments to Niberg as new G.G. Would leave before Puasa before end of Nov.

ID. to ID., 27 Sept. 1801: Serious sickness of Natayuda—visits him in secret. [^p97-3]

ID. to ID., 14 Oct. 1801: HB II to Peranggrahan & Bupatis Pajis. Rumour that HB II would refuse to accept letter relating appointment of Niberg, Niting on Dhampar as had always been the capet HB I. Would not agree to ceremony till after Puasa in 3 months time. HB II asks that Ambassador sent to congratulate Engelhard, PR II & 2 Bupatis to be allowed to go onto Tegal. [^p97-4]

ID. to ID., 20 Oct. 1801: At last allowed audience & HB II to give letters from Engelhard. PR II on Tuesday had to start in rain on river Panbaan, 5 Palem Bupatis, whilst HB II watched journey. Because HB II annoyed about journey of PR II to Tegal Arum. [^p97-5]

Argument: Capitan Cina & Panji of HB II about $4,000 R.R. worth of goods given to HB II but not paid for. HB II wants Regents to pay for them. Would wait until HB II in better humour before broaching topic of ceremonial for appointment of Niberg.

---

Javanese of Eng’n letts to HB II. Badly written.

**28 Oct. 1801:** 80 Dragoons as Bodyguards of Nahan/Naltan at Yogya/Nata court.

**6 Nov. 1801:** v.d.B to van Reede (Semarang) HB II recovered from sickness. “HB II needs all too clearly to take advantage of our miserable situation to show himself superior to us. Because the yearning for glory plagues him terribly.” Wants toast to HB II to proceed to G.G & Raad van Indie although such a thing had never been allowed before. HB II wants to be toasted at least above Raad van Indie.

**26 Oct. 1801:** v.d.B – PR II. v.d.B holding back strangers as retaliation for difficulties of HB II over reception of Eng’n letter. 3rd Inner Reg. 1st Inner Reg. Nangandipura/Numaniningrat suspended by HB II as one knows why. Reappointed within a week.

**8 Nov. 1801:** ID to ID. 
> “Meanwhile I count myself lucky that in my youth I was able to learn Javanese well and that now I know it better than my mother tongue!” 

Thus in Javanese to explain situation clearly to HB II. 
> “It is unbelievably difficult to have dealings because of his hankering after glory & desire for money everything goes badly.”

**Kieuw in pay of HB II?**
Turns a blind eye to nightly robberies of Kyai Adip. Purwadirignrat subjects in Madiun because gets a cut from them & other events occur which create disturbances in interior. PR II too afraid of HB II to talk frankly to him. v.d.B thinks should allow events in Europe to settle down then deal [with] HB II.

**12 Nov. 1801:** ID to ID. 
HB II quest for glory – Sign of decline of influence of V.O.C. that 1st Resident cannot get an interview & Sultan when requested. HB II wants v.d.B to always negotiate through PR II. But Kliwon/PR II always dependant on will of HB II. “HB II’s father who was in many respects a great man, gave his 1st Resident & Patih a free hand in many affairs.”

PJ. Br. 20. 37.

**Jav. Cloth.**

van den Berg to Raad van Politie 24 Sept. 1799: 210 corpyes Jav. cloth being sent to Semarang by L. Leberfeld.

80 " " " for num 7 J. 2, 640 taken by L. Leberfeld.

**HB II [^p99-1] Niz. Help to Dutch 1800**

van den Berg to N. Engelhard 21 Nov. 1801: HB II shown willing in past year when British strongly blockading Batavia to come to their aid, after watching journey on front Parebaan go into kraton.

HB II gives very abrupt answers, usually behaves in that succinet [sic] way to courtiers as well.

Asks HB II to give order to Rongga PWD to act firmly v. robbers/bandits in Eastern MN.

Kyai Adip. Purwadiningrat of Magelang asked to co-operate.

No further news about disharmony HB II - Natakusuma.

Although NK [^p99-2] rarely invited to court, he often goes in on own initiative when letters from Engelhard for Slametans.

If not provoked to extreme, this showy prince would attempt nothing v. HB II.

HB II (v. different from father) is often criticizing V.O.C., caring little for close relations.

NK: Biding his time - hoping to be chosen by V.O.C. as a better ally just as P. Puger had been chosen by Dutch in Semarang instead of Mangkurat II.

Extortions of HB II felt by NK as well as other bupatis. Difficulties of bupatis counters in speaking to HB II, amuses himself in private + wives = pays in young nobles [^p99-3].

van den B only able to carry on contacts in deepest secrecy + various nobles/bupatis.

But would not view any attempts by disgruntled princes to unseat HB II with favour.

van Overstraten ill - English appear again along coast of Java.

---

**DRI [Danureja I; transcribed as PRI] to R. Rongga, Rejeb, Be 1728**

Banditry in E. MN. Disturbances of peace by bandits. Rongga to co-operate with Kyai Adip. Purwadiningrat, Magetan in capture of robbers. Robbers captured and sent to Yogya if caught on spot. If they take refuge in Solo territory, they can be sent to Yogya as a [unclear] run. Rongga to co-operate with R. Adip. Mangkupraja. Similarly in Pasisir area, to contact Pasisir Bupati.

**Waterloo to N. Engelhard, 2 Mar. 1806.**

Talks to HBII about security on borders of Preanger/Cirebon. HBII to give orders to W. MN subjects on occasion of Garebeg Besar to help watch on boundaries near Banyumas/Ledok for fugitives from Cirebon.

**Cirebon, Rangeen (1806)**

HBII asks why couldn't V.O.C. drive out bandits from C. and if Dutch no longer in Cirebon. Perhaps V.O.C. not powerful enough to drive out bandits. Then HBII says V.O.C. doesn't lack strength but keeping it is decide more important in West British. HBII then promises to give necessary orders via Crown Prince. In order to reassure HBII of power V.O.C. tells of reinforcements from Batavia to Cirebon.

**Vanden Burg, Waterloo to Raad van Politie (Semarang), 9 May 1803.**

Head Bandhar (Tan Jin Ning). Difficulty buying up quantity of long cloths demanded by Batavia. Ordinary people no longer like to weave long cloth because not in demand from local traders. Raising also of price per corge from R.R. 16.32 to 18.32 per corge. Need to pay weavers higher prices than given by V.O.C. (Letters 7 Oct. 1798 / 5 Apr. 1799). Otherwise refusal of weavers to make that sort of cloth for V.O.C.

**ID to ID, 3 Aug. 1803.**

Tan Jin Ning appointed Capitan Cina in Kedu in succession to Nie Longie (dismissed).

**ID to ID, 3 July 1803.**

10 piculs cotton thread for V.O.C. and 6 corges flag material for cruiser squadron while [they] ply pocket.

1801
Dj. Br. No. 48

VdB has to talk to HBII in person in serious Javanese. Has to take advantage of occasions such as presentation of strangers, money to talk to HBII.

HBII tells Nederburgh that [he] desires to ascend throne without approval of V.O.C.

+ 1st Res. to be forced to sit in Pagelaran on Mondays / Thursdays / Saturdays to receive orders from Nultan.

IJ to IJ.
6 Dec. 1801

Celebrations: appoint of Niberg on Garibig Puasa.

Postponement of 2 1/2 months — no one can gainsay him.

Refusal of HBII to allow DRII [Danureja II; transcribed as PRII] to get Act of appointment from V.O.C. such as had happened to DRI [Danureja I; transcribed as PRI].

> "Despite all his whims and caprices, HBII pleases me better than that hypocrite of a Natan."

HBII complains about V.O.C. more in public. Natan in private / secrecy.

"HBII hated by subjects = few closely attached to him." In event of hostilities.

HBII never discusses important affairs of state & DR [Danureja; transcribed as PRI] only allowed to deal [with] Nata / V.O.C. connections. In secret HBII concerns himself & everything (despite appearances).

"DRII [Danureja II; transcribed as PRII] is a very obliging and very genial person who has stood the test and on whose loyalty we can depend."

Before appointed Rg. Patih appeared a terrible bungler but has grown into his position and now equals Natayuda in skill. His lack of knowledge of people / naïveté should improve & years.

He is beginning to show some firmness in dealing [with] HBII although he stands his distance.

"The times are certainly miserable and our strength is weak" [;] have to adopt firm tone to HBII.

Nederburgh's explanation of weakness of V.O.C. to HBII only emboldens the Nultan.

1801. (1798) ? 1795
*   **ID to ID, 9 Dec. 1801:** Embassy to pay compliments to Niberg to depart in Jan. 1802.
*   **ID to ID, 25 Dec. 1801:** V. unhealthy season—many dying. ? Cholera.
*   **ID to ID, 14 Dec. 1802:** MB II [^p102-1] very like Mangkurat Mas [^p102-2], ascends throne without waiting for approval of Dutch. Presentation of living appointment of Niberg takes place. Phancing [^p102-3] of a Nagar-mill at Medono. Chinese in charge looses many goods. Warns Natayuda not to take V.O.C. side too openly at court. MB II to send ambassador to pay homage in Dulkangidah.
*   **ID to ID, 27 Feb. 1802:** MB II appoints Natayuda, Purwadipura, and Ng. Prawirasètra to accompany DR II to Semarang.
*   **ID to ID, 30 May 1802:** 2,500 corges of tobacco exported yearly from Kedu.
    *   Jan Lekko, Capitan Cina Kedu 1776–?
    *   Gan Ke Ko 1764–1776.
    *   Ni Liong Ti –1803.
    *   Qué Tjia Ning, Capitan Cina Yogya [^p102-4].
    *   Tan Jin Ning 1803–1813.
*   **Van den Berg to Eag., 29 Jun. 1802:** Tax Bandhar = 21,333.16 Tax. MB II knows Res. makes enormous profit over Bird’s Nest; prepared to raise tax.
*   **ID to ID, 1 Jul. 1802:** Secret conference of MB II & Wayakas. Doing MB II himself, administering domains (hitherto carried out):
    *   Nukawati Domain to R. Rongga.
    *   Mataram: Natadiningrat, lot Inner Regent.
    *   Bagelin: Mangundipura + co-op + JP in Natayuda, administering Tols/Birds Nests in Bagelin.
*   **Mantri Tampings (Heads of Frontier Desas):** to administer Tollgates, not Chinese. Profit for N.E. coast and Resident Yogya.
*   **Note:** MB II takes Tax Farm out of hands of Resident = great loss of Rev. for Raxanigara and disharmony Solo/Yogya + Resident. Much of salary from Tax Farm.

## TOLLGATE REGULATIONS

**Dj. Br. 48**

**Tollgate regulations 10 Apr. 1802 sent by van den Berg to Nijvenheim.**

*   Chinese journeying from Yogya - Semarang with cloth (kheedjes) 6 dubbeltjes per horse now 8.
*   Javanese --------------------------------- with kheedjes and garens 17 1/2 stuivers now 22 1/2 stuivers.
*   Chinese --------------------------------- with ginger (gember) 12 stuivers per horse now 16 stuivers.
*   Javanese --------------------------------- diverse goods, indigo, oil 18 3/4 stuivers now 22 1/2 stuivers.
*   Chinese from Semarang to Yogya carrying opium pays 4 rondematten (? - f.3.40 - 1 ronde real) now 6.
*   Javanese Ibid. [^p105-1]
*   Chinese or Javanese from Semarang to Kedu and Bagelen with opium Ibid.
*   Chinese from Pesantren in Kedu to Semarang with mats pays 8 dubbeltjes per horse now 12 ds.
*   Javanese from -------------------------- 40 stuivers per horse now 48 stuivers.
*   Jav. from here to Semarang taking kleedjes 10 stuivers each pikul [^p105-2] ? now 15 stuivers.
*   Jav. from here to Semarang carrying various goods in pikuls earlier 10 now 12 1/2 stuivers.
*   Chin. from Bagelen to Semarang carrying kleedjes pays 1 rondemat per 5 pikuls now 1 rondemat per 4 pikuls.
*   Jav. from Bagelen to Semarang taking clothes (? per pikul) pays 21 1/4 now 24 1/2 stuivers.
*   Jav. from Semarang to Yogya carrying ginger 18 3/4 per horse now 22 1/2.
*   Chin. from Semarang to Yogya bringing ginger 12 stuivers per horse now 16 (? lower Chin. rate because of monopoly).
*   Chin. or Jav. from Semarang to Yogya bringing ginger pays according to old rate per pikul.
*   Jav. from Payaman in Kedu and Pesantren to Yogya bringing rice pays 5 stuivers per horse now 6 stuivers.

Chin. or Jav. from Kebumen to Pekalongan or from Kebumen to Semarang via Kedu carrying kleedjes pay 31/4 stuivers more than old tax of 20 [notes: 40?] stuivers.

From Yogya to Sala carrying rice 1 stuiver per horse.

From Pepedan (onderd. Beji, Klaten) carrying kapas 1 st. per horse.

From Yogya to Solo carrying kleedjes either on horseback or per pikul [^p106-1] same tax.

Toll of gedhong tembakau from Yogya to Solo for each corge 3 ronde reals thus 6 ronde reals for Solo/ Yogya now 31/4 for Yogya.

Tax of gedhong [special tobacco warehouse] of Sibebek (? Karangkobar / Banjarnagara) and Batur [coolies/porters carrying up to 62 kg in panniers] 5 ronde reals per corge passing through Yogya/Solo now 6 ronde reals.

## TOLLGATE REGULATIONS

**Dj. Br. 48**

Tollgate regulations 10 Apr. 1802 sent by van den Berg to Nijvenheim.

- Chinese journeying from Yogya to Semarang with cloth (kleedjes): 6 dubbeltjes per horse now 8.
- Javanese with kleedjes and garens: 17½ stuivers now 22½ stuivers.
- Chinese with ginger (gember): 12 stuivers per horse now 16 stuivers.
- Javanese with diverse goods, indigo, oil: 18¾ stuivers now 22½ stuivers.
- Chinese from Semarang to Yogya carrying opium pays 4 rondematten [^p107-1] now 6.
- Javanese Ibid.
- Chinese or Javanese from Semarang to Kedu and Bagelen with opium Ibid.
- Chinese from Pesantren in Kedu to Semarang with mats pays 8 dubbeltjes per horse now 12 ds.
- Javanese from [blank] 40 stuivers per horse now 48 stuivers.
- Jav. from here to Semarang taking kleedjes 10 stuivers each pikul [^p107-2] now 15 stuivers.
- Jav. from here to Semarang carrying various goods in pikuls earlier 10 now 12½ stuivers.
- Chin. from Bagelen to Semarang carrying kleedjes pays 1 rondemat per 5 pikuls now 1 rondemat per 4 pikuls.
- Jav. from Bagelen to Semarang taking clothes (? per pikul) pays 21¼ now 24½ stuivers.
- Jav. from Semarang to Yogya carrying ginger 18¾ per horse now 22½.
- Chin. from Semarang to Yogya bringing ginger 12 stuivers per horse now 16 [^p107-3].
- Chin. or Jav. from Semarang to Yogya bringing ginger pays according to old rate per pikul.
- Jav. from Payaman in Kedu and Pesantren to Yogya bringing rice pays 5 stuivers per horse now 6 stuivers.

From Pepedan (onderd. Beji, Klaten) carrying kapas 1 st. per horse.

Tax of gedhong [special tobacco warehouse] of Sibebek (? Karangkobar / Banjarnegera) and Batur [coolies/porters carrying up to 62 kg in panniers] 5 ronde reals per corge passing through Yogya/Solo now 6 ronde reals.

Een jarran van P. van den Berg, met namene de 10. fl. is der Borg paard orde hard verkocht met 5. fl. min.—

Een jarran, andries na jarran, diverse goederen, braag ofte averende betaalt 10. fl. orde verkocht met 2½. fl. min.—

Een bined van de Bagelen, na Anna, met namene de 10. fl. orde verkocht met 2½. fl. min.—

Een jarran van de Bagelen, na fanna, mede nemene de 10. fl. orde betaalt aan paft 2½. fl. orde verkocht met 3½. fl. min.—

Een jarran van fanna, na Djojo, mede brengende 10. fl. orde betaalt aan paft per paard orde verkocht met 3½. fl. min.—

Een bined van fanna: na Djojo mede brengende 10. fl. orde betaalt aan paft 12. fl. per paard orde verkocht met 4. fl. min.—

Een ban de jarran van fanna: na Djojo, mede brengende 10. fl. orde betaalt aan paft na de orde gebruiker 10. fl. per paard.—
Compagnie van Parakan, Perak en Djorto zijnde bezwaarde met het betaal van 5. Hfl. aan pacht per maand voor deze stukken grond, bij bevinding van voornoemde 3% hfl. meer dan de oude tolheffing.

Het is mede vereiste bevinding dat voornoemde grond zijnde "Parakan" en "Djorto" tevens bezwaarde is met voornoemde tol, daar men het recht der voornoemde tolheffing bij voorrang of prioriteit heeft geplaatst bij het einde van het tabaksmulden, als is voor de afzonderlijk 5 hfl. voor 't jaar 1862, en de grond en bezitter voor deze 5 hfl. voor de departementale goederen en platten voor de 1/4 lange of wijze van hfl. 2 1/2, voor reden dat te verlangen tot 9 voornoemde van deze 5 hfl. 6.82 voor Parakan, Djorto.

Yogyakarta, den 10 April 1862.

*Yogyakarta Regulation, 10 Apr. 1862*
This document is an 18th or 19th-century hand-drawn "Figuratieve Schets" (Figurative Sketch) on lined paper, representing a map of a region in Indonesia, likely the Central Java area near Klaten, Yogyakarta, and Surakarta (Solo). The map shows roads, districts, and toll gates (*Tolpoort*), with significant rust staining from paper fasteners along the top margin.

#### Annotations at the Top
*   "t Kemlaka" (Handwritten in red ink)
*   "Tollgate Yogya" (Handwritten in red ink)
*   "Solo Br. no. 17." (Handwritten in pencil)
*   "Figuratieve Schets" (Typewritten/stamped title)

#### Archival Mark
There is a circular official stamp for the National Archives of Indonesia:
*   "ARSIP NASIONAL" (Curved along the top)
*   "REPUBLIK INDONESIA" (Curved along the bottom)
*   The center depicts the Garuda Pancasila emblem.

#### Map Text and Labels
The map contains various handwritten labels indicating geographical markers and infrastructure:
*   **Locations:** "Magelang", "Salatiga", "Klaten", "Solo", "Yogya" (labeled as "Yogya").

    *   "Singesa"
    *   "Oude Tolpoort te Kemlaka" (or Kemlaka)
    *   "Oude Tolpoort te melatos"
    *   "Debangue dersten"

The document features a circular stamp from the National Archives of the Republic of Indonesia ("Arsip Nasional Republik Indonesia"). At the top, there are handwritten annotations in red ink reading "Kemlaka" and "Tollgates Yogya."
