*Attack on Tegalreja by soldiers 8 Mar. 1802*

*BM. Add. Ms. 12341, Factor 3.*

[Marginalia: *Killing of Ratu Ageng's business, Factor 14 Jan 1802, Mertalaksana.*]

Pengĕt ingkang nĕrat naha ingkang nalam taklim K.R. Adip. Mangkupraja dhatĕnga ingkang putra K.R. Adip. Danurĕja. Napampuning kadya punika awijos anak Adipati utusan naha amawi nĕrat inggih nampun dhatĕng ing kula naha kula nampun tupiksani naraoping nĕrat nĕdaya mungĕl ing nĕrat anak Adipati nuka uninga dhatĕng ing kula wontin abdi dalĕm sudagaripun Kangjĕng Ratu Ageng ing Tegalrĕja awasta pun Mĕrtalaksana, nawig ing malim numungah tanggal ping nonga, wulan niyam wingking punika, (14 Jan 1802), pun Mĕrtalaksana dipun kĕcu ing titiyang, nagadhahipun tĕlas nĕdaya, sarta pun Mĕrtalaksana dipun pĕjahi. Nunten ingkang sinuhun Kangjĕng Sultan, dhawuhakĕn timbalan dipun dikakĕn angupados tiyang ingkang awon wau punika, naring kawalon kapanggih wĕstaning kĕcu, pun Amad Tana, kalih pun Surayana, katiga pun Nadongsa, nakawan pun Kĕtatruna, pagriyanipun nani dhusun ing Pakuacin tumut Tegalrĕja, naring pagriyanipun kaprikya aturipun kĕcu wau punika, wontin malih panunggilapun ingkang dinĕng kacĕpĕng awasta pun Nur Ali, kalih pun Numadinala, katiga Pak Pakim nakawan pun Kĕtapangarsa gangsal pun Nurayuda pagriyanipun nani siti dhusun ing Tapĕn, sakilĕnipun ing Bojong, naring kaupadon sampun nani kesah minggat, nunten pun Amad Tana, kalih pun Surayana nani kesah minggat saking pirantos angangĕt dados duka dalĕm, anak Adipati nampun anglampahakĕn utusan abdi dalĕm priyayi konca Mantri Nawi, libit kiwa tĕngĕn purapa dening abdi-dalĕm Lurah prajurit dĕrĕng kapanggih ing mangkĕ anak Adipati anglampahakĕn utusan malih abdi-dalĕm priyayi konca Mantri Widana Prajurit Raden Panji Nunĕngrana priyayi konca kaliwon Raden Ngabĕhi Mangunjaya, priyayi konca Lurah Mantri libit Mas Ngabĕhi Jayapuspita, nakancanipun lampahipun angupados dhatĕng tanah paos, nawĕtan ridi Mĕrapi nauratipun kalih abdi-dalĕm kaliwon malih Mas...

---

Ngabehi Sindujaya, konca lurah mantri lebet Mas Ngabehi Rohadipura, lampahipun dhateng tanah Kedu, sakilen redi Merapi, nawratipun, panedhanipun anak Adipati dhateng ing kula, menggah lampahipun karsanipun anak Adipati, ingkang namung kepareng dipun tiyang ingkang awon namung kesah, minggat wau punika. Punapa dening tiyangipun ingkang awon ingkang namung kesah minggat wau punika, sampun kapanggih numering wonten niti dhusun bawah ing Surakarta, utawi nalibiting negari panedhanipun anak Adipati dhateng ing kula dipun lulupun kados ingkang nampun kalampahan, ingkang punika anak Adipati kula inggih anglulupaken kados ingkang nampun kalampahan, naha utusanipun anak Adipati namung kula kinen angupados tiyang awon wau punika, negari mewah ing dhusun napunika dereng kapanggih.

#### Report on the Journey of N. Engelhard

*   **p. 118:** Sunan sick—sends Crown Prince in his place to meet Engelhard at Gramat.
    Report on the journey of N. Engelhard to the Vorstenlanden Surakarta and Yogyakarta, to the Oosthoek, and the Residencies of Japara, Rembang, and Joana. Signed: N. Engelhard, 27 Mei, Semarang. [1749–1788]

*   **p. 119:** Remember journey of Greeve. Sunan goes sick and eventually dies. Sunan Swaga PB III [1749–1788].
    Private meeting with Sunan in apartment; also present: Barend Jan van Nieuwkerken [named Nijvenheim]. 20 July 1802: News of general peace in Europe: Hopefully this will be lasting. (Peace Amiens).

*   **p. 120:** (Finished 1804).
    Erecting a fort at Klaten (Klaten) between Solo/Yogya. Sunan/Sultan prepared to supply necessary stone and wood and lime and other materials.

*   **p. 121:** Construction of fort would go forward quickly. V.O.C. must send an adept engineer there to supervise the work.

## 1802

*   **1 Mar. 1802:** Van den Berg to Engelhard.
*   **25 Feb. 1802:** DRII Regents to Semarang to greet incoming G.G. N. Berg.
*   **17 Mar. 1802:** ID to ID. DRII Natayuda/ Purwadipura to Semarang.
*   **Mar. 1802:** Orders PB II to Natadiningrat conveyed by Panjis: Amal Jono / Nurgaya and to be executed for attack on Ratu Ageng's kraton. [^p122-1]
*   **Mar. 1802:** To Truna Wangsatruna. [^p122-2]

---

#### [Execution Details]
Clothed in yellow trailing *dodot*, white trousers, belt, body smeared, yellow *borch*, *sumping* behind ears, long krowon, and white hats (rejes) with bobble on top. Kris with gold *pendok* at side, *tungkak niwi* hilt only to be carried by Sultan. Precipitation on 2 horses and red *payongs*, led from prison westwards across *Alun-Alun* as *gamelan* *Ladrang Panganten* plays. In middle of *Alun-Alun*, *Gamelan* stop, krises taken, *payong* shut. Bikel to stab coat, kris (not a kraton pusaka). Bodies to be cut into pieces, heads and *sumping* to be placed on stakes at cross-roads. Tatruna: hand and foot to be cut off. After execution, money to be distributed to people from 2 *panggungs*, under 2 *panggungs* placed K/W of Waringin Kurung. [^p122-3]

---

*   **14 Apr. 1802:** ID to ID. Feasts of Binar celebrated. Roads in Yogya in a terrible state.
*   **9 May 1802:** ID to ID. Pending Eng. and wife horses from Kedu. ? Sultan's stud farm.
*   **24 Apr. 1802:** ID to ID. PB IV gives shelter to those implicated in attack/murder on factor of Ratu Ageng. PB II "paras ati" to PB IV.
*   **26 May 1802:** ID to ID. Vd Berg just returned from Jonno pepper gardens. Harvest this year would not be so large, as 1801 1000 pikuls.

---

Heavy rains continuing into May destroy pepper trees.

---

**10 Jan 1802:** Heavy earthquake in Yogya, 28 Dec 1801, 5 shocks. Some walls along streets damaged. Pyramid built by MB II at a Pinanggrahan. [^p123-1]

**Piagim van Ijsveldijk, 2 May 1792:**
Capitan Cina Kedu = *Pan lick* to *just died*. Taken over by Non: Gang tak Nung. Capitan Cina of Yogya, Que Tjin Ning, appoints Ni Liongue in Kedu.

2 x p.a. 7,708 Ronde Reals for tax-farm in Kedu. Take to Res. in Yogya 6 chests at Phasa [Mulu].

**Mangkupradja to PR II, 30 May 1802:** At Delanggu, journeying to Yogya. Rongga halts outside Pinanggrahan. Rongga orders dogs to warn of the goat. Jingadipa (wakil Dimang) attacked when he tries to demonstrate the kirtasari. Killed a pike; 7 wounds in body. Houses plundered at Rongga. [^p123-2]

**Res. Nygverheim needs gugat aluwatis to Yogya. Boodelag. (Legal complaint).** 
**17 Jun 1802:** R. Rongga, young man about 23. *Die zyn hart vry hoog draagd.* [^p123-3]

Sumadiningrat making inquiry at Delanggu on orders of MB II. Tries to put different cast on affair. Starts because Rongga takes little goat for his daughter. When Javanese remonstrate, Rongga attacks them and kills Ki Kartasari. 

Rongga is a lawless, proud person, addicted to drink, a 'kwaaije jongen'. Planned for a long time to put bad blood between Yogya/Solo because of problems between kingdoms. MB II would not allow Rongga to be punished by war/religious law for attack on Delanggu.

---

## 1802

*   Van den Berg to Engelhard, 18 Nov. 1802: MB II ill—better, according to CP, but loss of appetite. PB IV suffering from rheumatic pains.
*   JD to JD, 20 July 1802: Ney (cousin) of PR II to Aunt of PR II + wife, Pati of Kendhal, Cakrasimita. [^p124-1]
*   MB II to Eng., 26 June 1802; 25 Muh. 1729: N. Eng. to Solo 5 Aug; 3 Aug to Yogya. [^p124-2]
*   VdB to Eng., 24 Jul. 1802: Eng. plans to visit N. Coast and Temple of Prambanan which VdB has cleared of wild grasses. To spend a day at Tangkiran.
*   JD to JD, 10 Aug. 1802: Road Pelanggu—Tangkiran not passable for a carriage. For 10 years MB II makes no repairs on roads. It is back-breaking work to cross the road in a carriage; bridges only palm tree trunks. Better to go in palanquin. Phaeton to meet ladies at Tangkiran to take them to Prambanan. Tangkiran-Yogya: have to cross deep valley of Papringan. Road in same terrible state. [^p124-3]
*   JD to JD, 17 Dec. 1802: Murder of Eur. gardener at Nela by bandits. 2 cousins of P. Mongkudiningrat involved in murder of MB II Mantri at Pasampakkan. MB II wants to see them executed and heads on bamboo poles. Only Naltar able to do that.
*   JD to JD, 18 Dec. 1802: Requests V.O.C. to cut timber in Jipang. But MB II going from one pasanggrahan to another—difficult to talk to him. [^p124-4]

## 1802

van den Berg [to] Engelhard, 4 Apr. 1802 – 5 Nov. 1802. *Memorie overgegeven aan P. Eng.*

HB II [Hamengkubuwana II] in affair of Jaxa Term trying to put Resident in his place = making HB II's glory appear all the greater. HB II no wish to follow in steps of MB I.

During time of van IJ [van IJsseldijk] = HB II also makes changes in Admin. Jaxa Term. HB II would appear to Eng. as being more friendly and engaging than the Sunan, but he is not so civilized (urbane) and well bred.

*New Battlements & Batteries of guns.* 

Patih & Nayakas complain about HB II in secret to vdB [van den Berg]. HB II's aims:

1. To get CP [Crown Prince] appointed Naltan on his death [illegible] without permission of V.O.C.
2. Refusal to accept Wedburgh as being more [illegible] to himself.
3. Refusal to allow subjects implicated in affairs + V.O.C. subjects to be judged in Semarang according to govt. law.
4. Refusal to allow PR II [Pakubuwana II/the Crown Prince] to take oath of allegiance to V.O.C. on appointment. *Still not done.*
5. When krisos/swords (Pusakas) being carried in state about Yogya = V.O.C. officials should get down of horses & take off hats or ride behind walls of streets!
6. V.O.C. officials to get out of way when carriages of HB II going to Penanggrahan.

HB II shown lack of respect for V.O.C. in many ways. But no woe falling out [with] HB II as van IJ had done = vdB being always on cordial terms [with] HB II.

Van Overstraten counsels HB II to order HB II's concubines & one of most beloved wives to leave kraton because HB II gratifying his lust with them = & taking them out to Penanggrahan, etc. [He] also tells HB II to stop stealing wives of subjects for *Amazon corps*. One man who had refused to part [with] wife had been killed by being thrown down a well.

[He] should leave Pasisir alone = sends parties out to capture Chinese women.

---

Ova P.Ng. Falls out + van IJ—although HBI on deathbed had told Nor to live as a brother + resident and listen to his advice. Extremely avaricious + stingy = if orders a Tandhu + lurah brings it slightly late = would be fined 1 Rupiah.

Mangkudipura advises HB II to raise Tax-Farm: in 33 year Tax-Farm raised 16–50.000 Ronde Reals. HB II is now suspicious that one half his body [^p127-1] all he strives for is to advance glory at cost of others.

*   **ID to ID. 13 Jul. 1802:** Decay Re-organizing Tax-farm abandoned by HB II.
*   DR II to co-operate + Mangkupraja in dealing + Rongga over Munda perpetrated at Pilanggu.
*   HB II carried in Tandhu hawak to Dhampar.
*   **Jan Jean Kong** = Capitan Cina Nemerang.
*   Dynasty of Tradus in Yogya area = no protection in law against Seizures of HB II. 50 pikuls of cotton thread from E. MN each year.

*   **ID to ID. 22 July 1802:** Journey of Eng. to courts in Yogya.
*   **ID to ID. 3 Aug. 1802:** Rongga to be asked to make preparations for supplying V.O.C. + Rice from Grobogan.

*   **ID to ID. 29 Sept. 1802:** Rongga to let Tax-Farm of Grobogan for 1500 Ronde matin.
*   550 coyangs of rice from Grobogan. More expected from Jipang.
*   HB II 12 leagues + various bands of robbers in Yogya.
*   HB II wants to see Indigo Factory in Loano abolished. Only 3–4 pikuls p.a. Ground to be used to extend pepper cultivation.

*   **ID to ID. 22 Nov. 1802:** Robber bands quiet during west monsoon, active again in E. monsoon.
*   HB II refusal to take measures v. Rongga in Solo affair.
*   Ni Hiongü = 10 nets as Capitan Cina in Kedu; gives complete satisfaction.

*   **ID to ID. 30 Nov. 1802:** Natakusuma = needs a kris in a gold box + letter to Engelhard.

---

## 1802-3

**van den Berg to Engelhard, 30 Nov. 1802**

NK [Natakusuma] = exceptionally courteous character & great talents makes him a great Javanese character. Much like his father HB I, he is considered to be still greater. It is dangerous for HB II under whose bad government he is considered to be still greater. If troubles occur in Yogya:

*   NK would rot nit ntill [sic]
*   Stuff towards Europeans = should be encouraged in secret by Dutch to ensure support from him.
*   Eng. should reply to present a dagger onward.

---

**ID to ID. 28 Dec. 1802**

*   Regent in Grobogan captures 6 robbers amongst them a Chinese *peranakan*. [^p128-1]
*   Bp. of Pemak = v. close to bandits often crossing over Banda. [^p128-2]
*   3 points of difference Solo/Yogya =
    *   Rongga attack on Dilanggal [regarding] MB II's marti in Semar by a Javanese.
    *   Lobang/Parembon - Bonder.
*   Nijvenheim to be dismissed ultimo Aug. 1803, if embezzling V.O.C. revenues.
*   Wounds of 1790-91 between Yogya/Solo beginning to heal over.
*   MB II away from Yogya at Dananggrahan. None can ask him for permission to cut wood in Jipang.

---

**ID to ID. 29 Jan. 1803**

*   Feast of Puasa celebrated 28 Jan. 1803.
*   Before leaving MB II in kraton ask about possibility of felling timber in Jipang.
*   MB II planning to livemase [sic] = Ratu Anom. [^p128-3]
*   D. of Ratu Kencana Wulan.

---

**ID to ID. 1 Mar. 1803**

*   MB II non the Crown Prince is a charming person.

---

**ID to ID. 18 Mar. 1803**

*   R.T. Cakrawinata - of Solo. From same Kiden family as Natayuda.
*   Solo CW brought up & Natayuda by NY's father in Kartasura.

---

**ID to ID. 23 Mar. 1803**

*   PB IV making difficulties about van den Berg being transferred as bt Res. from Yogya to Solo.
*   Nijvenheim speaks ill of him.
*   N. much too dependant on PB IV speaking ill of old V.O.C. officers.

---

**Id. to Id. 24 Apr. 1803**
Pepper plantations in Loano = land still owned by MS II. 100 cacahs given for upkeep of common people. Regent pays 240 R.R. tax. Picul of pepper = 20 stuivers.

**Id. to Id. 17 Apr. 1803**
Ni Lonzi tax of Gedhong Tembakan in Kedu. 8000–8500 p.a. + bea 10,000. To keep tax on old footing. Messenger of MB II/Ratu to Demak + money to R.T. Jayengrana. Repair mosque.

**Id. to Id. 23 May 1803**
R.T. Cakrawinata of Solo killed toakris 5 houses from Solo at Nanarewu = for intrigues at Van Nijvenheim. R. Ayu CW also killed. [^p129-1]

When PB IV follows Van Reede, people say Salatiga to take leave here in 1796? Reede promises that if he became G.G., PB IV would get back a part of Mataram = Cakrawinata knew too much about this. Nijvenheim: too close to CW for comfort. PB IV tries to poison him? Goes to him + Jamu prepared by Ratu for illness. N. sick tries European medicines. N. refuses.

**Id. to Id. 25 May 1803**
Cakrawinata: ? strangled + cotton thread. Garrotted.

**Id. to Id. 31 May 1803**
CW strangled + thread = Next two white thoris. 2 priests to pray for him. 2 strong threats. PB IV on night of 8 May to N'N residence = gives him a cup of Jamu + some ointment. Both refused by Nijvenheim - on point of departing for Holland.

PB IV cuts off all relations + N = Refuses to take daily food from Residency kitchen. 

1. Cakrawinata carrying on love affair with sister of PB IV.
2. Nijvenheim CW follow PB IV to Salatiga know of conference at origin of Ruseun v. PB IV on designs on Mataram.
3. CW to mosque at Demak. Tries to interest V.O.C. in P. Prangwedana as possible replacement at Danar at death of PB IV. PW married to sister of Cakrawinata.

## *De Jong Opkomst* (pp 118-234)
### *Engelhard's Journey 1802*

1802:
*   **Sickness of Emperor:** Makes it difficult for the Emperor.
*   **Breleng Greet of Mangkunegara:** [? Klaten]
*   **Visit to Surakarta:**
    *   **Conference with Sultan:** Attended by Van den Berg, first Resident. Crown Prince = Adipati Anang, Adipati Adinegara.
    *   Really to get assurance there is a case of attack by English.
    *   Also regarding appointments in his court and general.

*   **Complaints about Danuningrat:**
    *   English on eye being kept on Panarakan line.
    *   Recently out in district, [?] = [?] [?] [?] [?]

*   **Complaints about delays:**
    *   Resident had to wait at least 3-4 months before being able to see the Sultan.
    *   Insistence that all affairs should be handled through right or court and ensure speedy execution.

*   **Danureja takes oath to Company:**
    *   Agrees to accept hands as adohan.

*   **Planting of pepper and indigo:**
    *   Insistence regarding bridges and roads in good repair.
    *   Annoyance at the fact that road at Boyolali was not kept up, lack of materials provided by Sultan.

*   **Importance of Jodemo Danuningrat:**
    *   All affairs handled through him. [^p130-1]

*   **Sunan embraces Engelhard:**
    *   Moved him [?] [?] [?] [?]
    *   Bad relations would be forgotten.
    *   But despite acceptance, [?] [?]: Sultan and Engelhard realized that Sunan [?] [?] [?].

*   **Crown Prince:**
    *   Very different character from Sultan. [^p130-2]
    *   Much easier in conversation; in endurance and room, expression.
    *   Much more friendly and much more talkative than his father.
    *   Time will show when he comes to the throne whether he will be like his father or grandfather.

*   **Adja as businessmen concerned:**
    *   [?] that they would not [?] by him in trouble.
    *   Although certain amount of respect.***

**Right-hand Margin Notes:**
*   *Verslag der reis van N. Engelhard naar de Konen van Yoerakarta en Surakarta naar den oosthoek en de residenten Japara, Rembang, Joana.*
*   *1. De Jong Opkomst van het Nederlandsch Gezag in Oost-Indie.*

of E.I.C.O.S. (East India Company) acting at Solo:
    *   Crown Prince got in the way.

**Importance of friendly relations with Java.**

Feeling van Overstraten was:
*   More to be feared from Solo than from Yogya.

**River back to 1790:**
The best one can say with certainty is that this comes down to this day does not forego the hope, that the empire Mataram will be once again reunited and that the empire will triumph even in Java.

**Marriage between regions/families:**
*   Between Java Company and Princely Princesses of Yogyakarta:
    *   If hostilities break out between Company and Princes, loyalties would be hopelessly divided.

**Treatment of wives/regents from Northern coast:**

## Solo 1803-1807 / Yogya 1803-05

*   **16 Aug 1803:** *Vanden Bus* -> *Watuloo*. Death of P. Natakusuma, son-in-law of P.W.P. R.M. Gandakusuma appointed P. Adiwijaya.
*   **23 Oct 1803:** Id. to Id. Death of Condokusumo P.B. IV [^p133-1].
*   **9 Nov 1803:** Id. to Id. Complot amongst Chin. in Banyumas [^p133-2] (Nokaraja). Chin. fugitives from Cirebon/Tegal. 50 men strong. Cut their fingers, let blood pour into pot and join. Drink it.
*   **15 Nov 1803:** Ratu Ageng illness - brought to Kadipaten where she died.
*   **Undated:** Appointed as Panewu Natalaya, Nantakusuma, Nilarong. Appointed new Bp. Panolan R.T. Natawijaya [transcribed as R.J.].
*   **28 Jan 1805:** Id. to Id. Death of Natayuda. PB IV [^p133-3] raises R.T. Nurbekti (Nong NK) as R.T. Natadiningrat to take place of deceased R.T. Natayuda. + name R.T. Natayuda II to Nong NY + 250 cacahs.

#### 1803

**JD to JD**
**31 May 1803**
PB IV supposed to have been promised part of Mataram on death of HB II.
N. designs on position of Gov. of Java's N.E. coast; perigratis Eng. to PB IV.
PB IV sees N as having seduced CW = PB IV quarrel + N Nyverheim.

**JD to JD**
**26 Aug. 1803**
**Solo.**
VdB to take leave of HB II.
Arrives 18 Aug. in Solo.
Crown Prince still at odds with P. Mangkubumi.
P. Mangkubumi has "a charming heart" [^p135-1] to be pitied.
Tries his best to clear up appoint CP.
But CP is a *wraakzuchtig en hart draagende mannetje* [^p135-2] without any intelligence.
MB comes up to CP in presence of VdB asking him to accept him again, calls him "Gusti," tears in his eyes, friendship.
But CP only toasts VdB and turns his back on his uncle.
On prompting of VdB = MB and CP toast each other.

MB v. downhearted = "I must confess I fear for unpleasant consequences at some time of this *onenigheid* [^p135-3], which seems to be unamendable and bodes ill for the injudicious Crown Prince." *aller nadeligst most disadvantageous.*

PB IV to go fishing at Boyolali for 3 days.
PWP has interview and VdB tells him of complaints.
Nyverheim tells him to make him pledge loyalty again to PB IV but this only annoys him.

**JD to JD**
**6 Sept. 1803**
**Solo.**
Negotiations for marriage of Yogya princess and Solo Crown Prince (Ratu Anom).
PB IV constantly asking about age of Yogya princess.
One of concubines of CP pregnant = "The young Prince *wat veel naar de vrouwen begint te zien*." [^p135-4]
Disproves famous rumor of impotence.
30,000 men under arms guide PB IV to Pengging.
Pajang / Mataram / Nakawati good, no less but coolies from Bagelen.

---

Refusal of [the Company] to allow Mangkubumi to visit PB IV in [his] bedroom at Pengging. MB’s fault that disharmony occurs + [the Company] because [he is] too often in harem of PB IV — [the Company] annoyed about it. Difficult to patch up quarrel.

P. Prangwēdana = asking back 27,000 R. maaten in gold jewels from van Reede. d'Abo insinuates himself into entourage of V.D.B. Would he be kept on in Java to [handle] Kararwar + copying. Eurasian = "He has a quick intelligence."

---

**ID. to ID. 11 Jan. 1802** [^p136-1]
MB II fines Patih + some Regents 2000 R. maaten because [they were] so much enjoy New Year's Party in Residency that [they] cannot attend Nedikah of Ratu Bendara in kraton on 1 Jan. 1802 [died 30 Dec. 1801]. It is high time the Company took away [from] his money — he needs to be made afraid of V.O.C.

**ID. to ID. 3 Feb. 1802** [^p136-2]
Smuggling of opium in Java. Can buy it at 450–500 a chest in Yogya when in Batavia [it] costs 700. 40 chests imported p.a. into Yogya. Chinese Head Bandar goes bankrupt = paying too high a cut per chest to Gov. Gen. 250 R.R. Capitan Cina Yogya [kēduh?] [ne?] 4000 R.R. worth of opium p.a.

**ID. to ID. 14 Feb. 1802** [^p136-3]
Sugar mill at Medono plundered. Capitan Cina loses 4,000 R.R. [?] stolen by Panji Padmawijaya, later finds its way into coffers of HB II.

**ID. to ID. 24 Feb. 1802** [^p136-4]
Panji [Nying?]gengraza / Kuwon Nindureja. Near to foothills of Mt. Merapi to recapture bandit who had plundered / killed Ratu Ageng's trader to Semarang. 3 bandits had been imprisoned by HB II in 40' deep well on an island in Taman Sari. But escape over 3 v. high walls.

## Notes on Archives 1803–1808 (Dj. Brieven no. 38, naar Surakarta)

*   **11 Mar 1803:** Van den Berg to Engelhard. Letter of Eng. asking to be able to cut teak in Nultan forests. Allows it under certain conditions.
*   **Exp. v. Robben in MN:** Expedition v. Robben in MN—brings back many ears of dead bandits and many prisoners.
*   **7 Mar 1803 (PBT to van D.B.):** Asks for permission to cut teak in Jipang and areas bordering Pasisir from Rembang to Nidayu. Wood only to be taken by HB II's inhabitants in Pasisir. No Chinese to be allowed into the regencies and no Pasisir inhabitants without permission V.O.C.
*   **31 Jan 1804 (Waterloo to Engelhard):** Questioning waste lands of Nultan/Nultan: any interest for V.O.C.? Not included in cacao of rulers. Always plans to survey, but quashed by HB II in conference. Van Overstraten in 1792 (3 Apr 1792).
    *   1755: PBT (85,450 cacaos), MBI (87,050). 
    *   1774: New registers (over 100,000 each). 
    *   V.O.C. want to survey waste lands; HB II does not allow it. Fears that 1773 register would be taken as a basis—all rest would belong to V.O.C.
*   **Behind Lamongan, Joana:** Especially in Grobogan, new regencies laid out. Difficulties over desas PBT—MBI, Silang, Pakidulan, Pasumpakan, Delanggu and Bp. Wira. E. MN (R. Rongga).
*   **5 Mar 1804 (IJ to IJ):** 80–90 pirates on N. coast of Java at Prigi. Mandharese ship from Bali Buleleng.
*   **1 Apr 1804 (IJ to IJ):** Murder king of Banten.
*   **19 Apr 1805 (IJ to IJ):** In Pacitan. Nigarawedi in Solo area = 32 ships anchor = 400 men came ashore—from Bali/Bugis. R.T. Purradipurat 15 Mantris from Sidrong Tengen would leave for Pacitan to investigate.
*   **Netradipura to Mast. Netrawijaya at Yogya:** 32 ships—17 stay anchored out at sea, rest taken ashore. 400 men—lettie(?) give presents of ginger, borek, benzoin. Local net chickens ½ Rp. each. 1 real—bakul of rice for a gun. Tompo rice for 3 brakes of ginger.
- 3 Buffaloes exchanged for 200 Reals worth of Benzoin, Ginger, Iron, etc.
- 9 days long: Watch cock-fights in evening. Tandhak dances (Balinese).
- Locals refuse to tell them about roads into interior.

---

**19 Apr. 1805**
H.B.I. needs 2 3lb cannon to Pacitan + 24 balls to guard harbour.

**26 Apr. 1805**
News from Solo that various pirates from Negarawidi penetrate as far as Solo + on 29 Besar 1729 [^p138-1] to battle + reg. of Pasuruan/Bangil Malang in Malang Desa Gondang legi. Path of Malang wounded.

**8 May 1805**
Reksamerta Cirebon Javanese captured for 2 months by pirates, escapes at Pulo Tempo.
- Reksamerta captured + 20 others (men, women, children).
- By squadron of 19 pirate ships. Captured on N. coast.
- Each ship crew of 20 + 2 6pord. cannon + muskets/blunderbusses.
- Learns that pirates from Timor (speak Malay).
- Names of pirates named Raja Datuk Namak, Raja Unus.
- At Cilacap capture more inhabitants + 2 cannon.
- Escapes by swimming to shore at Negarawidi.

**R.T. Purwadipura to R.T. Natadiningrat (n.d.)**
- Dimengs of Cilacap: Nagayuda, Mastarana, Suraminggala, Kartajaya—2 beheaded by pirates.
- Pamerg Nagakarya + Solo Region of Pacitan.
- Raja Datuk Namak, Raja Unus.
- Patih Pul Rawu + Chinese Encik Putih.
- Pirates from Timor / Bugis: 35 ships each + 50-60 people. 4 large, 20 small cannon.
- At Desa Prigi, nayub dance at night, watch lock-fights by day.

## YOGYA 1803 (July)

**26 July**
Wateloo & Engelhard:
* Presents for Sultan HB II on taking office (July 1803): carriage, elephant, Persian horse. 
* Van den Berg would add a Bimanese horse. 
* Leaves for Semarang. 
* No Javanese allowed into stables of the Sultan; Patih not allowed to own Bimanese horses.

**3 Aug.**
Wateloo & Engelhard:
* HB II appoints 2nd Bupati in Grobogan area (Sasrakusuma) to subordinate to R. Rongga. 
* All bupatis in Yogya.

**7 Aug.**
Wateloo & Engelhard:
* Lacks a translator in Yogya. 
* DR II comes to discuss affairs of state.

**17 Aug.**
Wateloo & Engelhard:
* HB II comes out of Kraton to watch tournament. 
* Complaint of costliness of parties for Javanese at Jest days. 
* Van den Berg 50x busier than Wateloo. 
* [Morbon] Kraton not yet finished. Van den Berg goes to check from Jalaj.
* "Het afstejd dat your Excellency had with CP HB II now resolved in a friendly fashion" (Negotiation over land).
* Hopes that Engelhard's bolt to Jara will go well. 
* Carpenters arrive for work on commandant buildings. Wateloo discussion / Regent / Patih.
12 Aug 1803 Waterloo-Engelhard: R. Rongga Prawiradirja [^p144-1] to help VOC. Ways payment of taxes, 6 months in arrears. [^p144-2] Waterloo-upbraids him; says he has not done well 1,300 Rood molten silver money.

20 Aug 1803 Waterloo-Engelhard: Waterloo + 2nd Res Domis: Visit to HB II [^p144-3] / Ratu. CP/Ratu.

4 Sept 1803 Waterloo-Engelhard: Extreme/gross lack of economy. "Verrepaarde inzaagligheid" of the Sultan in money matters. So unable to do anything in money matters to help him. No further news about proposed marriage between Yogya-Solo.

8 Sept 1803 Waterloo-Engelhard: 3 minutes before 10 o'clock (7 Sept 1803). Very severe earthquake. According to old people in Yogya had never felt such a strong tremor before. Much damage in Kraton Taman Sari: Ngawatreja new building put up by the Sultan's estate (Sultan damaged). Chinese temple completely damaged, all water drained from pools. N.W. bulwark & fort damaged. In 17 days 4 serious earthquakes.

13 Sept 1803 Waterloo-Engelhard: Quarrel Rongga-HB II. [^p144-4] Quarrel between R. Rongga & HB II because Rongga seizes some land which had been given in perpetuity to a court favorite. Rongga's wife summoned back to the Kraton (Ratu Maduretna). Mother-in-law & Rongga excluded from affair. Fear he would go after other women. Later convoked Rongga & Ratu summoned to HB II, which was a good sign.

Yogya Letters A.N. No. 49

1803 (Sept) (Yogya)

**15 Apr 1803**
**Waerloo-Engelhard**
*   Waerloo to meet HBII (Hamengkubuwana II) and European horses at Sultan's country pavilion at Purwareja.
*   HBII extremely taken with English horses.
*   Mandering Ong and Bimanete Horse - Nidayu jail.

**22 Sept 1803**
**Waerloo-Engelhard**
*   Payment to Raden Rongga of 1,366 Rixdollars for work.
*   Rice trade in Grobogan should pick up, but new 2nd Regent of Grobogan appointed by HBII (Danukusuma) still in Yogya.
*   Question of marriage of HBII's daughter R.A. Dlairah; rumors that she would be married to R.G. Natadiningrat.
*   Merapi erupting/also eruptions noted on Cumbing/Sundara (1803).
*   Raden Rongga many enemies at Court, especially biased against him.

**28 Sept 1803**
**Waerloo-Engelhard**
*   Trouwfeest (Wedding party) / Birthday feast for Sultan's children and royalty.
*   Dania confirmation from Patih Danureja; Natadiningrat to marry R.A. Dji Supirah.
*   Though P.N.K (Pangéran Natakusuma) more circumspect, HBII fears that N.K. would get even more followers by such a marriage.
*   R. Ayu Triwulan (Rencaka Wulan) very much in favor of this marriage.

---

**30 Sept 1803**
*Warloo, Engelhard*
Meeting at R.T. Oadrakusuma about need for rice from J. Grobogan to come onto the market to feed VOC troops in Pasisir.

**26 Sept 1803**
*Van den Berg, Warloo*
High time HBH [High Government] opposed as regards new measurements instituted for land. News that takes 100 cacahs[1] away from a desa near Vala (called Luyuk). All inhabitants due to Vala area and submit to P. Mangkupraja.

**2 Dec 1798**
*Letter Baron Van Reede tot de Parkeler, MBH[2]*
Receives letter G.B.H. 14 Jumadilakhir 1725. Marriage plans G.B.H:
*   R. Rongga P.D. III → R. Aj. Murniah (orphan song P.D. II)
*   R. T. Natadiwiria (MN Regent) → R. Aj. Murdiah (Raden Yayid)
*   R. G. Prawirayuda (MN Regent) → R. Aj. Warima

Marriages to take place: 18 Jumadilakhir year Jimawal.

**6 Oct 1803**
*Transcribed letter, Dril-a Adipati Vala Mangkupraja*
News of R. Rongga that a European from Pasisir named Gabriel Eenst comes to Jipang/Panulan to buy teak wood (in area controlled by R. Natawijaya). Takes a house in Desa Gelempang; but robbed, killed with Chinese assistant. Decision to send commission from Yogya to investigate and apprehend R. Rongga.

## 1803 (Oct) (Yogya)

*   **A.N. Yogya Letters, A.N. No. 49, 11 Oct 1803 (Waterloo & Engelhard):** Ratu Ageng (Tegalreja) improving somewhat in health. Raden Rongga still in bad favor at court [^p147-1]; attempts to stay long away from court, but manner not one to endear him to [the] *regents* [^p147-2] enough to deal [with] it.

*   **17 Oct 1803 (Waterloo & Engelhard):** Ratu Ageng dies at 7 o'clock in the morning; affairs for Raden Rongga [^p147-1] going better - fears would be pardoned, [the] Resident or [the] Government goes surely for his good behavior in the future.

*   **(Djakarta):** Rongga receives pardon through intermediacy of brothers-in-law [Natadiningrat, Orit] [^p147-3] and Ratu allowed to go back.

*   **18 Oct 1803 (Waterloo & Engelhard):** R.T. Sumadiningrat [transcribed as R.J. Jumadiningrat] says Ratu Ageng had long drinking spell but not yet dead, in fact dies at 3 o'clock in the afternoon; Waterloo visits Kraton to condole [with] it.

*   **25 Oct 1803 (Waterloo & Engelhard):** (Of) Burial of Ratu Ageng at Tegalreja, lying in state in Kadipaten (Dalem) [^p147-4] and all family present. All Sultans, family would go to Imagiri, [the] Court would follow corpse to rear Passebaan (V. Alun2) [^p147-5]. 5 o'clock return to Kadipaten met by 2 old wives [unmarried to rear]; but refuses loss of prestige, wishes to enter from the front.

Ratu Asengs [^p148-1] based at Pnagiri, 4 hours distant from Yogya. NB: Reman at home, a both feel very distressed at how Ratu Ageng [^p148-2] allowed follow corpe to Pnagiri.

Apparently on Julta death bed had said:

Seems makes deep impression on MB II [^p148-4].

**31 Oct 1803**
**Waterloo Engelhard**
R.T. Sumadiningrat [transcribed as R.J. Jumadiningrat], stupid antagonist [^p148-5] - (Drit + outer regents) causes difficulty in clearing up affair, murdered European Gabriel in Grobogan.

30 NOV 1803
WAT. ENG

Vendu: Rim letters to Patihs & Palayogya to clear up remaining affairs between [them].

> "Vara Different Inhabitants from Here (Yogya) The Sultan is a dissembler in his heart, who will never be on good terms with Van den Berg, unless I take him into his confidence."

> "Van den Berg must truly in Sala must practice more politics and hypocrisy - whenever he talks with the Patih on outstanding affairs."

> "Natural hate from the adequate for this court" (Yogya)

> "Men zeggen van de Keizer goed opgehoord, zo a dat de [ken?] in de zaken gewerkt is, zo moet ik mij volkomen vertrouwen op de compagnie stellen, dat zij nimmer zal gedoogen dat mijn hof voor dat van Yogya zal vernederd worden, want ik ben vorst genoeg over geschillen tusschen Meer en Yogya met het zegt niet inlaat, en dat alleen toevertrouwd aan mijn Rijks bestuurd dus het best daarover met de van wege de VOC aan mijn kraton heeft gemaakt beraadslag heeft." [^p152-1]

Story told by trustworthy Bupati [to] Sindunagara. P. Mangkubumi (Vara) in league & [cannot?] in Banyumas. News sent to V.d. Berg. Despite hatred & [cannot?] after [Kanjasa?], P. Mangkubumi [is] so bastardized [that he is] "A prince that would be capable of anything."

Spy sent to Banyumas - plot only consists [of] 27 persons. Only Sultan a brotherhood [Kongsi]. (Peravakan)

A.N. Yogya No. 45
30 Nov 1803
Wat-Eng

1803 (Nov)
Yogya

Java's security appears to be in a crisis. Would peace last? Every day, every [one] from outside [re: peranakans too] [much] falling during s.s.g. van Overstraten. van den Berg, a translator at Yogya.

Best to have a kampong, especially for Chinese peranakans, reckoned at 25,000 in Central Java. van Overstraten plans to set up a kampong on the W. side [of] Surabaya river (Brantas).

New Uang Bandhan set up at Yemen. Some Yogya Daengan soldiers robbed by Chinese (Tambour Nitiprawira). C.P. draws up 2 companies of Prajurits to send [to] Yemen. Waterloo, Damono, Wiraguna, C.P.'s patih to withhold C.P.

Wat: Message to Vala – do v.d. Berg orders Chinese to hand over Tambour immediately. Very nearly open conflict Solo-Yogya.

Sultan is adopting his old, former behavior and day & night makes the rounds of his places (pesanggrahans). He resides preferably in the limestone hills. "Zijn hoogheid de Sultan begint naar zijn ondergang weder te gaan, en zwart nachten dag op zijn plaatser rond, hij logeert naar bij preferentie op de kalk berg." [^p153-1]

Gamping

**11 Dec 1803 (Wat. Eng.)**
Approaching Puasa in Yogya. Payments for ending a term for tax on Grobogan area. Showed a new term be instituted? Need for negotiation [with] R. Rongga (new payment to him). [? VOC leasing teak forest in Grobogan].

**28 Nov 1803 (Wat. Eng.)**
Plot of Chinese in Banyumas. In connection with plot [in] Tegal/Cheribon. They [the] Chinese had asked for troops from [?] (Juwengging comes from Solo). Our only brotherhood sworn in Monday Chinese in Cheribon. No political danger.

**26 Dec 1803 (Wat. Eng.)**
Visit to pepper gardens in Loano. Found them in a deplorable state.

**31 Dec 1803 (Wat. Eng.)**
Waterloo to meet Surjan at Wanacatur: To give horse to CP (but horse so beautiful that MB II takes it for himself). So Waterloo has to speak out boldly and give horse in person to CP. 

Talk to MB II about means of getting rid of bandits from VL.

MB II gives Waterloo a present of horses.

**1 April 1804**
Res Yogya → MB [Mangkubumi]: Waterloo to talk over Javanese affairs. One cannot spend too long because a royal wedding [?wedding NO]—Danureja's wife has to wait; as non-Javanese [NOT specified in the letter] Bengal ahis.

**6 April 1804**
Res Yogya → Solo: Dismissing Patih Mangkupraja, sent to Banyumas on the advice of outer Regent Cakranegara, 1st Dalem Regent Suradilaga.

**10 April 1804**
Res Yogya → Solo: Letter for Cakranegara/Suradilaga from OR [Outer Regents] about dismissing Mangkupraja.

**13 April 1804**
Res Yogya → Solo: MB dismisses R.T. Wiryadiningrat, given 50 cacah. Replaces him by Kliwon Mangkuyuda. [LXXVII]

**24 April 1804**
Res Yogya: Letter [to] Gov. Verheul about purchasing pikuls (125 lbs) of cotton thread for 15 guilders a pikul (catoen garens). Only once a year cotton thread can be bought; when MN [Mangkunegara] Regents come to Yogya.

*Marginal Note (Left side):*
> Cotton thread from MN Regents

*Table/Calculation:*
* After sorting = sold by the 100 threads.
* 4 1/2 - 5-6 Rood Realen [midling price 5 1/2 Rood Realen].
* 600 strings (threads) = 1 pikul.
* Must pay 3 1/2 → 42 Rood Realen.
* Would be difficult to gather together.
* 25-30 pikuls: would be extremely difficult to get 50-60 pikuls.

**2 May 1804**
Res Yogya → Solo: Letter from PB IV [Pakubuwana IV] by Gandik Prayaninggala → MB telling of dismissal of Mangkupraja, appointment of R.T. Cakrajaya as R. Adip. Danuningrat.

## Building Fort at Klaten

Underlaying Batavian Republic, salute 21 guns, first stone laid. 25 July (see Copiebook p. 91). 28 July Rest [Resident] Yogya Priyayis return.

### Indigo Factory in Loano
9 Aug 1804 Res Yogya: Only factory on HB [Huis Binnenland] Domaine. Stood for 8 years (1789-1802). Destroyed on HB. Request in 1802.

Produces:
- Dec 1796: 125 pounds
- July 1798: 625 pounds
- July 1800: 488 pounds
- May 1801: 250 pounds
- Total: 1488 pounds

100 families in Loano: Delegated to tend indigo plants: production small for labor—but land not suitable for indigo production. Land produces 2 pikuls in Loano as would 10 pikuls in Pekalongan/Surabaya.

### Cotton Procurement
30 Sept 1804 Res Yogya: Asked humbly to be excused buying cotton yarn at 15 sp. D. [Spanish Dollars]. Could only get it at between 3½ - 42 sp. D. pikul. 1 Rond Real = 2 Sp. Dollars. 2.80.

HB says DR [Directeur] for 60 pikuls = 21 2/3 Rond Real p. pikul. Payment 1,800/2400 sp. D. for 60 pikuls (40 sp. D. pikul). But some pikuls missing.

Cotton thread cheaper to buy on boundary and North coast regencies. Suggests Res. Tegal could produce the cotton thread. Cotton may be transported ran into VK [Vorstenlanden]. Cotton not produced in VK. Has to be imported from Banyumas/Kedu to Yogya = at least 40 sp. D. p. pikul in tolls. Material cost double/treble in Yogya.

**20 Nov 1804**
Res Yogya -> Solo: Communication that Sunan permits Mangkubumi [^p163-1] to return home. 
Regency Japaraga: R.T. Sasrajaya raised as R.T. Sasrawijaya.

**29 Dec**
Res Yogya: Letter Gung Java 23 Dec 1804:
Why is rice so rare & expensive?

*   **(1) EXHAUSTING FIELDS**
    That the increasing planting of rice: over a number of years — exhausting the fields: usually 2 sometimes 3 harvests of the fields in one year —
    Production over 25-30 year period fixed: say at 3 sacks (Mangmatan) down to malkardan [^p163-2], later only 2 (Mangmatan).

*   **(2) INCREASE IN TRANSPORT COSTS**
    Earlier 1/2 Ducatoon would hire a horse from Yogya to Semarang. Now at least 1 Ducatoon.
    Each horse 2 Pikuls Rice = :: price increased by 1/4 per sack.

*   **(3) MUCH GREATER POPULATION OF JAVA**
    Pop had doubled itself in 50 years: (throughout Java)
    1755 HB I has 5-6 children = 2 still alive Ngabchi [^p163-3]
    1792 = 200 grandchildren = NK born after 1755
    P. Mangkubumi not yet 30 years old has: (born c. 1775)
    (Jong Ratu Anom) (has 28-29 children)
    (Later Ratu Mas)

116986

AVJ 1804 (Yogya) -1804-

29 Dec Res Yogya DJM

**2nd Question**
? RICE CULTURE = DECREASE/INCREASE over last 25 years.
If Rice Farmer remains near rice crop after allowance for his own consumption & deliveries for Sultan?

Waterloo Replies: That throughout JAVA CERTAIN INCREASE in RICE CULTIVATION.
> "One has only to direct one's eyes to the lands which produce rice, and which before 20 years ago were still WASTE and UNCULTIVATED."
> (men behoeft slechts 3yn oog te bevestigen op die landen waar thans rijst voortteelt, en die voor 20 jaren noch woest en onbebouwd lagen.)

RICE FIELDS reclaimed from woods - make the most lonely e.g. JAMBUS MAS in Kedu. RICELANDS.

RICE FARMER usually brings his increase/extra crop to market even from the furthest désas. But because he has to pass a large number of toll-gates and bandars - the price of rice increases.

**PRICE OF RICE?**
For White Table Rice = along road through Kedu from Semarang -> Yogya. For some time:
For a HORSE PICUL (i.e., 250 lbs)
240 CATTIES = 20 PICULS.
65 DUBBELTJES = 6.50 guilders.
For less good quality: 60 DUBBELTJES.

Generally ONLY 2 MAIN SORTS OF RICE: ON MARKET in Yogya.
More usual price table rice = 100 DUBBELTJES p. picul.
Lesser quality = 90 " "
## Example Profit Margin & Producer

*   Bakul = Buys 240 catties White rice @ 60 Dus [^p165-1]
*   Marketing Payment
*   Hire a horse to take produce to Yogya @ 16 Dus
*   Tax on each horse pikul @ market @ 2.8"
*   Tolls/Gates: Magelang/Wening/Kamlakka/Malater each 20 suits 0.5 = 3.-
*   Hire horse almost same as profit = 15.6.
*   91.6 = profit = 8.4 - overhead [^p165-2]

Usual price for one horse pikul rice in Yogya market = 96 3/5 Dus/catties. On 240 catties = Bakul gets 2 3/5 per pikul. Scarcely 4 Dus/catties. 
Has to work 11 hours = passes bad/impassable roads. Also must look out for robbers. One pikul = 125 lbs. Carrying rice = 3750 lbs for 30 pikuls.

Rice in Yogya = more expensive than other places:
*   Poorer roads.
*   When compared to road along N. coast to Surabaya.
*   And Surabaya via Solo River.
*   Rice in Solo 1/2 the cost than Yogya.
*   Bagilen/Banyumas/Kema/Kedok = vast yielding rice: but transport difficult.

27 Sept. 1804 (pp. 87-96)
Meeting PB II / Danuningrat at Sala. Signed J.G. van den Berg 21 Jumadilakir AJ 1731 (27 Sept. 1804).

For local security in the:
1. Mannis / Tampings to have right to round up vagabonds and send them to Sala.
2. To swear an oath never to aid/abet thieves.
3. Ones oath taken to be given Royal order to hunt out thieves.
4. Every apanage holder to give order to lowest Biku to maintain local security.

---

5. Small apanages & 3-2 pungu Nawah Tenjungar. Also to similarly guarded.
6. Once mantris/tampings properly bricked to return to their districts.
7. If fail to carry out orders [^p167-1] to be punished by public display on pasebaan to be fened in for a week long without kris. Guilty to be placed in nun from 7-11 on pasebaan for 1 week.
8. Vagabonds to be handed over to BP for punishment.
9. If desa attacked by robbers, surrounding desas to come to rescue. To follow robbers until trail lost.
10. If trail lost, dikil to be punished by being placed on crossroads of pasebaan for 1 week.
11. If dikil still cannot find trail after 40 days, to be beaten & tanjer blows.
12. If suspicious people come to spend night in desa, report to be made to village head.
13. Purchasing of stolen goods/aiding, abetting criminals to be punished + 15 tanjer blows + display on pasebaan + goods to be sold.
14. If heads of prajurits/kliwon aid/abet criminals, for 2 weeks to be stripped of bawat/upacara [^p167-2] until tried by court as ordinary subjects & punished accordingly. Family of lurah to be punished by taking away kinayal bawat/hampit [^p167-3] but not paying it for 2 weeks.
15. Similar punishment for apanage holder if allows robbers onto his fields. High officials & members of royal family.
16. If attack from Yogya or Solo by robber but no proof found, affair to lapse; if proof found would be treated accordingly.
18. If mantris/tampings take part & robber in activities to be banished to woods, kabara & wife/children to be kept in kraton.
19. Kamipuhs/heads of bupatis/kliwons etc. to take oath to cooperate & dikil.
21. If chia. bandar attacked by robbers, 300 reals to be set aside to track down robbers. If robbers captured, within...

---

27 SEPT. 1804
Yogya-Solo Agreement on Local Security.
Pp. Br. 42a
Pp. 87-91

21. Cont. Apptd. time 300 Reals not to be given as indemnity if not 300 reals [text illegible due to strike-through]... & those villages who had helped track down robbers to be given a *douceur*. Chinese living in villages who are not bandar or guards at bandar to be treated in same way as Jav.

22. If robbers not captured within 40 days = to be punished. Imprisonment to be stopped immediately robber captured.

23. If Pantha (Chinese) puny [or possibly "persons"] involved in criminal activities robbers to be punished by Dutch Resident. If crimes small proportions to be imprisoned in Rontia Solo for a short time; if serious to be referred to Raad van Justitie Semarang.

## 1804–05

*   **AN 'Yogya Brieven' no. 36 1803–1810.**

---

**Waterloo – N. Engelhard, 20 Mar. 1804**
In presence of Patih, & all palem / lield Tumenggungs, Pangéran Adinegara, Dipakusuma, Regent of CP-Wiraguna, [and] 2 kt Panjis of Sultan. In all 12 persons. Represent Sultan & whole court. Salute of 21 guns from Fort & Raising of new Batavian Republic flag at Residency house.

**Id. to Id., 22 Mar. 1804**
MPI sends suppliant Boyolali to enquire into anything lacking in court. Pemang of Boyolali at present in Yogya for Garebeg Besar. Therefore, commission to be postponed for some days.

**Id. to Id., 24 Jun. 1804**
Jan Jin Ning – contracts to build new *Stadhuis* at Semarang.

**Id. to Id., 12 Jul. 1804**
Capture of 2 Jav. smugglers, Trunadangang Putra, in Yogya area. Had sailed from Pulo Pinang / Malacca / Batavia / Semarang.

**Id. to Id., 10 Aug. 1804**
Suicide of Chinese Bandhar Jan Joen of Japan accused of extortionate practices on Sultan's mancanagara subjects. Placed under arrest in Blokkamer of Fort. Hanged himself by kabaya from nail on door. [^p170-1]

---

23 Sept. 1805

Soldier Frans Paul settles at the foot of Merapi, earns a living by making/selling butter.

Takes on a girl called Kuwak, earlier a Jarak girl who had fled from the court.[^p171-1]

Renawijaya or Nitradirana[^p171-2] in charge of Jarak girls in the prov. of Mataram discover Kuwak at pasar of Trowulan.

Paul chases after them, shoots Renawijaya dead at Netis, [and] seriously wounds Nitradirana.

Paul placed under arrest in fort; Sultan informed of what steps should be taken.

Murder of a Chinese near pasar Karang Mojo. Bagong/Kulud, 2 Javanese accused of murder.

Waterloo to Eng, 19 Jan. 1805.
Concept. Aparth Necut Midnive, 19 Jan. 1805. M. Waterloo to N. Engelhard.

Immediately MBI ascends throne:
Tax Farm out [to] Kapitein Cina on Mataram Tax Farm in Yogya.
Join Pajang, Bagelen, Nuhawati/Romo & Birds Nest Clipping. + Kedu, MN Lands.

1754 1755 [at] 3 year Tax Farm 16,000 Ronde Reaalen @ 28 stuivers p.a. 2.80
8,000 paid at Phasa.
[4] " " " " [paid]

Every Time Tax paid = Presents to MBI =
34 small Pathoelan
34 small Nots of Chintz
1 gold payong
1 yellow "
1 white " + gold bands.
+ Confiture.

+ Payment of Hang Bikti. 4,000 for 3 years on Receipt of gold piagem.

1757 1758 2nd 3 year " " " " Raised to 6,000 " " " "
Ki Mantri, a v. ordinary Chinese Tax Farmer. But cannot pay sum required.

1762: Tax Farm placed in hands of DRI [Danureja I; transcribed as PRI] + favourite Inner Regent Pindwija.
To order to get administration of Tax Farm generate a capable person.
.: Kyai Tum. Reknaregara appointed as Tax Bupati.
.: gold Piagem given to RN every 3 years.
Every [time] Reknaregara no longer directly in charge of Tax Farm. MBI no longer bothers.
Rekhanigara = delegates tax farm to Capitan Cina & Kedu Kie.
RN pays money to AB II keeping small profit for himself.
But after 1 1/2 years = 3 Tax Terms Kie gives up tax farm.

Riknanegara = worried about tax responsibility. Seeks help of Res. Lapro.
But Lapro has eyes on administering tax farm himself :: lets it fall into even greater disorder.

Then Lapro agrees to take it over = giving RN advancing money to pay for tax shortcomings.
RN/Lapro = share tax farm.
RN non included later as tax farm regent.

Tax of Royal Domains & Bird's Nest since 1762 in hands of Resident.
Tax of MN lands given by Lapro to Bp. Widana & Madura for sum of 1750 R.R. p.a.
MN tax for them 1750 R.R. p.a.
Now tax from MN = 2,500 R.R. p.a.

1765 4th Tax Term = 20 karg bikti paid.
At beginning Lapro pays = 23,000
At end of residency = 31,000

1774
When van Rhijn takes over.
1774 raised by [van Rhijn] to 34,000
Tax farm 3,000
2nd Renewal under van Rhijn raised to 44,000

Lapro complains bitterly about raising of tax farm by 10,000.
AB II tries to appoint 3 Jav. Regents to take care tax.
But admin. of tax farm inefficient.
1805 

An Yogya Brieven 20.86.

Given back to Repro who eventually accepts raising of tax to 10,000 N.p.P. 

3rd renewal of tax-farm under van Rhijn: no raise but V.R. forced to give a "present" to M.B.I (i.e. Hang Bikti) 2 combs set with jewels worth 1,200 R.R. each. 

Remains as such till van IJ takes over from van Rhijn in 1786. Renewal for 6 years @ 44,000. But Hang Bikti raised to 1,500 + clothes + beautiful cane set + diamonds. 

*Ironderial = /2.10*

1792: Renewal for 3 years, raised to 46,000. No Hang Bikti. 

1795: Tax farm raised by 10,000 to 56,000 N.p.P. 

No change 1795 - 1803 under van den Berg. On renewal date, Dec. 1804, Waterloo presses for tax to be lowered, but tax would probably remain as 56,000. 

Tax farm now entirely stipulated by wilful choice of Sultan. Resident never interfered in Royal domains/birds nest clipping. 

N.p. Real 10,000 p.a. Strandgeld Company paves. 1,000,000 N.p.P. paved. In strandgeld payment.
9031

Eng. to Waterloo, 13 Dec. 1805

Report of Javanese Malays from Tringgano who had arrived in Semarang. British military build-up in Pulo Pinang. 30 many war/40 trading vessels, many destined for China.

Asks Wat. to encourage MBII to keep watch on N. coast under pretext of preventing piracy.

Asks how practicable landing on N. coast & ability to march to courts.

How far could central Javanese rulers be trusted.

---

Relate of Narip Hussein (Arab) from Tringgano, 12 Dec. 1805, written in Jav.

Lives in Buginese kampong in Semarang & Nuragan Nadan. Goes on chaloup to Tringgano to trade. After 3 months in Tringgano returns Nadan to Semarang. After 28 days at sea arrives back 12 Dec.

At Karimon Java on outward journey meets 32 pirate boats, Elanong Parjajaps.

Dispute between Raja Klantan, brother-in-law king of Tringgano. Raja Klantan gets upper hand.

2 English trading ships to Tringgano to get load of pepper. Pepper now at 12 R. maatter per pikul.

Many Javanese from Solo on British ships as sailors.

16 Dec. 1805: Arab pricot [sic] taken as prisoners by Elanong. Sea pirates from Banyumas / N. Cirebon. Now at Malakka for 16 Rohde haatch to Yogya join But. English captains, treat id v. well by " " given upkeep/clothes, with money. No wish to return to Java.

Rajan Tringgano = idea of sending ships to Riouw to trade, take on cargo of ginger & sell it in Semarang. Than Muda allows him to sail with merchandise. But trip cancelled at last moment because news that British about to make an attack on Java.
## 1805-9

**Prokjo Brieven no. 39**

*   **31 Jul. 1805** (Waterloo to DRII [Danureja II; transcribed as PRII]): About dispatch of HBII's ambassador to Mekka, embassy sent from Semarang to Batavia.
*   **5 Aug. 1805** (Id. to Id.): Repair of Fort in Yogya: But sirap delivered of poor quality. For 2 years already HBII promising that something should be done & repair of fort, but nothing achieved.
*   **26 Aug. 1805** (Wat to DRII [Danureja II; transcribed as PRII] / Id. to Id.): HBII appoints Ratu Anom - Ratu Mas:
    *   R. Ay. Sriwulan = Ratu Kencana Wulan
    *   R. A. Natadiningrat = Binawa
    *   R. A. Danureja = Angger
    *   R. A. Rongga Pwa = Madureza
    *   R. Ay. Nepuh = Azom. 
    *(Marginal note: Tituq wives of HBII)*

    Wat replies to envoy re: appointment of R. Ay. Sriwulan as permaisuri of PBIV.

*   **4 Feb. 1806** (Wat to DRII [Danureja II; transcribed as PRII]): HBII's pilgrims to Mekka: [^p179-1]
    Kyai Haji Muhamad Mataram, Benawi, Nenawi, Muhidin, Haji Mukmin, Yanup, Raphim, Haji Nur Kasim, Abdullah Imeran, Haji Arifin, Sukur, Haji Muhamad, Muhamad Nemann, Dono Nicir, Haji Ahmad, Nur Rahman, Habit, Amad Menir, Amad Iman, Caruben, Tayar, Wirawijaya, Bahi Rawi, Amad Arif, Jaka Ria, Umar, Abdullah.

    27 pilgrims to Mekka: Sent back by government in Batavia without making journey.
    1. Serious war in S. India between French/British.
    2. Illness amongst pilgrims, one already dead.
    [But later have on own initiative.]

*   **27 Feb. 1806** (Id. to Id.): Band of 3,000 from Cirebon infesting country above Batavia & making roads unsafe.
*   **27 Mar. 1806** (Id. to Id.): Destruction to Yogya Fort because of earthquake. Similar repairs needed at Klaten.
*   **23 Aug. 1806** (Id. to Id.): Complaint of Ir. Cornelius about Yogya work force building fort at Klaten. Planks/timber not produced on time, men not present.

---

Wiraguna in charge of Yogya work force but absent, workers in pondhoks playing cards continually. Yogya 3 months behind Solo workers. HB II needs to give necessary orders to spur on workers.

*   **Wat. to PR II, 9 Sept. 1806:** Yogya work force 66 days working on fort achieve very little.
*   **ID to ID, 9 Sept. 1806:** Capitan Cina of Japan Jan Junko. Attack on Chinese in Jumbling, one Chinese killed and Pajang as goods plundered.
*   **G.W. Wiese to HB II, 4 Palkangidah 1735 (22 Dec. 1808):** Question of HB II providing a sum of money to government "as sign of attachment to Dutch government." Wiese no doubt that HB II would pay such a sum.
*   **Wiese to PR II, 24 Jan. 1809:** All supplies of gunpowder for salutes at feasts etc. to be stopped. Limited to supply of 500 lbs p.a. [Note: MN Regent given orders to hold themselves in readiness to march at first opportunity.]
*   **ID to ID, 31 Jan. 1809:** HB II gives orders to hold Military Exercises for Yogya. Daendels had warned HB II in 1808 that such military manoeuvres should be interpreted as a declaration of war. [Marginal note: *Mil. Exer. HB II*] Asks PR II to prevail on HB II to halt military exercises. Refusal of Wiese to appear in kraton unless stool provided for him.
*   **Wiese to PR II, 3 Feb. 1809:** Orders of HB II to halt military manoeuvres. Ask for 500 lbs of gunpowder to be paid at Fp. 200 Ronde Real.
*   **ID to ID, 4 Feb. 1809:** Need for building new powder magazine in Fort.
*   **Daendels to Wiese, 21 Jan. 1809:** Annoyance of Daendels about PR II: (1) arrest of 4 Dutch Jagers, (2) sending letter to Solo (30 Dec. 1808) without permission of Resident. If PR II makes 3rd mistake would not escape necessary punishment and Prang Wedono's corps would be sent to kraton to garrison the fort.
*   **Wiese to PR II, 1 Mar. 1809 (13 Nuva 1736):** Report by Lt. Col. de Chasteauvieux that 2,000 cavalry of HB II/other princes carry out exercises on Saturday (? at tournament). Promises that everything would be organized satisfactorily when D [Daendels] visits Yogya.

AN 'Yogya Brieven 20.86.'

*   **Relays of Captains of British merchant ships at Trenggano.**

*   **12 Dec. 1805:**
*   **Semarang:** British ship arrives from Pulo Pinang laden with cloth, iron, knives, cannon.
*   **Jav. sailors tell of 30-40 British merchantmen at P.P. destined for China & enemy war to make attack on Java in Jan. 1806.**
*   **Tell of planned English landing on N. coast & intention to march into interior.**
*   **English Capt. Nay afraid of krises/pikes of Javanese until assured by Riouw/Lingga pirates who had captured 1000s of Jav. on N. coast that Jav. run at slightest firing of cannon/musket; always fall on knees & beg mercy.**

*   **Dutch N.C.O.s to be sent to strategic places on Java's N. coast:** Karang Bolong, Kadilangu, Rawa, Mancingan, Rangkot, Pacitan.
*   **To report on any sighting of English ships.**
*   **Writes to PB II about fear of pirates on S. coast.**

---

*   **Landing on N. coast difficult because of "Terrible Breakers." But English able to make landings at Madras despite breakers :: why not at Java's N. coast.**
*   **PB IV: How far Sultan's princes loyal to V.O.C. If British attack would HB II / PB IV remain at peace with each other.**
*   **Memory of 1750 still fresh: PB IV would try anything to get back other ½ of kingdom.**
*   **Whilst Dutch busy & foreign enemy: Nuzan/Sultan would turn against each other.**
*   **HB II should be warned of impending danger.**

(209) 3081

+ European N.C.O.s.
3. Sultan/Nusan should send troops to places on N. coast most likely to be attacked.
4. Royal Troops to be placed on prepared footing for attack.
5. European soldiers to be kept together to be used at once in a body v. enemy.
6. MN Regiments to be ordered to hold themselves in readiness to march troops to Yogya/Solo.
7. Yogya/Solo to be provisioned for possibility of long campaign.
8. Preparation for ammunition.
9. Yogya closest to N. coast in case of attack: artillery should be strengthened + capable artillery officer.
10. Residents Yogya/Solo should be sent to Yogya to co-operate with each other.
11. Any new 2nd Residents appointed to coast should be briefed on situation/thoroughly acquainted + kratons.

Waterloo - DRI,
24 Puasa, 1732
16 Dec. 1805.
Warning v. Pirates on N. coast.
Asks measures to be taken to strengthen coastal areas.
Fear that ever European would land as Pirates on N. coast would try to capture kratons.
Nusa Kambangan would be a prime target for a landing place.

Arab priest (? Kyai Melangi) fled from Yogya to Semarang, had joined British merchant marine [^p182-1], + had told British that Yogya on N. coast [^p182-2] had a near Sultan huge treasure.

Yogya 1805 1805-6

An 'Yogya Brieven' 20. 86.

**Eng to Waterloo, 23 Dec. 1805**
800 men might be spared from Adm. Hartsinck's squadron wintering in Oonthoek. Nat. Artillery Company to Boyolali. Adm. H's force to be conveyed down Solo river to Solo. Hartsinck's men to be placed between Solo/Yogya. Necessary preparations to be made for their reception. [^p183-1] to be camped at Boyolali.

**Waterloo-Eng. 27 Dec. 1805**
Boyolali suitable for army camp but too far (15 1/2 hrs journey) from Yogya. Would take 4 days march. B unhealthy, breezy winds in rainy season. Prices of food in Boyolali would rise steeply as was found during recent troubles in 1790. Tangkipan/Delanggu more suitable. Both 1/2 Hartsinck's force to Solo/1/2 to Yogya. Yogya more vulnerable from S. coast than Solo. Sunan/Sultan would be prepared to provide food for men of Hartsinck's squadron. Difficulty of getting down Sala river in rainy season.

**Eng - Wat. 5 Jan. 1806**
Hartsinck informs Eng. that until Java actually attacked by a foreign enemy, he could not allow sailors/marines to be garrisoned in Central Java. [^p183-2] would have to rely on Central Javan rulers. No reinforcements would be sent from Bat/Semarang garrisons.

---

**Wat - Eng.**
**10 Jan. 1806**

In order to encourage HB II [^p184-1] to greater activity, [the] watchfulness on S. coast should be informed of impending danger from British. Dutch citizens should watch fort to allow military/dragoons & Nultan’s [^p184-2] troops to withstand attack.

Would never be able to persuade HB II to leave kraton, even if an enemy attacked on S. coast. [He] would not entrust treasure to V.O.C. because of treasure. [He is] not courageous enough to lead troops in person v. enemy.

If HB II’s force [is] fighting far away, HB II might give himself up to V.O.C. If kraton taken, HB II would probably retreat to Jambu [or] Ambarawa [and] would get enough food from Kedu area. 

Proposes conditions on which forts [and] V.O.C. building in Yogya might be surrendered.

---

**Eng - Wat.**
**11 Jan. 1806**

News from N. A. van Braam in Batavia. Report [from] Nayio Alu Naga [from] Malacca. No further news of proposed British attack on Java. [10-11] men of war return to India.

**A.M.**
**Eng - Wiese**
**11 Jan. 1806**

French land reinforcements, 7000 men at Pondicherry. 

Brit. Adm. Sir Edward Pellew [with] squadron of 10-11 ships at Pulo Pinang. Returns to India to seek out French. 

25 British troops, 400-50 sepoys at Malacca. Plans of Brit. to destroy castle at Malacca. Would leave only small garrison there. [^p184-3]

Because of nagging pain in the head, Waterloo reluctantly goes to Oea [? Juwana] (Jolone).

**14 Jan 1805, Waterloo, Eng.**
Verding [? Pieter] Engelhard, a huge Javan snake, apparently very good for health if eaten; captured in Pepper Estate in Loano. MB II from the beginning, too unfriendly and too conceding a prince.

1. "Te onvriendelijk door dat men omwijken hem in eene terkes aangevallen, en voor zijn daden over beschrant heeft gemaakt."
2. "Te toegeveent omdat men dan eene en zaak aangevangen hebbende, hoe ook nu eene beschouwt, is het zelfde, men niet genoegzaam heeft volgehouden, en de Sultaan als het waar zijn zou gekomen heeft."

Better character than PB IV—could place more trust in Yogya than in Solo court.

[3. Son-in-law g. W. Engelhard]
[Brothers-in-law van Buck Holtz, sailing in Java despite this to O. Coast = pain in head & fever. g. Wisman, etc. v. great.]

**16 Jan 1805, Waterloo, Eng.**
Raden Kedahen sick again. Does not get on well with MB II because MB II wants to get R. Ayu Oriwulan appointed. [Arat]—as he would choose [R. Ayu?] [Kedahen] situation, but old man MB II [into a bad humor (was knorrig / peevish)] spurred on by R. Ayu Oriwulan, opposed by Raden Kedahen; [caught between 2 fires.]

## 1805 (Yogya) (Jan)

*   HB II retires to cool of Ngawatreja: R. Ayu Sriwulan & children.
*   Border incident Kendhal/Kaliwungu.
*   Proposal by Van den Berg to come to Yogya from Vala but Waterloo still indisposed.
*   Attempt to get new Buitenzorg contract: VOC - VH Rulers.
*   Choosing a new 2nd Resident for Yogya: either Domus or Van Meemert.

#### 31 Jan 1805 (Waterloo & Engelhard)

Message from HB II: 2 Mantris, because bad behaviour, had been taken into custody in Kraton but escaped. Sutadiwiria flees to father in Vala desa Bulus (12½ hrs journey from Yogya). 

Jumadiningrat planning to send troops to Bulus to reseize him. 

Refusal: Waterloo to allow armed men to be sent, but asks representatives at Vala court (Yogya-Solo relations in jeopardy) -> Danuningrat [Patih Vala].

But 50 Katangsung soldiers to Klaten: Representatives to HB II to get them recalled. Sutadiwiria & others flees from Bulus.

#### 27 Feb 1805 (Wat-Eng)

P. Ngabehi in extremely poor health: heavy attack of diarrhea.

#### 28 Feb 1805 (Wat-Eng)

Visit: HB II to P. Ngabehi.
28 Res.

MB [Majoor/Resident] spends 11 hours in Resident's house. Very interested in garden—wants Waterloo's help to lay out a new garden; accepts glasses & wine: [^p188-1] especially tree guards.

Promised Resident help to repair house. MB asks for Javanese horses.

MB goes out to P. ngeshi'a Dalem in carriage; everyone else excluding Crown Prince have to walk.

7 March 1805
Waterloo-Engelhard
At Surakarta
During troubles of 1805.

30 March 1805
Waterloo-Eng.
(Cannonade from Yogya)
Lucas Leberfeld is sent to Grobogan to take a troublemaker, Tirtajaya, prisoner.

12 April 1805
Waterloo-Eng.
Journey undertaken by Waterloo to Tegal/Cirebon.

Cornelius makes verse at marriage ceremony of d'Abo + Mrs d'Abo. [^p188-2]

5 April 1805
War-Eng.
MB means to be very lively and can envisage many parties this year.

Plan for eight-day hunting party & camp to be set up behind Prambanan. [^p188-3]

## 1805 (April) - Yogya

**5 April 1805**
**Waterloo & Engelhard**

*   Plan still afoot to make R. Ayu Sriwulan a Ratu.
*   But Ratu Kedaton set against it.
*   MB II plan to give Ratu Kedaton title of Ratu Agung and house at Tegalreja [^p189-1].
*   But refusal of Ratu Kedaton.
*   MB II allows R. Ayu Sriwulan to carry gold payong in Ngawatreja and at Kraton.
*   CP bears great hatred for R. Ayu Sriwulan; which MB II notices: CP bent on revenge.
*   New rent for Grobogan for R. Rongga.

**7 April 1805**
**Waterloo & Engelhard**

*   Hunting party of Waterloo & CP afoot; plan to go to Klaten.
*   MB II -> Ngawatreja: gives orders that at least 4 tigers to be captured in the coming days.
*   Mas Panji Jayeng-Asmara, earlier greatest favourite of MB II, falls into disgrace [^p189-2] with MB II because of Mas [^p189-3] - whose wife was a concubine in the Kraton.
*   Plot downfalls Panji.
*   CP puts off trip to Klaten because of Jati's rage.
*   Panji beheaded, while clothed before Srimenganti.
*   Panji followers exiled to Ledaya.

---

**16 April 1805**
**Waterloo - Engelhard**
Hawick van Pabst to Yogya: Brings Japanese prints - most beautiful that bay of Nagasaki.

**19 April 1805**
**Waterloo - Engelhard**
Concerning boats allowed to sail without paying dues on Solo River: Prau Pengluput [about has 10 such praaus]. Anoyp Rongga + Capital Cinag Japara in charge of tollgates for Yogya.

> **Important?**
> **Prau Pengluput**
> Annoyance that these boats make journey to Griseek and back passing Sultana tollgates without tax. Usually Sultan does not use these praaus himself, but Raden Rongga lends them to merchants for 200 Reals p.a. apiece. Now P.B. IV [^p190-1] is making his own praus by using them directly.

**17 April 1805**
**WCT - Eng**
Mas Panti fined 300 Reals; allowed back to post.

## YOGYA LETTERS (1805)
### - KI TAPTAJANI -
#### (22 June 1805)

**22 June 1805**

**OUTER BUPATIS / PATIH:**
A priest named Ki Taptajani leaves Yogya area for Java. Taptajani does not return.

Taptajani leaves Yogya area:
1. Because he was given to Patih Danu his upkeep by Patih Danu taken away from him.
2. Ki Penghulu Yogya refuses Ki Taptajani to meet any of the Yogya Princes.
∴ COMPLETELY ROBBED OF MEANS OF LIVELIHOOD.

Taptajani = Born an inhabitant of Yogya area. Lived in Merangi. Was held in high regard by nobles of Yogya court. Learned to speak Javanese. 

Was there during the present Patih Danu II. Extremely learned in Islamic law. Manages to get positions for his sons when they were still very young with Abuminata.

> "Whenever people leave this area as young men, more attached to Java than area of birth." 
> ∴ Ratih does no difficulty in taking them away from Surakarta.

CERTAINLY ABLE TO HIDE UP WITH HIS SONS IN VUNANU AREA.

---

Sunan PB IV gives [Tono] ki Taptajani 3,000 cacahs [land] as [mark] esteem. (Only 1 hour distant from Java)

Translate a certain book for PB IV: *Sierattalmustakkiem*; which previously no one in a position to do it causes great success for them.

? Taptajani may receive land also from PB IV.

Wishes to go to Mekka so he can live his life in peace without being bothered by either court.

Taptajani & 2 sons must be seen as extremely dangerous subjects. (? Arabs/learn to speak Javanese) Very important influence on PB IV.

This priest must always be regarded as dangerous but especially in this period. Best to exile himself [away from Central Java] would looe [lose] all his influence in court.

16 July 1805 Waterloo Engelhard: Waterloo making representations at Yogya Court to get Taptajani affair settled (?exiled priest).

Attempt to get Patih [Drit] to issue an order demanding return of Taptajani to Yogya territory to be placed under Sultan's control again.

H 311: Constantly asking after messengers to Mecca [sent] already (no).

MB II not treated very well by Goddess of U. Sea: Because clothes/jackets/kain (Borst kleedjes) thrown into the sea by MB II. All thrown up onto beach again by the waves.

## 1805

**An 'Yogya Brieven' no. 86.**

**Eng. to Wat. 25 Dec. 1805.**
> MB II should be prevailed upon to allow rice to be exported to N. coast without tolls. Great expansion of rice production in Yogya in recent years.

i. Asks how many rice fields laid out in recent years.
ii. Production of each district.
iii. If 2nd harvest generally planted.
iv. Use of buffalos in agriculture.
v. Amount of bibit (seed padi) to farmer.
vi. If farmer not discouraged by terms/tax in cultivating land.
vii. If new ploughs should be introduced.
x. If pop. increases in last 5 years. If desas increase in last 5 years.

**68 page Reply of Wat. [Similar letter in Yogya Br. no. 37]**
**Wat. to Eng. 28 Feb. 1806.**
Asks for free export of rice to Semarang & N. Coast from Yogya & encourages opening up of new sawahs. MB II active in encouraging rice production in Yogya. Not possible to open up any new ricefields in immediate vicinity of Yogya. Everything has been done which could be done. Special messengers sent to F. MN by Wat to enquire into state of affairs there.

**Concerning Export of Rice to N. Coast:**
i. Situation in MB II's lands = Mataram/Pajang/Kedu/Bagelen etc.

**Regarding commerce & panier.**
*Mataram:* Very fertile. Farmers. Well irrigated from Merapi/Jan. Lay out simple but effective irrigation system. Almost completely opened up to sawahs. Mostly rice produced.

***

## Post Java War to Yogya

**Pajang** = Rice produced here next to Solo.

**Gunung Kidul** = Beautiful Teak forests. Teak used for buildings in Vl amongst them new fort at Klaten.

**Kedu** = V. fertile = sawahs high on flanks of Merbabu / Sundoro. A lot of water - but soil v. clayey. Mostly Tobacco/Rice.

**Bagelen** = Mostly owned by Sultan. Rice/cotton - cotton woven by inhabitants.

**Exports to N. coast considerable:**
*   Kedu Tobacco / Rice from N. Kedu to Kendhal / Kaliwungu / Jambu via market at Jambu.
*   White / Striped / Batiked cloth from Yogya / Bagelen.

Most of Rice produced in area destined for local consumption. Cost of carrying rice to N. coast means little exported. Besides sale cost on N. coast less than in Yogya per pikul. Cheapest rice sold at 2 1/4 silver Rijks dollars per pikul (125 pounds). Garinos at Yogya supplied at 1 silver Ducatoon per pikul.

---

#### E. Mancanagara
In general more land devoted to rice. Rapid transport of products along Solo river.
**Grobogan (Pindaj wirasari / Magetan)** = Can send products down Solo river. Receive back salt.

If tolls should be free along Solo river would help trade. But corruption of chiefs, local farmers not encouraged to poor price paid for product produce more. If more rice demanded = farmers would have area deserted.

1806
(Yogya) -1806-

31 Jan 1806

No Javanese, even without the exception in Kedu, ever devotes all his energies/land to tobacco production. Usually rotation: 2 years rice = 3rd year tobacco: but Javanese farmer learns from experience that he cannot plant tobacco 2 years via row on same field.

Whole Kedu including area = sele Yogya produces 2000 corong tobacco in Kedu giturver (? dried) each year and dispatched for commerce; known via Javanese *penang nbak* (Penang tobacco).

One corong = 40 kanjangs = 9 20 pikuls = 2 kanjangs = 1 pikul.
1 pikul = as much as a man can carry = 50 lbs.

Complete production = 2 mn lbs. (2.50 grams). Gets 200,000 reals. Each pikul 5 reals = 10 sp. d. local market price.

## 1806

**An ‘Yogya Brieven’ 20.86**

**Wat. - Eng. 20 Feb. 1806.**

1. What ricefields laid out in Yogyakarta during past 10 years and suitable for what expansion?
* 25% increase in Yogyakarta lands during past 10 years (Nagara Agung).
* Around Kalk Berg: Gunung Samping, N.W. of Yogyakarta. Kali Bedhog. Earlier just jungle but on HB II’s instructions.
* **On Irrigation:** Dam built in River (? Kali Noka) [^p4s-1] and irrigation *sawahs*. Skillfully laid out. Roads thronged with merchants/traders/farmers. To W. of Yogyakarta that can nearly get by on horseback. [^p4s-2]
* E/N of Yogyakarta many new lands laid out: *sawahs* spreading up Merapi. New *sawahs* opened up alongside old ones. [^p4s-3]

2. How much value of harvest in each district:
* Impossible to answer question accurately.
* But rice much cheaper now than had been for a long time (1806) (only 2 rix-dollars per pikul). [^p4s-4]

3. If farmers take care to plant 2nd harvest in time:
* Yes.
* *Sawahs* very rarely left fallow. When one end of a field still being harvested, sometimes the other end already being prepared for next harvest.
* Usually in space of 2 years = 5 rice harvests gathered in Mataram/Kidhul.
* Enough water/irrigation to plant in dry season.

* But low lands in Bagelen in W, Grobogan/E. MN in different situation. [^p4s-5]

---

For months on end [they] suffer from floods. And just as in Pamek[asan] / Kudus / Pati[?] lenghal[?] cannot be planted. Even after floods subside, irrigation walls have to be built up against [the] systems [and] strengthened.

Jav. Farmer [is] much more hardworking / diligent than those who rule / administer over him. If not so oppressed / extorted by chiefs, [he] would certainly produce more.

Av. Jav. Farmer would always be prepared to work himself out of any predicament = religious convictions do not militate against this.

Would not be politic at this time for V.O.C. to interfere [in] Dutch [the] Internal Admin[istration]. [^p5s-1]

- If population [is] raising of cattle facilitated in each district [and] enough ploughing animals in [the] Jav. in general v. poor = little progress made in buffalo / cattle rearing.

No lack of beasts of burden - mainly because Jav. Farmer makes great use of those that he already has. Even when cow has just had a calf [it is] still expected to work - this has deleterious effects on quality of livestock. Many buffaloes brought from E/W MN to be slaughtered in Yogya for food.

1806
An Yogya Breven No. 86.
Wat-Eng. 28 Feb. 1806.

(III) Yearly Profit of M&I [Mangkunegaran/Mangkunegara I] from Rice product usually included in general Taxation of Nuwala [royal charter/letter regulating tollgates; transcribed as Naltan/Naratas] organized by Reksanegara [R.T. Reksanegara, head of Yogya tollgates; transcribed as Riknazegara].

8 Tollgates control trade in Rice in Mataram. Take 9,500 R. Realen p.a.
1 Tollgate & subordinate pasars for Rice in Kedu. Brings in yearly 2,800.
Pajang - 3,450 p.a.
General sum from Rice 15,750 p.a.

M&I would stand to lose 30,000 from Rice if declared custom free product on [the] way to N. Coast. Because Bandars would not dare to bid v. high on tax farm if Rice withdrawn - most of profit made from Rice.

M&I would also lose 10,000 from MN Tax. Not conceivable that [he] would give up Rice Tax just for sake of the V.O.C.

General produce of Tax p.a. 56,000 in silver money from Riknazegara.
If paid in copper money:
Every Real 70 stuiven.
An interest of 6 stuiven. [Total] f. 2-50.
Total increase 5,250.

If paid in copper money.
Pangiring money = Homage money paid 2x at Puapa [Puasa?].
3,356 1/3 Ronde Realen for various articles.
3,000 R.R. paid on inception of new Tax Term to Sultan for 3 years = 1000 p.a.

Everything Reckoned Together = 60,356 1/3 R.R.
MN Land Tax payments bring sum to 72,000 R.R.
Gold farm letter (Piagem) sealed by Sultan regarded as holy by subjects.

Rekwanegara technically in charge of tax, although first Resident organizes farm of tax. Sometimes matters of dispute about tax-farm, but if prepared to stand up to Sultan over this, D.R.I. (Deputy Resident I) is normally able to regulate it, although honour of Sultan saved by gold terms of Piagem.

Great difficulty in keeping tax renewal on old footing. 

1781: Raised 10,000 [^p7s-1]
1796: " "
1806: Tax allowed to remain on old footing. 

Despite great treasure, Sultan always prepared to accept a present in money from 1st Res. (very compliant). MB II cannot boast about his good character. MB II often lays heavy fines on wives/children/favourites if cannot pay in short time stipulated = fine raised 2x or gold/silver ornaments demanded. No one dares to gainsay MB II for fear that rice would be doubled. 

Always difficulties negotiating with MB II over money; would never get him to agree to abolishing tax on rice. [^p7s-2]

D.R.I. – to most junior Bupati, afraid to broach topics relating to finance to MB II for fear of falling into disgrace and being fined heavily. 

Wat always deals in writing to D.R.I./Nayakas, and if D.R.I./Nayakas blamed by MB II, can always take refuge behind Wat’s letter. 

Sometimes D.R.I. declares himself afraid to take message to MB II. 

Renewal of tax last year took months of negotiations, Wat forced to give MB II 3,000 R.R. before new Piagem given.
