1806

An’Djoijo
Brieven 20.86.

Wat. Eng.
28 Feb 1806.

Most oxen used as beasts of burden & some bulls.

5. If enough *bibit* (seed padi) at hand & if sold to Javanese farmer at their price. During failures of harvest, seed padi often eaten. Those farmers who do eat *bibit* get more from heads/neighbors. Price depends on scarcity. Lack of seed padi not usual. Sometimes local heads of villages take care that always enough seed padi [^p8s-1] and these villages often most prosperous. Although agriculture follows old patterns, amount of land cultivated constantly increasing in size.

6. If farmer discouraged by unfair/heavy conditions imposed on cultivation e.g. taxes etc. Exactions/Extortions on common man well-known. "O Heaven! how is it possible that he can endure it." "Harima" altitude of Javanese peasant = Believing in fate: "Nadah naya punya untung begitul" [^p8s-2]. Heavy exactions by lurahs/bikils. Hierarchy of officials works like clockwork. Every Javanese, from greatest to smallest, oppresses others. If Bup. takes a horse from a mantri, mantri recuperates loss from villagers.

7. If land/till remain fallow because of lack of activity by lurahs/bikils. Sometimes wet land uncultivated. If Javanese farmer finds it impossible to make ends meet, would flee to another area.

---

Sometimes 2/3 of a village moves in that fashion. Once exactions cease, [people] would return to village. If [a] farmer lacks plough/buffaloes, [it is] often the case that [the] local lurah will lend them necessary equipment, but sometimes [they] have to pay a great deal.

"Suspicious nature of HB II: [He is] known as [the] 'bounder'!" [He is] jealous of his greatness. [He is] touchy about property. [He] pepper gardens in Loano laid out by V.O.C. = Refusal [by] HB II to let them [be] extended. Refusal also to countenance laying out of pepper gardens close to Yogya.

Suggestions to N B II (by approval of Eng.) to turn over petty criminals to V.O.C. to work in pepper gardens, but refusal of HB II says it would make him ashamed in front of his subjects. HB II decides to ban petty criminals to MN and to order Rongga PWP to see that they never entered Yogya again. Rongga puts them to death in order that [they run] no risk of none returning to Yogya and Rongga falling into disgrace [with] HB II. HB II [has] very little vision [or] understanding of [the] consequences of his orders.

[The] possibility of introducing another plough in [the] Dutch plough. If a deeper driving plough [is] introduced, land would be more fruitful, producing 2 coyangs instead of 1. But farmer/draft animals [do not have] enough strength to manage [such a] plough. [The] farmer [is] closed to new innovations in agriculture.

Population: Great increase in past 5 years can be judged from age structure of population — numerous children below age of 5. 7 births to every 5 deaths.
## 1806

**An Yogya Brieven no. 86.**

**Wat - Eng. 28 Feb. 1806.**

Question: Free import of salt from N. coast. N. coast salt only consumed in E. MN [Mataram], Pajang, as far as Boyolali. Rest Kedu, Bagelen, Banyumas, Ledhok, Rima get salt from N. coast.

If salt from N. coast free import — great profit would be made by a handful of traders; rest would not benefit.

#### Population VH:

But many great infant mortality, for children below age of 12 (See also Kedu Reg.)

Increase in pop 1 in every 23 in 5 years. 4% p.a.

Number of families (Hearths):
* Nunan: 87,000
* Nullan: 90,000
* MN: 4,000
* Total: 181,000

Reckoning every family @ 5 = 905,000 people in VH.
1760-5 = Increase 5,000 for every 100,000 = 950,250

1801 = 1 million 393,000 393,472.
In 45 years increase in VH pop. of 488,472.
Increase 50% in 45 years.
Estimation above probably on conservative side.

1800 - 1805 Increase 63,887. 1 in 23.

Great increase in Desas can be seen by taking a longer time scale:
1785 - 1805

Great increase in av. number of people in each Desa.
+ Opening up of new land.
Est. of new Desas in waste lands.
Do not deleterious for old Desas.
(10)? Inhabitants of Desas own land/ricefields:
The greatest part of Desa inhabitants own their own ricefields. All land is divided into cacahs owned by Sultan. Only mountain dwellers/Blandhong people in Teak forests are excepted, and coolies on roads who find it easy to earn a living, often employed for trading purposes by Chinese/Arabs. As time goes by, courts would get economically/politically stronger vis-a-vis V.O.C. should bide its time in asserting its superiority to Yogyakarta.

(11)? Nature of land tenure/permanent or not?
Some districts: 50 men can live on 1 jung of land; in others only 2-4. Size of a jung in one district differs from another. Sawahs split up in a heterogeneous fashion, irregular, but each farmer can recognize his own plot. It is clear that a grandson remains in possession of lands that had been owned by his grandfather. Very little exchange of land unless Lurah/Bikil of a village takes the land for himself and gives it to another. Reasonably common.

(12)? Size of a jung of sawah, whether proportion of ricefields/inhabitants in each village is correct.
Some Desas better administered by Lurahs and have more population than others. Some orang menumpang in each village who have no land, but cannot be estimated. Taken: mountain dwellers/Blandhong people who have no land form a very small % of the whole. Great differences in size of a jung in different kabupatens. Locally, Javanese who own no sawah perform corvée services for others. But most prajurits [also some prajurits of Sultan] own no land, but few in number. But most prajurits have land.

---

## 1806
**AN 'Yogya Brieven' 20.06.**

**Wat. – Eng. 28 Feb. 1806.**
Still some lands fallow suitable for *sawah*. But usually in remote areas. All land usually v. scrupulously used. If no *sawahs* laid out:
1. Because of lack of people.
2. Lack of water, or lack of skill of villagers in constructing irrigation schemes.

? If MB II [^p12s-1] would be constrained to force excess pop. in heavily populated areas to move out to remote lands to open up rice fields?

MB II v. clever at squeezing most profits from land. 3 years ago new measurement of land comes into force:
> **Pancas (Cutting)** = all land remaining over had been land taken over by MB II + income directly paid to him by new tax/land assessment.

Sometimes pay labourers used for projects but rarely. If numbers of idlers get too great can be near to Batavia. 
*   Some idlers take on followers of Javanese [^p12s-2] nobles.
*   Others become *wong durjana* / bandits.
*   Idlers mostly too lazy to work in *sawah* prefer to work as coolies. Transport goods for v. small payment of Van Nenenhoven.

---

**Eng. – Wat. 15 Jul. 1806.**
State of affairs in Cirebon = getting gradually worse each day. Eng. chosen to go on commission to Cirebon to help put down disturbances.

---

**Wat. – Eng. 29 Jul. 1806.**
Wat. to tell Eng. departure for Cirebon + necessity for better boundary division between Yogya / Cirebon lands. For fugitives from Cirebon + robbers.

---

HB II asking about whether Hajis dispatched by him to Mecca had left Semarang yet. [See the notes] Already over a year since Hajis had left Yogya. Should be back from Mecca now if had been sent directly on V.O.C. ship. Tell HB II the difficulties of sea-traffic because of British blockade.

A.A. Canna - Wat. 4 Aug. 1806.
*   HB II’s Hajis leave for Mecca/Jeddah on board ship owned/sailed by Arab Muhamad Wasir.
*   From Tegal.
*   Had already started from Jeddah.

---

Wat. to Eng. 8 Sept. 1806.
Karta Truna - Javanese from Banuwirna. Taken prisoner after breaking in the grave of Sultan Prabu Natamala at Giri. In July, a canary had eaten a flower placed on the grave. Lives behind, Dalim of Bp. Ponwirasentika of Banuwirna, brother-in-law of Jaksa. Macan Gembor/a pagan priest join him on having earlier robbed graves at Blongnong in Jipang and Tuban. Escapes from gaol in Banuwirna - recognized as mad, often given name as *R. Trunajaya*. Captured at Giri attempting to steal Pusaka Kris from grave. Priest in Tuban known as Macan Jura.

---

Eng. to Wat. 28 Oct. 1806.
*   Attack on and destruction of Dutch frigate *Maria Reigersbergen* by British men-of-war in Bay of Batavia.
*   Other ships captured at Onrust, Middelburg.
*   HB II informed of presence of Adm. Pellew’s squadron in Javanese waters and should prepare Yogya auxiliaries.

#### Notes on PB IV

PB IV [^p13s-1] is a "presto" — a good / Edelmoedig ruler. He is well-loved.

PB IV has a tenacious memory (stale memorie). He is a great friend of priests, who can get him to do anything.

Mulud, Phasa = great numbers of hajis, kannes, and pradikans come to Solo from all parts of Java.

Drove of them, espe. Bysone Garebeg Mulud. Which is not so in YN. / Yogya?

Each priest gets 6–8 ronde pilaar [^p13s-2] from PB IV; PB IV spends a great deal of money.

Extremely shy; always very courteous/polite to residents. PB IV likes to talk and reside at native tournaments, but Van den B. breaks it off. PB IV likes to put off decisions on things [^p13s-3].

PB IV is not ashamed to meet and talk to common Europeans. He gets large sums of money on loan from them: *New Barend Nammel lends 68,000 Silver Ronde Maarten*.

PB IV readily gives his word, but goes back on it just as easily on frivolous pretexts. He often feigns indisposition to get out of awkward invitations.

---

2 legal brothers of PB [Prince/Patih?]: Mangkubumi / v. proud / should be watched and kept under one’s thumb. 

PB does not trust brothers - [no] discovers affair with them. Often goes on town by moonlight with bros. [family]. But leaves all affairs of state to Patih and leaves bupatis a free hand until things get out of hand = then official brought low by NW [new] yet underhand means.

---

Mangkubumi = closet Chinese = nit on door of house. Capitan Cina often visits MB. Nie Ting Hoa [?] sits with villa on ground. Close contacts [with] Chinese community in Solo/Kraton.

---

#### Chinese Firework Displays [^p14s-1] v. popular.

MB only wearing short breeches sees display from bridge nr. fort and troop of young Jav. panakawans = [princes/leaders] of panakawan group.

On occasion of a New Year's party - drawn dagger on Mangku-praja = Patih sends off blow and copper dish pot. Npitoon [?]. Capitan Cina witnesses fireworks to panakawan. One firework hits MB who in cold blood hits Stats [?]. Panakawan and his burns him alive + gunpowder. Creates an uproar.

---

MB goes hunting at Nima = 8 hrs away from Solo or to Ho. at Bay alali without asking permission of Res. All members of Kraton who leave Solo have to ask permission of Res.

---

* [Marginal note]: Espec. no princes allowed to leave Kraton when Yogya MN [Mangkunegaran] Regent returning to MN Kalpasan [?] along old Kartasura Rd.

---

? TO PREVENT AFFRAYS [^p15s-1]

Yogya Res. warns when Mr. Bupatis returning to kabupaten from Yogya should not be over de troon bij de hand geleid [^p15s-2] [^p15s-3] only for PB IV and Crown Prince.

Buminata: a v. courteous/kind - discrete man.

[Crossed out text: Nuffis from eye sickness.]

PRANS WEDANA.

Nong Nong R.M. Naid + eldest sister PB IV = MNI / Praywidana. Ratu Aut.

Has 4,000 cacahs [^p15s-4] + tollgates from which he derives a considerable income.

* Never goes to Solo kraton except in company of Res. Independence of PWD from Solo kraton should be maintained as far as possible.

Only duties = has to help in repairs to mosque, private apartments of PB IV. Gets 2,000 N.g. [^p15s-5] for 2,500 road maintenance p.a. for region's upkeep.

Dalem of PWD also guarded by 12 dragoons + bodyguard of Resident.

* Patih Jayaningrat = because does not carry out PB IV orders to take away gold payings of PWD at Pascaan. Had died long since.

P. Prabuwinaya brother PWD who served in Solo kraton + chosen as successor of PWD. 27 Oct. 1803 when Eng. [^p15s-6] comes to Solo.

---

MN II + M. + J.P. PB IV legal: PWP tries to get one's daughter of PB IV in marriage + ingratiate himself in Solo court. Talks to N. Eng. about it in Semarang, Nov. 1803 = But Eng's view, PB IV seems to be prepared to allow marriage although D. only 13 years old + PWP already 40. But more than meets the eye to with nation.

But such a marriage would cause great difficulty for V.O.C.

But PWP not well-disposed to PB IV + Brother. Therefore should be given as little opportunity as possible to suit PB IV.

PWP heavily in debt + preparations for marriage. Should not be allowed to contract further debts. Much gambling / card playing at house of PWP. Would lead to financial ruin. Robbers / thieves in entourage of prince = get money for Nijverheim = also no gambling in Res. House = VdB never permits it.

PWP loves going now / then to Semarang. Goes 3/4 x during period of Van Reede + 1 x when VdB in Solo (1803-06). Adjutant Desentie / Ordonaans Pontia = [?] accompany PWP.

R. Panji Manguningrat sent by PWP to Batavia. Brother PWP [lives] on allowance of 1,200 Pond. Real p.a. PB IV pays 400 R.R. (200 at Hnue / 200 Phaon). Manguningrat had fled 3 x from Solo under Van [?]. Nijverheim = 1 x to Nima / 1 x to Jimangsnag / 1 x to Pipadhan. Tries to gather village people + arms to attack PWP. Therefore MN should not be allowed back from Batavia.

Crown Prince (P.B. IV)
*   A r. unsociable person.

Resented by other members of court.
Crown Prince at loggerheads & M.B. Beernink [^p17s-2]
*   M.B. makes no recognition of P.B. IV.
*   M.B. driven from court if P.B. IV becomes Sunan, goes away for M.B.

Gusti Timor (P.B. IV)
*   Sisters Ratu, also 2 daughters by Ratu.
*   Other children by unofficial wives = not important pol.
*   But have a r. high opinion of themselves = hardly deign to get out of way when Res. carriages drives past & only greet Res. a nod of head.

Danuningrat = not a good Patih = tries to put off all affairs.
*   Greatest friend & favourite of P.B. IV, not unintelligent.
*   A great friend/champion of priests.
*   "Fanatical man" goes to mosque every Friday, takes part in prayers, but drinks wine.

Onto Rys (R.T. Rakranagara) = Tries always to be on good terms & Res. likes to borrow large sums of money.

---

Van den B[oeck] tried to communicate directly with PB IV and not use Regents/Patih. Nijvenheim had conferred with B[oeck] every Monday/Thursday *dienst dagen* [work days]. 

Purbanegara lost his life; Cakrawinata lost his life; Van Reede [lost his life]. 

Purbanegara imprisoned without food/water in a bamboo cage on Paserpan and then banished to Wood of Ayah. 

Cakrawinata = captured/arrested in broad daylight in Nrimenganti after PB IV had conversed with him pleasantly until 12 a.m. the previous night and had given him new lands at his request. 

Banished to Panaraga and killed on the way by a Martalutut (executioner of PB IV). Danuningrat the Kliwon bowing at execution. 

Javanese & Solo = generally known as sly in character (*listig van karakter*). But in cordiality/refinement/friendly politeness, much greater in Solo than in Yogya.

1. Materialistic—like to give presents on understanding paid back 2x/3x. 
2. Rarely return money lent.

Both Cakrawinata & Mangkupraja = great Javanese politicians. But know too much, especially about V.O.C. [matters]. [They] tell PB IV that English [are] just as powerful as Dutch. [^p18s-1]

PB IV allows V.O.C. to fell teak in Blora. But Bp. Blora [is] not to sell wood to Chinese. Bp. allowed to continue to cut as much for own needs as for needs of Solo kraton.

Huge areas [in] Blora would not be filled in 1000 years. Even 1/4.

## 1806

**26 Oct. 1806:** Eng. to Wat. Disturbances in Cirebon still not suppressed.

**29 Oct. 1806:** Wat. to Eng. HB II [^p23s-1] to be asked to prepare auxiliary troops for V.O.C. to defend coast v. British squadron. But HB II could not be prevailed upon to take action unless in extreme emergency. HB II to be asked to guard borders of Cirebon and cut off correspondence of inhabitants of that Cirebon.

**29 Oct. 1806:** Eng. to Wat.

**3 Nov. 1806:** Wat. to Eng. Hear from Eng. 29 Oct. that British squadron returned Dutch prizes (ships) to Pulo Pinang.

**2 Dec. 1806:** Eng. to Wat. Reappearance of Adm. Pellew's (8 ships) squadron in Bay of Batavia.

**5 Dec. 1806:** Wat. to Eng. Wat. promises to ask HB II to prepare auxiliary troops.

## 1806-7

*Wataloo to van den Berg, 8 Feb. 1806*: Homage/compliments of Both Patih to A.H. Wiese. Earlier 27 Mar. 1802 Homage paid to J. Niberg. R.T. Natadiningrat, [Jr.] T. Natadiningrat to accompany Patih [M]BII Secretary Ng. Prawiranastra. Solo Regents to ride out before those of Yogya.

*Id. to Id., 29 Jul. 1806*: PB IV Recipes Name of Sons as Princes:
- R.M. Huncin = P. Aria Ngabehi
- R.M. Andani = P. Kusumajaya
- R.M. Ternadi = P. Natabrata

Ngabehi in month of Rabingulakir to marry P. PB IV’s sister, Ratu Purbanegara.

*Id. to Id., 13 Sept. 1806*: MB II not keen on V.O.C. measuring up his lands.

*Id. to von Liebeherr, 29 Sept. 1806*: MB II appoints 2nd Regent in Roano:
- R.T. Pringgadiwiria, 1st Reg.
- Kyai T. Manghadiwirio, 2nd Reg.
- Carik Kliwon Manghajaya.

*Id. to Id., 12 Nov. 1806*: MN Regents returning home from Yogya.

*Wataloo to F.J. Boumeyer, 11 Jan. 1807*: 11 Jan. arrival of 10 Europeans in a boat in Bay of Pacitan. Ship had gone down. Capt/Lt. Ntersman, born in America. 3 sailors: 1 from Würtemberg, 1 from Copenhagen, 1 from Amsterdam, 1 from Berlin. Ship's cargo: Brandy, Jenever, Cheese, Butter, Large mirrors, Fine glasses.

*Wataloo to von Liebeherr, 18 Feb. 1807*: Running from Solo: Mas Pengulu, Mas Kitis Anom & Kitis Arum looking for [?] Solo P. Natapura Yogya prince. [P. Above Aruling] [? Studying] Pengulu in Yogya. In order to act as a witness for marriage of R.Aj. Aliya, d. of Solo P. Natadiningrat to marry R.Ng. Wirayuda, son of R. Ria Natapada.

## Translation of the Gold Farm letter given by H.H. Sultan Hamengkubuwana II, residing at Yogyakarta Adiningrat on the island of Java

I, Sultan Hamengkubuwana etc., give this letter under my seal to my subject Reksanegara as a proof (evidence) to all my subjects both Bupatis and Manttis who come under my rule in Yogyakarta Adiningrat as well as those in the mancanagara provinces, to make known that the reason why I have accorded this letter to Reksanegara is because I have once again favoured him and hereby permitted him to take the farms of all domains such as bandars or tollgates, rangkahs, salarangs, the gedhong tembakau[^p29s-1] in Kedu, the slaughtering of buffaloes, the salterns (salt works) on the south coast and the birds' nest rocks; everything which is under my administration in Yogyakarta Adiningrat for an amount of 60,000 ronde realen silver per annum and in case payment be made in copper duits, the same shall be acceptable at the rate of 1 real for 20 dubbeltjes of 10 duits each.

The tax farm money must be paid in two installments each year; half or 30,000 r.r. at the festival of Mulud and the other half, also 30,000, at the feast of Puasa. Under this tax farm are not to be included my pasars and rangkahs withing the capital (Yogya) as well as those in the vicinity, these being also excepted such as Tanjung Tirto, Tegalsari (with its tollgate) and the so-called pasar perdikan which belong to the priests as well as my pasars and further the bandars in the mancanagara provinces as well as the tollgates along the great river (Bengawana Sala) and the birds nest rocks in those areas be also excepted from the same---- furthermore if the tobacco harvest has not been placed in the warehouses and the time of this farm be expired, this article will be considered to relate to this and not to the subsequent farm (similarly with the three collections of birds nests each year) -- the farm of the birds nests is worth 12,000 r.r. for each half year, but this is to be separate and not to be reckoned under the previous tax returns, and it is also stipulated that I shall be allowed to send down for my own use 110 sapi pengluput or beasts of burden three times each year without having to pay tolls or other taxes on passing through tollgates, rangkahs and salarangs.

On each occasion that the farm moneys are paid, the pangirings or presents must be: pathoolen (kains); (see n. of Rouffaer), Gold thread, silk, tea, candy, sugar and Chinese sweetmeats (confituuren), candied fruit; consisting of 10 pairs Pathoolen jelamprang, each pr. worth 25 r.r.; 10 pathoolen kanigara, each pr. worth 15 r.r.; if I want pathoolen kenanga people must produce them for me at the same price as the pathoolen jelamprang and kanigara; 10 pounds of Gold thread each of 12 threads and 18 heavy threads; worth 24 reals; 20 catties of silk, of which 9 catties of green and 9 of red and 2 of rose (merah jambu) or light red silk, the cattie I fix at 8 reals worth. These together are worth the sum of 1230 r.r. under which is reckoned the sum of 430 r.r. per half year for the payment of candy sugar, tea and candied fruit (confituuren); the patompos money is reckoned at 10 per 1000 (1 % of the installment), 30 r.r. for the purchase of the serat papak and 31 r.r. for the panglamar or accountant (goldsmith) or Sultan's goldsmith.

If there are some of my subjects who pass tolls carrying loads (pikulans) which do not contain merchandise (negotiable goods), I permit them to pass without paying a toll -- if there are any of my family, children or great or small priyayis who possess ricefields and they transport the padi or rice to Yogya either by coolies or on horseback or on other beasts of burden they are also to be allowed to pass through (without paying tolls) -- whenever one of my subjects at the time of the Garebeg feast journeys here or even outside the time of the Garebeg and they carry loads with negotiable goods (merchandise) I do not allow that tolls should be raised on them but if the load is asked to be inspected at the tollgate this must be allowed -- and if they are found to carry merchandise they must pay the due toll for it, but they must pass through freely if they have no merchandise; people should make a distinction between merchandise and non-merchandise and I forbid that tolls be raised on small quantities of kains and tobacco which are being carried as presents for their lords apanage holders. In the event that a tollgate keeper notwithstanding my order raises tolls on any of my subjects who do not carry merchandise and an argument ensues, he shall be punished by me after an investigation.

- 2 -

investigation of the case and if it is a Javanese who passes the toll with merchandise and he hides it in order to pay no toll or instead of going through the tollgate takes another road and after due investigation this is found to be the case, he shall also be punished by me -- and Reksanegara shall retain his 5 Mantris, who shall do work for him when there are issues (historiaalen) concerning the tollgates or when I order him to carry out things and at each Garebeg ceremony Reksanegara must march out as many prajurits armed as an outer Bupati is obliged to do -- and Reksanegara shall keep the tax farm for the period of three consecutive years which I have fixed as long as all goes well but if he behaves badly or does not produce the tax properly he shall be dismissed even though the 3 year tax term is not yet up

By order of H.H. Wed. 1 Sawal Je A.J. 1734 (2 Dec. 1807).
H 6986

**23 Mar 1807**
**Wk. Eng**

HB II [has] rent [and] Pajang does not want VOC money. [It is] ridiculed by Pangéran. 
> "100 Rijksdaalders each month borrowed by children [at] 2 1/2 % per month."

[HB II]

"Lost 25 years [having] lost much of its authority in Java, and the longer [we wait], the less authority we shall have" because [of the] increasing population in Java. 

> "War [is] triggered from outside - weakness inside: 'Oorlog van buiten - zwakheid van binnen.' En het alleen de heer die over heerst!" [^p37s-1]

Jumadiningsrat [is] a completely broken reed. Purwadipwa "een zieke schoolje" [^p37s-2]. Now, Prayu [has the] most important role.

"And neither DR II or anyone else can say anything against it."

Makes comparison to Frederick the Great. [Only 40,000 men (good European boys)]

## 1807–8
*Dj. Brieven no. 38, Zaar Yogyakarta 1803–1808*

*   **25 Aug. 1807 (Wat. to von Liebeherr):** Marriage of PB III’s legal daughter, R. Ay. Natariah, to son of P. Mangkubumi, R.M. Aria Natakusuma.
*   **28 Aug. 1807 (ID. to Id.):** Death of R. Ayu Natadiningrat after a long illness. [^p39s-1]
*   **27 Oct. 1807 (ID. to Id.):** 7 followers of Yogya pass through Cilacap and go to Piegad [Pecang?] to inhabitants v. pirates.
*   **10 Dec. 1807 (Wat. to P II):** Great increase in robberies, even heads of districts placing themselves as heads of robber bands. [^p39s-2]
*   **3 Dec. 1807 (Wat. to Eng.):** Robberies in Semarang by Yogya/Pala inhabitants, especially from Asam/Kartasura. [^p39s-3]

#### Villages centres of Robbers
*   **Semarang:** Village near Kartasura Asam & Yogya & Pala—home of many robbers.
*   **Plawarang (Pala):** Belonging to P. Adinegara. Demang Potrayudat, inhabitants robbers.
*   **Janti (Pala):** Belonging to P. Adinegara.
*   **Brenopang (Pala):** [Belonging to P. Adinegara.] [^p39s-4]
*   **Kolangin (Kartasura):** Demang Truna.
*   **Joak nr. Klica (Pala):** P. Adinegara; Lurah Angawijaya.
*   **Bangak (Yogya):** Lurah Wanatruna.
*   **Kali Niruk nr. Asem (Pala):** P. Adinegara.
*   **Sangir nr. Pala/Kartasura:** P. Prangwedana; son of Demang Pincasewiria, head of robbers.
*   **Puki Combang Lampir (Yogya) nr. Asem:** Near Lobong Palam, heavily populated large village. Demang Coplagi, Reknadirja, Kertadiwangsa.
*   **Gagah Nipat nr. Kartasura:** Mertayuda/Ningadrawa.
*   **Gabang Bangak (Yogya):** Close to Banyudana-Asem. Demang Kertalihsana.
*   **Jan Angerin (Yogya):** On foothills of Merbabu, heavily populated village; all villages, even priest, robbers. Demang of Pisanggrahan; Ngedo, Konco Ninia, Trunadilaga, under P. Prangwedana.

---

Dukuh, Nikar Lampin (Yogya) area of R.T. Prawiranata, Japa legi, Ridawijaya, Kentayuda Demangs.

**17 Dec. 1807:** Wat. to von Liebeherr. Rumour of British attack on Gresik. Attempt to destroy rest of ships in Oosthoek to cut off connections along Solo River & Gresik. [^p40s-1]

**24 Dec. 1807:** Id. to Id. British withdraw squadron from Java. [^p40s-2]

**25 Jan. 1808:** MB II to PB IV. Aunt of PB IV d. of P. Hangkupraja of Demak died. Steeking in side of chest & heavy cough. Pneumonia/T.B.

**5 Feb. 1808:** Wat. to von Liebeherr. Considerable stealing of buffaloes in Tuban & Blandhong Trambalang.

**12 Feb. 1808:** Id. to Id. Investigations to be sent to Blora/Panolan.

**1 Mar. 1808:** W. Schraag + Lt. Petel (in command at Klaten) to Waterloo. Extensive earthquake damages lost at Klaten. 28 Feb. 1808 early in morning. [^p40s-3]

**3 Mar. 1808:** Wat. to von Liebeherr. PB IV's favourite & brother-in-law Cakrakusuma appointed as Prince & placed in charge of troops. Nong recently deceased Tum. of Panaraga (Wiryadiningrat) to succeed Java. [^p40s-4]

**4 Apr. 1808:** Id. to J.A. van Braam + von Liebeherr. J.A. van Braam taking over from von Liebeherr. Departing Resident (Java) ceremony on 4 Apr. 1808.

**31 May 1808:** Relaas of Ranawijaya (Mataram). From village of Bunder (Surabaya). 250 bandits attack Desa of Bunder on 25 May. Armed with pikes, clubs, & torches. Plunder Chinese houses, burn of them. Retreat into forest of Kertasana.

Burning of Chinese kongsi (tax office): 200 Np.P. / 3 pieces white mori / 5 Surat clothes / 1 gold stomach plate / ginger / benzoin / 4 pieces bar iron / 2 copper pots / 5 packets of kapas / 1 sirih box.

Apart from Ratu Kencana Wulan, no-one at court has real trust of HB II; clearly the most favoured person at the court.

R.T. Natadiningrat - no. 12 law of HB II also v. highly favoured.
R.T. Purwadipura - also often called close to HB II, knows HB II's character v. well & has most say in financial matters. A very ordinary trader known how to give advice most pleasing to HB II.

But like NP, has to pay fines; not so long ago has to pay 1000 R. Reals because without permission HB II had used a certain gamelan.

No one really a favourite or someone who has HB II's full trust. No-one in Yogya kraton really trusted by HB II, still less a favourite. Not even HB II's brother/nephews really has his trust. Not even Crown Prince.

Waterloo - 9 years in Yogya - occasion to really get to know characters at court.
Crown Prince = always does much [for] / makes much of second wife / d. of R.T. Natadiningrat.
HB II more in favour of J. Rongga.
No declared 1st wife on accession to throne in 1792.

#### Memorie of Wat. to P. Res. 4 Apr. 1808

**Outstanding affairs:**
*   Murdering Chinese Nie Jan by Reg. of Negaran Kyai J. Purwadiningrat.
*   Patih tries to push aside case by money fine.
*   Continual difference between govmt. inhabitants in Rembang/Tuban = Blora/Jipang/Padolan.
*   Recent large theft of buffaloes.
*   Also theft of buffaloes in Kaliwungu.
*   Robberies committed by people of Jetis/Bulu in district Satrian - Prov. Kedu Jangaang.

**Building of new magazine/hospital in fort**
*   Need for complete renewal of infantry barracks.
*   Much more has to be done to fort at Klaten:
    1. Wall/ditch around outer logie (outer defense).
    2. Building of Eur. Kampong.
    3. New vegetable garden for fort and penanggrahan.
    4. Placing of gladhak of Tazkinan.

**MN Labour Force work 90 days in Yogya:**
*   On fort/kraton - But HB II expects more than they can perform.
*   Pacitan too far from Yogya.

**Loano =**
*   Area of Tanjung belonging to Solo entirely placed under control of Solo 1st Res. [^p44s-1]
*   Yogya area under 2 Yogya Regents.
*   Report of Waterloo 8 Feb. 1804 on pepper in Yogya.
*   On decline in pepper plantation in Loano. [^p44s-2]

**Attempt to raise coffee culture in Mataram**
*   Test at Klaten [^p44s-3]
*   Merapi/Merbabu = area suitable for coffee plantations.

## "Gold Farm Letter"

*See AvJ*
*“Gold Farm Letter” 2 pp. 1807*
*Wed 1 Sawal, 1734 / 2 Dec. 1807*

An 'Yogya Brieven' 20.86.

**Gold Farm Letter.**

Renting out of Bandhar / Tolgates / Rangkas / Pelarongs / Gidhong Tebak in Kedu to Rexanegara for 60,000 Np.D. in silver money. For 3 years. (1 Np.D = 28 dubbeltjes).

*   Papan - 30,000
*   Muleo - 30,000

Exceptions: Tanjung Tirta / Tegalpari / Tolgates in Papan / Bandhar / Tolgates on Solo river. Also: Pasar Perditan belonging to Priest.

Farm rent of birds' nest harvest: 12,000 Np.D. per ½ year.

2x per year gathering of Bird's Nest.

No Panghupat Napi belonging to HBII can go through Tolgates without paying toll.

Pangiring / presents to HBII:
*   Pathoolen / gold thread / silk / tea / candy / sugar / Chinese confiture
*   10 pos Pathoolen, Pelamprang, each 25 Np.D.
*   10 pos Kamigara, each 15 Np.D.
*   + Pathoolen kencana

1,230 Np.D. worth of 10 ponds gold thread & 12 Pypen + heavy thread. (18 Reals per catie).

20 kaves silk (9 green / 9 red / 2 sambu (pink)). 8 Reals per catie.

Candy / sugar / confiture: 430 reals per ½ year.

*   Patompono: 10 per 1,000 of chest of surat
*   Papak: 60 piculen
*   Penglaman: 30 piculen

If inhabitants pass Tolgates not carrying goods for market - no tax can be levied on them.

All members of HBII's family exempt from tolls, i.e., bringing in rice from lands.
If Bupatis & others coming to Yogya for Garibeg to pay taxes, but have no negotiable goods, they can pass tollgates without charge, but Pikuls should be examined by Bandhar.

Difference Negotiable / Non-Negotiable Quantities:
i.e. small amount of cloth / tobacco as presents for Lords. No toll to be levied. If toll attempted to be "unjustly," Bandhar would be punished.

Every Garibeg = Reksanegara to produce as many prajurits for parades as on a Regent. If RN does not pay tax on time, would be dismissed.

Payment to HBII for Bandhar: 60,000 p.a.

*   + 800: Pangiring / Homage money, Farm goods.
*   1,500: Homage money.
*   + Tea / Sugar / Confiture for CP/PRE/HBII.
*   600: Razombo money.
*   1% of Tax money from Bandhar.
*   Taken by HBII.
*   60: Panglamar money - 1/10% to Royal silver smith who assays money paid for Tax of Bandhar Farm.
*   120: Pelthuk money - 1/5% to Nce of HBII for Receipt for Tax Farm payment.
*   200: Wang Cap (Seal money) 1% paid to HBII for Sealing of Gold Farm letter.
*   3 years = 600 Np.D.
*   50: Nangn. gratuity / Soncur money.
*   For Gandiks of HBII bringing in Gold Farm letter = 150 Np.D. paid at once for 3 years.
*   616 2/3: Wang Bikti on payment of Tax.
*   Homage money = 2,000 Np.D. for 3 years.

Solo Brieven no. 55.
B.F. von Liebscherr to N. Engelhard, 15 Jan. 1806
1806 - Solo Br. 55.
New Year celebrations pass off without mishap.
Marked cooling in relationships P. Mangkubumi - CP (See earlier van den Berg). Has been restored somewhat.

Celebration of raising of Louis Napoleon to throne of Holland as a sign of special favour: 6 Jul. 1807.
Special lunch in Residency.
PB IV [^p49s-4] + P. Mangkubumi + Brothers-in-law.
R. Adip. Danuningrat. P. Cakrakusuma.
Letting off hot air balloons.
Dances: Pawireng (War, Fighting Dance) or Royal Dance. + especially beautiful gamelan music.
Done at house of Solo Resident since time of Res. Palm. But sometimes in kraton on special occasions.
PB IV gives Liebscherr a gold tobacco box.
Nirimpi dancing in morning.
12.00 Banquet. Semi-circle of tables in centre.
Mock Greek temple constructed by Ir. Cornelius.
+ Statues of Bacchus / Head of Appis / Minerva / Juno.
Bas-Reliefs.
+ Burning flame in temple.
Hot air balloon released, rises 200' before going to N.E.

- **22 Jun. 1808:** Secret Instruction for Lt. Col. Reinking & 2e Res. — To be in charge of Residency during absence of Res. van Braam.

- **21 Sept. 1806:** Waterloo to von Liebeherr — Impossible for 3rd Instructor of Marine School at Semarang, Lt. Kortzius & Bombardier Cadet du Bois to survey coast from Meulengat [to] Pautan because [of] rocks. To continue survey of Nuzany Sultan's lands.

- **1 Nov. 1806:** von Liebeherr to Engelhard — Need for Government economies in minting coins. 200,000 Rijksthalers less would be minted in coming year. To be covered by paper/copper money. Economies necessary to avoid confusion in finances. But impossible to economize expenses in Solo.

- **[Marginal Note:]** Res in Solo also takes over tax farming Demands.
- **[Body Text:]** Heavy debts & interest of von Liebeherr in Solo. Nutmegs lost through European war. Res only paid 15,000 in paper p.a. He has to get by, by taking over Tax Farm of Nuzany Domains. But heavy costs of administration — only makes a profit out of Bird's Nest. All payments to [illegible] have to take place in silver money. No longer able to make a profit on Opium monopoly — Chinese/Javanese too poor to buy opium.

- **3 Dec. 1806:** [Res] to [Res] — V.O.C. must pay Strangeld payments in order to keep up garrison in Solo. Best party at ready in case [illegible] honour at court. Needs to take actions to prevent disturbances.

- **[Marginal Note:]** Pellen's squadron
- **[Body Text:]** Appearance of Brit. warships in Bay of Batavia. Burning/destroying Dutch shipping on Pasinir. PWPJ Nuzany warned to stand by to give help to Dutch if necessary. Would not march 1/2 of Solo garrison to Semarang unless receives direct order from Eng.

- **7 Dec. 1806:** [Res] to [Res] — PB IV asks whether setting up of civil guard in Solo composed of leading citizens not [to] defend [illegible] at this time.

- **5 Jan. 1807:** [Res] to [Res] — PPN; Crown Prince. [illegible] Kraton/Haji.
- **15 Jan. 1808:** [Res] Eng. to von L. — Tries to repair cooled friendship. Rest of Royal Family to Residency House for N. Year's Day celebration. Between us — P. Mang Kubumi.

## N. Engelhard

"Speculatieve Memorie van N. Engelhard [^p52s-1]
over zaken betreffende het Bestuur over Java's N.O. kust" 1808. 14 May 1808.

- Bylage on Relationship & courts.
- Yogya Siyanti war costs V.O.C. f. 4, 286, 000 [^p52s-2]

DR II. [^p52s-3] Refusal of HB II to allow DR II to accept het g. appoint. from Semarang in 1799. Because of mention of words *Leen/Leenheer*. Only permits it in 1802 when Eng. visits courts.

New Regents of land cannot be made at time of appt. of HB II because of objections of HB II. Much new land opened up since 1774. Trawas drained.

Much new land opened up in areas bordering N.E. Coast. Espc. Grobogan, Lamongan & Joana Border.

Idea of drawing new boundary between Pasisir/VL. on occasion of change of ruler & get new rich lands for govmt. area. (cf. DAENDELS = 1810 TREATY).

& also suggests Annexation of Pacitan / Tangong/Loano. Chap. IV & Malang which PB IV claims.

Better arrangements for forts & get rules to surrender lands completely on which Res./Fort. Enr./Chin. kampongs built. Coolies provided by courts to be increased to 250/300. 40 coolies for upkeep of fort.

---

+ upkeep of water canals.

*   Once sufficient, the force got together to overawe courts. Policy of annexation to be attempted.
*   Bps [Bupatis] who were not relations of rulers could be kept on. Ending of rent of 10,000 N.p.D. for *strandgeld* should be considered.
*   Get lands/desas around Boyolali, Klaten, Salatiga.

#### STRANDGELD:
Originally as indemnity for tollgates / *Shahbandars*.
(Rice production, Teak wood)

*   2,000 Rijksthalers for PB II [Pakubuwana II]
*   1,000 for Ratu Amangkurat
*   2,000 for 2 Patih [chief ministers]
*   1,000 for Nayakas [officials]
*   2,000 for Crown Prince

[Marginal Note: Nov Namarang, Rembang, Surabaya, Japara & other districts]
[Marginal Note: Bps supposed to pay respects at court at Mulo / Phesa but sales into disuetude.]

Later 5,000 R.d. [Rijksthalers] for *Shahbandars*.
Agreed in contract = 18 May 1746.
1755 10,000 N.p.D. Yogya / 10,000 - Solo.

1755 Giyanti Treaty = HB I [Hamengkubuwana I] waives claims to Madura pasir.

Income of PB IV p.a. = 44,016 N.p.D.
Besides *padjutan dalem* / penaring money 10,000 - 12,000 N.p.D.
HB II gets 60,000 N.p.D. + presents etc.

Van Alphen - Engelhard
no. 235

* Tollgates in past 20 years - v. corrupt Admin. Need reform.

Since 1755 HB I waives claims to Paninir. RB II cannot really get Strangel.

* CHINESE
no. 9 male Chinese over 14 years old in VH = 2,040. Solo = 1,282. Yogya = 758. [^p54s-1]

Gradual coming together of Solo, Yogya and understanding between various Solo-Yogya courtiers.

Eng. does not agree v. Van Reede/Van Overstraten that Yogya/Solo jealousy so deep that could never make common cause v. Europeans.

Eng gets to know PBN well and informed by Von Liebherr that although PD IV outwardly friendly - in fact a v. cruel/revengeful character. V. suspicious / master of disguise / sly tricks.

PD IV puts up/eggs MN I on against HB I in 1790.

Extract of letter Van Overstraten to W.A. Alting, 14 Mar. 1795. Idea of PD IV to marry daughter of R.A. Numa to Yogya Crown Prince. But prevented by Van Ov.

Young war. Found behind chest in Solo court. Suspected of having affair with R.A. Numa sister of PB IV. Cruelty of PD IV for preface: killed by PD IV / brothers = MB wants to eat heart and genitals of young man. Contents burned and raw ear = which he swallowed and indulged pleasure. [^p54s-2]

---

PB IV pretends to pardon sister—R.A. Nama only 16 years. But tries to poison her 7 times—fails because of strength of her constitution.

Then goes at night to her bedroom and strangles her with his own hands.

PB IV disguises barbarism under guise of friendliness.

PB IV tries to poison N.V. Venium (1796-1803) by giving him jam prepared by own hands.

Cakrawinata dismissed/arrested after being entertained by PB IV = wife/children clothes in white ordered to take leave of him and then kept in kraton.

Kept in prison until 1:30 p.m. then went to Lodhaya murdered on way. All goods and chests with money confiscated/taken to kraton.

Mangkuraja = only competent Patih which Java court has dismissed/killed because of suspicion of connections and Prangwedana.

PB IV listens to complaints of MB.

Sent to Banyumas = rendered by Nwanegara and wife/children.

Unlike PB II, PB IV does not have finances in poor state/many debts.

In 1807/1808 PB IV raises forced tax of 100,000 r.p.d. from subjects to help pay off debts. But creditors get very little/most kept by PB IV.

PB IV IHB II asks for Persian horses/elephant but cannot be imported because [of] Brit. blockade. [^p59s-1]

? Elephant could be got from Lampong Districts of Sumatra.

*Rulers often ask for none sheets of gold Surat paper for writing* (eenige vellen Surat papier tot het schrijven van brieven) *should be got from India as soon as possible.*

Pepper cultivation in poor state.

100 cacahs set aside for indigo factory in Loano to be returned for pepper plantations since indigo does not grow well. Never more than 2-3 pikuls produced because Jav. farmers averse to planting it + preparation of indigo [is] time consuming + difficult.

Huge increase in rice production in VL [^p59s-2] over past 25 years. New lands brought into cultivation. Eholia forests + high mountain reaches. Each year increase in cultivation.

Increase in tollgates/pop. + greater consumption [of] rice. Prices rise. Pop. have more needs than earlier 25/30 years ago. Increase in taxes / payments to local heads. Owa padi fields not as fertile as previously because used continuously, not allowed to lie fallow + no fertilizer held as in Europe.

5. Van Alphen - Engelhard no. 235.

Other crops also increasing. Farmer / Trader would be getting more wealthy if taxes not increasing so fast.

Increase in pepper coffee cultivation (especially in Kedu). But farmers do not like working in coffee estates.

Coffee/pepper delivered / traded to N. coast paid for = but money saved by farmers.

People in Nagaran/ Salatiga/ Bayalati = most suitable areas for coffee, should be encouraged to plant the crop. Commandants of forts should give advances for coffee crop.

If one ruler falls ill / kraton should be occupied by Res. until next ruler apptd.

Prophets / Tapas should be bought out by V.O.C. to make prophecies favourable to fortunes of Dutch.

## Daendels in Java (26 May 1808 – 22 Jan 1809)

* **26 May 1808:** Regulations, Houtbonchen in Java (1895), pp. 766–767.
    * Trop. Geng. Konst. Carel von Wolzogen.
    * Pres. Admin. Houtbonchen = J. Knops.
    * Member Res. Yogya = Waterloo.
    * Tskral Semarang = N. Minnat.

* **21 Aug 1808:** Vol. XV (1896), pp. 116–143, on Houtbonchen.

* **19 Jun 1808:** Vol. XIV, p. 836, on Indonesian Troops used by Daendels.
    * **Strengthening Garrisons (Central Java):**
        * Buginese / Balinese in Semarang.
        * Ambonese to be garrisoned in Yogya.
        * 2/3 Regt. Infantry – 2 Comps. Grenadiers – party recruited from Ambonese.
        * Garrison corps / Klaten.
        * 1,000 Buginese to be dispatched from Makasar.
        * 750 Balinese recruits + purchased Balinese slaves by Engelhard via Rothenbühler – gezaghebber Oosthoek.
        * 1,500 men from Groote Oost.
        * Slaves from Makasar / Bali.

on Ridderorden(1808)

26 Jun. 1808
Ridderorde
van het
koninkrijk
der
Nederlanden
from
SIMON SCHAMA
Director of Studies in History

CHRIST'S COLLEGE
CAMBRIDGE
CB2 3BU
Cambridge 65454

March 22nd 1976

Dear Peter,

Thanks very much for the felicitations. As you know I really didn't expect to get the job so it all came as an extraordinary surprise. I'm hugely looking forward to coming to Oxford even though I'm fully briefed as to the agonies as well as the ecstasies. I hear from Richard that congrats are in order for you too for getting that plum award from the BA to go East. Lucky s.o.b. — I'm green with envy at all that lovely research time. Roll on the first sabbatical.

I do feel terrible about that balls-up I made in Cambridge — how can you ever forgive me. I was so discombobulated what with the job news and the sudden and rather dramatic crisis it had plunged Christ's history into that everything else got swept clean away — milles salaams. That being so I wish I could be more helpful about your Orde problem. But I'm not sure I can. Louis did indeed create a Dutch "nobility" on Feb. 14th 1807 but it was called the Orde van de Unie not, as far as I know van de Nederlandse Leeuw. On the other hand the latter, as a Lodewijkian creation does ring a faint and tiny carillon at the far recesses of my memory but I'm damned if I can find any ref. to it. Have you tried the relevant Colenbrander narrative or the Gedenkstukken for Feb 1807 or the NNJBk, or the absurd Labarre de Raillicourt which is very strong on flummery.

Now, as for the Orde van de Unie, my Almanac de Cour for 1809 has it divided into three orders, the Grand Croix which goes to men of Ministerraad rank or Rd van Staat as well as to top foreign ministers (Mollien), princes of Empire, allies etc. Below that comes Commanders including other Raden van Staat (Appelius, Calkoen) as well as high nobs and notables like Anthony Hope, and Hodshon the Amsterdam banker-schepen; Pieter van de Kasteele and others of that ilk. The Commanders including people like Gerrit Pijman and Robert Voute seem to me to be partly comprised of a list drawn up by Gogel and another by Mollerus in 1807, but the King was constantly asking for nominees, meeting them and making up his own mind. Third comes the Ridderschap (Chevaliers) of which in 1809 I make there to be roughly some 7-800! Nearly all native Dutch and obviously (and erroneously) including anyone who could be thought to be a friend of the regime or a man worthy of note or open to persuasion by flattery — thus it includes a Bentinck van Diepenheim (how optimistic can you get) as well as Johannes Busch, Anton Falck, Carel Hultman, Reuvens the jurist — virtually all the high elite of the old nobility, Torck van Rosendaels; a van Schuylenburg van Bommende, the van Hasselets and so on. No Indonesian potentates though. It seems likely that the Ridderschap got made up on the same basis as the Napoleonic amalgame — of restoring the trad. nobility and supplementing them with those whom the King was tied to honour. More than that I can't say. Commanders and Grand Crossers had the right to nominate I think so perhaps an award of another Orde was within Daendels's purlieu — he was a holder of the Grand Cross of course along with Gogel and Schimmelpenninck. But it's odd that in the official Almanac there shouldn't be a mention of the Leeuw, it's not. I should perhaps add that none of the imperial gazettes will be much good since N. refused to acknowledge that Louis had any right to create or reaffirm such ranks and a condition of the limited partition of March-April 1810 was that it was abolished.

Sorry I can't be more helpful. Have you tried writing the Ilde Afdeeling or even the KB?

See you soon I hope — I'm off to the States for a bit,

Eve.

Simon
## 1808, 27 May - 28 Jun.
**AN 'Yogya Brieven' 20.41**

PB IV to Daendels, 27 May 1808, 1 Rabingulakir 1735. Telling Daendels that P. Mangkubumi [^p72s-1] and P. Aria Cakrakusuma [^p72s-2] being next to Semarang to meet Daendels to present PB IV’s compliments to "Grandfather". To depart on nat. 28 May + J.A. van Braam. Asks P’s pardon if messengers do anything against local custom.

#### Messenger PB IV -> P

HB II to Daendels, 26 Rabingulawal, Dal, 1735 (23 May 1808). Receives letter of Daendels stating arrival in Semarang 12 May. Daendels [^p72s-3] in person + Pijnacker N. Engelhard from Govmt. of N.E. Coast. King Louis’ friendship to HB II / treat Javanese as his own children + to prevent English from capturing Inland. 

Daendels promises that even if English have secret friends in Java, would be able to prevent/defeat all attempts. Daendels concentrates troops on Semarang to be able to prevent an attack on any quarter. Fortifies Semarang. 3-4,000 troops reinforcement at Dutch at Semarang to prevent English landing. Asks HB II not to attack Yogya. Asks HB II to send nobles to Semarang to witness measures P taking for defense of Java + sentiments of P towards Javanese.

20 Aug. Daendels would be ready to receive embassy from Yogya to present compliments to incoming G.G.

**BUYSKES** [^p72s-4]
A.A. Buyskes sent by King Louis to take Daendels’ place as G.G. in case could not get through to Java. Now appointed Lt. G.G. in Batavia.

HB II promises to exercise troops in Yogya so that could come to P’s [^p72s-5] assistance at all speed. Messengers from HB II to P to depart on 25 Jumadilakir (22.8.1808).

---

PB IV to Daendels, 27 Rabingulawal, 24 May 1808. P writes from Semarang 16 May 1808, arrives in [Semarang] 12 May = did mindes [sic] Eng. Name as letter of MB II above. Appointment of Mangkubumi Cakrakusuma as ambassador to Semarang (cf. MB II appoints no ambassador). To present *Hulde* (homage/tribute) to [the Governor General] on Sat. 2 Rabingulakir, 28 May 1808.

Id. to Id., Mon. 18 Rabingulakir, 13 Jun. 1808. Receives P’s letter from Semarang, 7 Jun. 1808, telling that Mangkubumi Cakrakusuma, ambassador, arrived in good state & being [greeted] by Daendels 31 May 1808 together with PB IV’s youngest son Gusti Timor. Meeting had been marked by friendship & respect. Return on Thursday 14 Rabingulakir (9 Jun. 1808) to Sala.

Id. to Id., Sun. 2 Jumadilawal, 1735 (26 Jun. 1808). Receives P’s letter from Semarang 17 Jun. 1808, telling him of abolition of post of Gov. of Java’s N.E. Coast. [The] Opperhoofden at courts to correspond directly to P in Batavia. PB IV prepared to regard them as "ministers" of King Louis—directly representing Daendels. Have a much higher rank now than earlier when subordinate to Gov. of Java’s N.E. Coast. J.A. van Braam given *Ridderorde van het Koningrijk der Nederlanden* [by] King Louis [creation].

MB II to Daendels, Tues. 4 Jumadilawal, 28 Jun. 1808. Receives letter of P 17 Jun. 1808 about changes in N.E. Coast admin. Opperhoofden at courts to be recognized as ministers. MB II prepared to do so.

27 May - 28 Jun.
AN 'Yogya Brieven' 20.41

*   **PB IV to Daendels, 27 May 1808 (1 Rabingulakir 1735):** Telling Daendels that P. Mangkubumi [^p74s-1] and P. Aria Cakrakusuma [^p74s-2] are being sent to Semarang to meet Daendels to present PB IV’s compliments to "grandfather." To depart on Nat. 28 May + J.A. van Braam. Asks P's pardon if messengers do anything against Adat custom.

*   **HB II to Daendels, 26 Rabingulawal, Dal, 1735 (23 May 1808):** Messengers PB IV -> P. Receives letter of Daendels stating arrival in Semarang 12 May + Daendels' envoy in person (N. Engelhard from Govrnt. of N.E. Coast). King Louis' friendship to HB II / treat Javanese as his own children. To prevent English from capturing inland. Daendels promises that even if English have secret friends in Java, would be able to prevent/defeat all attempts.

*   **Daendels Fortifies Semarang:** Concentrates troops at Semarang to be able to prevent an attack on any quarter. 3-4,000 troops, reinforcement of Dutch at Semarang to prevent English landing. None Yogya not to attack Yogya. Asks HB II to send nobles to Semarang to witness measures P taking for defense of Java + sentiments of P towards Javanese.

*   **20 Aug.:** Daendels would be ready to receive embassy from Yogya to present compliments to incoming G.G.

*   **BUYSKES:** Rear-Adm. A.A. Buyskes sent by King Louis to take Daendels' place as G.G. in case could not get through to Java. Now appointed Lt. G.G. in Batavia.

*   **HB II promises** to exercise troops in Yogya so that could come to P's assistance at all speed. Messengers from HB II to P to depart on 25 Jumadilakir (22.8. of 1808).

16 May - 1808
AN "Yogya Brieven" 20.86.

Daendels to Engelhard, 16 May 1808: Wants immediate report from Eng about situation in Yogya Residency (Financial Report).
Eng to Daendels, 8 Jun. 1808: Report on situation at Yogya cont.

HB II's subjects supposed to work on roads for 90 days in a row after harvest period. But HB II burdens them with many other building projects so that they never achieve very much. Road at Klaten labouring under name difficulties. Road at Boyolali, responsibility of Reg. of Semarang, 1/2 responsibility of HB II.

Bps. of Kedu, Bagelen, Mataram, Pajang, Nukawati now have Residences in my Regencies [^p75s-1] = all reside in Yogya.

"Bonda Pippule" = commission sent out from courts to inquire by Dutch territories and accompanied by Europeans.

The Inner Regent Purwadipura: All communications to HB II have to pass through him.

Enough suitable ground in Mataram for coffee production. See Memorie of Waterloo.

Roads to Semarang/Solo from Yogya in a very poor state. Almost no bridges over rivers.

"Uang Kerigaji" = raised from local population as contribution for road building = but HB II hands over very little of it to DRE for this. Most kept for HB II's treasury. Despite stipulation in Treaty of 1792 that HB II must keep up roads.

The lines on this page contain faint, mirrored or reversed text that is primarily bleed-through from the reverse side of the paper. There is no intentional, readable body text on the front side of the page.
5 JW. - 15 Aug. 1808

AN 'Yogya Brieven' 20.41

PB IV to Daendels, 11 Jumadilawal, 1735 (5 Jun. 1808). Receives P’N letter written at Bidaran, Wed. 28 Jun. 1808; arrives at Bidaran on 28 Jun. 1808.

#### Troop placement in Yogya [^p81s-1]
* In order to ensure security in Java in face of English threat = Artillery at Lingaran to be dispatched to Solo.
* Comp. of Jagers [^p81s-2] 10 cannon/150 men.
* 7-800 men to Solo.
* Infantry at Solo/Yogya/Klaten to be increased to garrisons. Each 100 men. Similarly for cavalry.
* Mounted artillery at Salatiga reinforced + 4 cannon. Now 10 cannon/300 men.
* PB IV promises to prepare bamboo barracks to house garrison reinforcements. To be prepared before 20 Jumadilawal (14 July '08).
* To help transport. Ball/cartridges to Solo. To coordinate Col. Gordon in Semarang.
* Promises to keep Solo troops on war footing.

ID to ID. 21 Jul. 1808 (17 Jumadilawal, 1735). Receives P’N letter from Surabaya 16 Jul. 1808.
* Pan. Cakraningrat appointed as Sultan of Madura [^p81s-3] because of services in providing troops for Batavia/Groote Oost/Ambon etc.

MB II to Daendels, 29 Jumadilawal, 1735 (23 Jul. 1808). As above on receiving P’N letter of appoint. of Pan. Cakraningrat as Sultan of Madura. Also greetings of Ratu Kidhaton/Ratu Mas/Ratu Kencana Wulan/CA - Daendels.

ID to ID. 15 Aug. 1808 (22 Jumadilakir 1735). Receives letter of 6 Aug. 1808 saying had received MB II's letter of 13 Jumadilakir about new ceremonial of 28 Jul.
* Eng/P. Annual MB II that not aimed at tending MB II's honour.
* Opperhoofd = now Minister.
* 2nd Res. = Secretario.

---

"Minister" much higher rank—does it hold more honour than *Opperhoofd*. Name title as given: Ambassador in Chinese Empire.

HBII protests about Minister taking his place at same level or *dhampar* at left-hand side, 1–1½ feet from HBII. Because "Minister" is immediate representative of Daendels, should be treated as Governor of N.E. Coast and name ceremony.

The art: CP/Princes of Blood to be received by Minister, although Princes of Blood regarded as lower in rank than DRII [Danureja II; transcribed as PRII]. Earlier, *Opperhoofd* higher in rank than PRII; lower/more junior in rank have to "nowan" [?] *nenion*. New ceremonial laws to be accepted equally by both courts—PBIV makes no request for changes.

Now that Minister's coach not required to stop when meets HBII's coach, in order to avoid unpleasantness, HBII to warn Minister wherever he plans to go out. HBII will place confidence in discretionary [powers of] Minister Daendels.

P. Eng. asks to be moved to Batavia for family reasons. Daendels appoints as replacement J. W. Wiese. Brother G.G. A. H. Wiese. Newly appointed Minister "man of most character and true heart" would act in same way *foot* as Eng. had done to HBII. HBII/CP had to see Eng. go because he had all qualities necessary to fulfill important post of Minister at courts.

PBIV to Daendels, 18 Rejeb 1735. Receives letter of D. from Semarang written on 6 Sept. 9 Sept. 1808. Unable to receive "hulde" from PBIV's ambassadors on appointed day 20 Aug. 1808 because of meeting N. Coast Regents to discuss improvement in lot of Javanese.
## 1808
### 9 Sept. – 9 Oct. 1808

**PB IV to Daendels, 9 Sept. 1808 (cont.)**
PB would receive PB IV's ambassador on 25 Sept. 1808.

**HB II to Daendels, 10 Sept. 1808 (Nat. 19 Rijel 1735)**
Name as above; not able to receive "Hulde" on 20 Aug. as promised. "Hulde" to be paid on 25 Sept. 1808.

**HB II to Daendels, 28 Rijel 1735 / 19 Sept. 1808**
Ambassadors of HB II have gone to Semarang on 19 Sept. 1808:
*   PR II + Kliwon R.T. Natadiningrat
*   Kyai. Nizdunagara
*   Mas T. Sumanegara, Rg. of Jipang/Padhangan.
*   + Nec. Ng. Prawiranastra.

**Presents of HB II to Daendels:**

*   3 pikes + gold mounting

*   2 small boxes/cartons

**PB IV to Daendels, 27 Rijel 1735 / 18 Sept. 1808**
R. Adip. Danuningrat; R.T. Natawijaya; Arung Binang; Yudanegara from Banyumas.
As above, but 6 small horses / 2 large horses. 6 krises Naganari everything in gold. 16 pikes in gold.

1808

HBII to Daendels, Mon. 19 Ruwah, 9 Oct. 1808.

HBII's embassy pays compliments to D on 25 Sept. 1808. But D had already left for Batavia.

D returns to Batavia for a short period after receiving presents. Before HBII's embassy arrive. J. A. van Braam replies to HBII instead of Daendels. But with presents of D for D received [by] HBII. 

Assures HBII of affection of Daendels for him. D would write to him later to thank him in person.
14 Apr. – 22 May 1808 | Yogya / Solo | [Archive mark: P. Br. 23 'Bundel Brieven naar Batavia']

P. Engelhard – Daendels, 14 Apr. 1808: Takes over Res = P. B. II hands over from H. B. II. Note saying that Waterloo owed H. B. II Jp. 8,000 @ 3/4% p. month. 2 Debt notes - 1 dated 10 Aug. 1803: 2,000 Np. P. (Ralingulakir Nimakir) and 1 dated 19 Jan. 1807: 6,000 Np. P. (Dulkangidah, Nimawal). [^p85s-1]

+ H. B. II asks that Waterloo should pay off debt before leaving Yogya / Refuses Tan Jin Sing’s offer of security. But Wat. [Waterloo] unable to pay off debt. H. B. II Engelhard goes surety to H. B. II for Wat. debt not being able to say when it would be paid off. H. B. II wants payments to start this month, Sapar. [^p85s-2]

P. B. II says that he gives presents to S. G. Wiese on departure from Semarang, but no return presents given. Earlier S. G. Wiese had given on departure: 1) Rou mantelstof, 1 stuk Bodidaar, 1 piece black velvet for a jacket, 1 piece green kasimir for a jacket, gold thread, red armozijn, 2 cannas. [^p85s-3]

24 Apr. 1808: Tells P. B. IV of dismissal of Soer of Java's N. E. coast + brings presents of P. to court.

Von Hielcher leaves 23 Apr. for Yogya. Departure not much regretted. + P. B. IV goes out of way to be vrolijk [cheerful] with van B. family which McHad Reru [probably "had been"?] don't want. [^p85s-4]

Shortly after arrival P. B. IV opposes new Army commander. [^p85s-5] A Pangéran = a young/vile person. P. Cakrakusuma [^p85s-6] more diligent in exercising H. P. B. IV's troops [^p85s-7] - but, lot of great moment (van engelinge [trouble/anxiety] consequentie).

Suspicion aroused at court by D.'s admin. changes and assembling troops in Semarang + van B. This to give proper assurances to calm Solo fears.

---

New equipment for Dutch cavalry, mounted artillery to be sent from Semarang.

**12 May 1808**
Eng. to Daendels. Nine recently—2/3x p. week inspections [of] HBII [^p86s-1] soldiers takes place. Yesterday corps of MN [^p86s-2] soldiers [of] Rongga, 3 horses + muskets, 3 pikes passed Res. House. Asks DRII [^p86s-3] about it = says order of HBII for forces [of] Yogya officials to assemble 1/2 subordinate troops (krijgsvolk) to be able to go to help of Daendels with [English] if necessary.

**15 May 1808**
Van Braam -> Daendels. Sending on meeting Prangwedana (? About money taken by Van Reede).

**21 May 1808**
Eng. to D. Needs official letter [of] relating his arrival in Java to HBII / needs to make good impression. Lessen suspicions somewhat of D's setting up of new cavalry-artillery regts.

> **Position of Buyskes as Lt. Gov.-Gen.**
> Explains position of Buyskes as Lt. Gov.-Gen. (First) problem of ceremonial later who had been sent to take D's place if D had been captured by English. Lt.-Gov. Gen. to take writings of Raad van Indie in concern of Daendels in Central Java etc.

**22 May 1808**
Van B - P. After travelling for 28 hrs arrives in Sala. After seeing P [^p86s-4] in Semarang + coming back + Ang. Wirjekuma[a]rk [^p86s-5] + tells PWD [^p86s-6] of D's arrangements concerning him + to be given 2-3 lbs = seems v. pleased. + gives letter of P to PBIV [^p86s-7] dispelling fears of PBIV concerning D's troop concentrations at Semarang. PBIV says he was notified + HBII [is] believe... [;] Van B says D had also written a letter to HBII at which PBIV replies finely "that gives me real pleasure, now he will at last be convinced of the honest [opregete gevoelens] of the Hollanders". PBIV v. pleased + letter from Daendels. Promises to send some Sala princes to meet D in Semarang + to stay a night for Brg. Wirjekuma[a]rk following Monday.

Fr[ie]drich von [?]
F.C.P[?] von [?]

#### Marginalia
*   **Top right:** "YOGYA TROOPS EXERCISING"
*   **Top left:** "MN Troops in Yogya"
*   **Middle (Red ink):** "Position of Buyskes as Lt GG"
14 Apr. - 22 May 1808 1808 [^p87s-1]

P. Engelhard - Daendels. 14 Apr. 1808: Takes over Res = DR II hands over from HB II. Note saying that Waterloo owed HB II Np. P. 8,000 @ 3/4 to p. month. 2 Debt notes - 1 dated = 20 Rablingalakir nimakir 1750 (10 Aug. 1803) 2,000 Np. P. 1 Dulkangidah, Nimawal, 1733 19 Jan. 1807 6,000 Np. P. [^p87s-2]

+ HB II asks that Waterloo should pay off debt before leaving Yogya. Refuses Jan Tin Ning's offer of security. But Wat. unable to pay off debt. I [and] Engelhard goes surety to HB II for Wat. N debt not being able to pay when it would be paid off. HB II wants payments to start this month. [^p87s-3]

DR II says that gives presents to S.G. Wiese on departure from Semarang, but no return presents given. Earlier G.G. Wiese had given on departure: 1 Rou manteau, 1 stak bodidaar, 1 piece black velvet for a jacket, 1 piece green Casimir for a jacket, gold thread, red armozynen, 2 cannas. [^p87s-4]

[An] Braam to D.D. (Solo) 24 Apr. 1808: Tells DR IV of Didimnal & Soro, Java's N.E. coast and brings presents of D. to court. [^p87s-5]

Vox Hichcherr leaves 23 Apr. for Yogya. Departure not very much regretted. I + PB IV go out 9 way to De vrolijkt van B. + family which metad revu doret vonk. Shortly after arrival, PB IV opposes new army command [^p87s-6] A Pangéran = a young/vling person. P. Cakrakusuma [^p87s-7] more diligent in exercising PB IV's troops, but not of any great moment (van languinge connywatie).

Suspicion aroused at court by D's admin. changes and assembling troops in Semarang + van B. this to give proper assurances to calm Solo fears.

---

22 May – 21 May 1808
Yogyakarta

**22 May 1808:** After journey, van D. reads D.N. [Dutch Note] with to Solo Princes/officials. van D. goes to see van B. incognito that evening, to say whom he had chosen to accompany him to Semarang (van D. had not slept all afternoon). Pleased at letting D. vent to HB II: "dat hij lange in gene geruste situatie is" [that he is long in a state of unrest]. [^p88s-1]

van D. wants to send P. Mangkubumi to N. [^p88s-2], despite fact that in PB IV's words: "not a courtier & does not have fine manners." [^p88s-3]

Then van B. suggests that P. Cakrakusuma, the young troop commander, should be sent; brother-in-law of PB IV & his confidante, a "knap Javaan" [clever Javanese]. PB IV's envoy had also chosen Cakrakusuma. [^p88s-4]

Kyai Adip. [Adipati] of Semarang to arrange lodging for them not far from Bojong.

**23 May 1808 (Mon.):** Solo delegation to leave following Friday evening for Semarang & to return following Friday, under escort of Nervatens – 2nd Res. Solo.

PB IV wants Cakrakusuma MB to stay in name H.O. in Semarang.

**5 May 1808 (9 Mulud Dal 1735):** Eng. [English] asking for PB II's [sic - likely PB IV] help in purchasing horses for cavalry, which he had raised in Semarang. 150 horses had still not been delivered. HB II must be prepared to come to help the Dutch at earliest opportunity (e.g., during an event such as Eng. attack on Grisek 1808) & to be forced to sell horses to the Dutch at 50–70 Sp.R. [Spanish Real].

---

HB II—Daendels in reply to D’r [Dir?] 16 May—MSs 23 May 1808. See PJ. Br. 41.

24 May 1808: PB IV replies to D for letter 9, 16 May. PB IV + Ratu gives Van B a young boy (a Panakawan) d. of Pan. Pamekasan, 10–11 years old, to met [sent to?] Daendels to be taken by Van Braam to Semarang. Show of good faith/trust which PB IV had never shown before. Van B had PB IV been *opgeruimden* (cheerful/in good spirits) as he had been at Res. yesterday evening—could drink no wine. *Decanwy verkonwenheid.* Mangkubumi/PWS to give present of horses to D in Semarang.

25 May 1808: PB IV asks who had spoken with him to Daendels. (HB II). Van B promises to act frankly (*uit opgerichtheid*) to D. Says that HB II had now come round to needing peace + D = gives PB IV pleasure. PB IV says that had no secret news from Yogya—although had sent spies to sound out HB II’s real views vis-à-vis Daendels.

25 May 1808: Eng—D. Png. Von Winkelmann arrives in Yogya for rearrangement of infantry garrisons = Yogya/Klaten.

28 May 1808: 10–12 large ships (praus) owned by Solo Pop. at Ayah/Cilacap for rending of pirates.

28 May 1808: Eng to D. HB II asked about sending some courtiers to Semarang. HB II chooses to send D. Dipakusuma, R. Rongga (son-in-law of HB II) + brother-in-law of HB II, Bp. Rawa, R.T. Pringgakusuma. Would leave for Semarang 12 June 1808.

Next Wed—A general review (mustering) of HB II’s troops to take place with Crown Prince at their head. Von Winkelmann allowed to observe troop exercises. (NU BL. Add. MS).

---
